# Bugs we have had

Moved here from CLAUDE.md on 2026-10-13.

Every one of these was found by the user in-game or by a game test, and has a regression test unless noted. Check this list before touching `AntNavigator`, `AntPathFinder`, the goals, or anything that places blocks.

**Pathing and climbing (the legs, `nav` package)**
1. **Route that was only the ant's own cell crashed the game.** When the ant drifted out of that cell, the walker had no next waypoint. Fix in `AntNavigator`, with a test. Lesson: a route of length 1 is a legal route.
2. **Ant went up and down one block for minutes.** It planned its route while hanging in the air above a pit. Climbing back out passed through that same cell, which the walker read as "back at the start", so it walked down and climbed again for ever. The stuck check never fired because the ant kept moving. Fix: the cell above the start of an up-and-over stretch is never read as an earlier waypoint (`cornerOf`), plus a safety net that replans after 160 ticks without a new waypoint. Lesson: "moving" is not "making progress".
3. **Ants floated up in front of a wall** before climbing over a ledge (each hill step). Now they walk to the foot of the wall first.
4. **Ants hovered at the foot of the shaft wall** with the model tilted up. They slid sideways while already counted as climbing. Now they walk to the wall and only start clinging once they touch it (only while `onGround`).
5. **Ants stuck and hovering inside the shaft** (three causes): a walk that ended in the shaft made the ant fall and re-climb about 15 times (now `holdOnToWall`); the wall was chosen cell by cell, so ants slid across the shaft in mid-air (now one wall per straight climb, `chooseWall`); an ant coming off a stair counted as "in the air" (now `standsOnFloor` checks the world). `watch` in `NavigationTests` now fails any walk where a climbing ant hangs without rising for more than 4 ticks.
5b. **Ants floated across the shaft when a new job came while they hung on a wall**: the new route wanted another wall of the cell and the ant slid straight to it. Now it crawls round the corners (`crawlAim`). Lesson: any change of wall while clinging must go over the walls.
   *Where lessons 2 to 5b live now* (checked in the navigator tidy-up, 2026-10-16; the names above are from before surface walking): **2** the walker goes through the route by step number (`AntNavigator.next`), never by guessing from the cell it is in, so "back at the start" cannot happen; a step not done in 6 seconds (`STEP_TICKS`) is planned again, in place of the old 160-tick net (`cornerOf`, `CIRCLE_TICKS` are gone); test `antsClimbOutOfAOneBlockPit`. **3, 4** an `ONTO_WALL` step walks to the foot of the wall first (`ontoWall`). **5** a walk that ends on a wall keeps holding it (`hold`, was `holdOnToWall`); every step of a route names the wall it holds, so one wall per climb is planned, not chosen on the way (was `chooseWall`); `standsOnFloor` is still there; test `antsStayOnTheShaftWallWhenTheirWalkEndsOrIsStopped`. **5b** a change of wall while clinging is a planned `TURN_CORNER` or `ROUND_EDGE` step (was `crawlAim`); test `antsHangingInTheShaftCrawlRoundToTheOtherWall`.
6. **Ants never cross the open shaft** and must get against a wall before rising. Keep this when adding new moves. (Since surface walking this is built in: no move of the planner crosses open air.)
6b. **A body a hair over the edge of its cell catches on the next block** (surface walking, found by the thin-soil test: the wide queen stuck for good going over the edge into the stairs, workers now and then on a climb or crawl). The collision checks the up/down move first, while the body still pokes 0.003 blocks into the neighbouring column. Fix: `moveAttached` makes tiny corrections first, on their own. Lesson: exact motions next to blocks must keep the box inside its cell.
6c. **A motion smaller than about 0.0003 blocks is thrown away by the game** (`Entity.move` ignores it), so an ant waiting for a tiny correction waited for ever (thousands of stuck steps in one test run). Corrections under 0.001 are now skipped (still inside the cell's 0.002 margin). And any move along a surface that gets nowhere for 20 ticks counts as blocked: the legs remember that foothold for 20 seconds and plan round it (`AntNavigator.isBlocked`, `avoid`, a `StepCosts` of the legs themselves).

6d. **The body spun half round going over a thin wall** (found with `tools/chimney_preview.py`, the chimney's rim): the client tipped the body up onto the new wall from its body yaw, which had already turned toward that wall, and a body not yet flat turned its wall yaw toward the opposite wall through 180 degrees. Now the tip starts from the last tick's body yaw (`floorYaw`) and onto a wall facing the other way the body lies flat first (`AbstractAntEntity.turnBody`). Lesson: the client gets position, yaw and the synced data only every 3 ticks; draw motions from what the body looked like, not from the newest number.

6e. **Crash: a chimney mouth turned into a spire piece under a termite** (user's crash report, 2026-10-13, several termites peeking out at once on a growing mound): the builders reshaped a mouth into an ordinary hollow spire piece (the spire grew taller) while a termite was going in by it; the walker read the block as a doorway without checking and the game crashed (`ClassCastException` in `AntNavigator.intoMouth`). The per-tick check missed it because a spire piece is still a channel. Now `AntPathFinder.whatChanged` also checks that a mouth step's block is still a mouth (the termite plans again), and `intoMouth`/`outOfMouth` never cast blindly. Unit test `aMouthReshapedIntoASpirePieceIsNoLongerAWayInOrOut`. Confirmed in-game by the user. Lesson: anything the builders can reshape can change under an insect in the middle of a move.

6f. **Termites stood on top of the mound in daylight** (user's report with a picture, 2026-10-13: "they all get stuck at the top trying to place the bottom of the spire" going from stage 13 to 14). Read from the user's save (`tools`-less: a small NBT reader in the session scratchpad): the main spire's bottom piece had been cleared (stage 14 wants a tunnel there, the spire moves up a block), the two pieces above it hung in the air for now, and termites stood in that empty cell and in empty cells of the tier below. `Nest.isInside` counted every cell the plan makes a tunnel, spire piece or mouth as "inside" even while it was still open air, so by day they thought they were in and stayed. Now such a cell is inside only once a channel block is really there. Game test `termitesFinishTheUsersHalfBuiltStageFourteen` puts the user's own half-built mound (dumped from the save into `data/antcolonies/gametest_user_mound_14.txt`, same shape seed via `Mound.setSeedForTests`) round a test colony, with night and day taking turns, and checks stage 14 gets finished; `termitesGrowStageThirteenIntoFourteen` does the same from a stage 13 put up at once. Note: the game test world has random ticks off, so wet soil never sets there by itself: a test that needs it must set it (the user-mound test does it at about the game's pace). Confirmed in-game by the user.

6g. **Termites buried in a spire piece** (found by `termitesBuildTheYoungCathedralFromInside` after the "reshape in place" fix, 2026-10-13): a spire piece filled up into solid soil while termites were crawling up its channel walled them in for good (three builders idle in one cell for 70,000 ticks, no foothold, so the young cathedral was never finished). Now a piece with anyone in it is filled up into a tunnel block instead (the channel stays; it then stays a tunnel block where the plan has soil, which only adds a way through), a termite standing on it outside the channel is lifted out, and a player or animal in it stops the job (`Mound.shape`). Lesson: like `placePellet` and `addLayer`, anything that fills a cell must deal with who is in it, including insects inside channels.

**Several ants, one cell (ants do not collide)**
7. **Hill last spots deadlocked** because several ants stood in the same spot. Ants now prefer free spots and step aside before placing.
8. **Hill jam with 4 to 12 ants in one gap** (user's report, found with the `[pellet]` log). All ants with soil walked to the same spot, which lies where every ant leaving the hole passes, so someone was always inside and `placePellet` (refuses while anyone is in the cell) never got a turn. Fix: one claim per spot (`moundClaims`), the soil nudges ants out onto the new block (`nudgeOut`), and a player or animal in the spot still stops it. Lesson: any rule of the form "do X only if the cell is empty" will fail for ever where traffic is dense.
9b. **Sealers shut themselves in** (surface walking, `workersCloseEveryHoleInTheNestWalls`: 4 or 5 workers stuck for good in a crack). The test's crack runs through the seed store and the nursery; a hole between them could be closed from the nursery cell below it, which was only reachable through that very hole (the new climbing made it reachable). Up to 4 ants went through, packed the hole above themselves and were shut in. Now a sealer goes to the first spot it can reach without passing through the hole (`NestWalls.plugSpots`, `SealGoal.walkToAPlugSpot`, the walk forbids the hole with `StepCosts.forbid`), and a digger only stands inside the nest's own space, at the entrance or above ground (`Nest.standSpotsFor`), never down a crack beside the nest. Lesson: never let a job put an ant on the far side of something it or another job is about to close.
9c. **Route started from a floor that isn't there** (surface walking): an ant standing at the rim of a hole counts as on the ground although the block under its middle is open, so routes started from a floor foothold that does not exist, failed on their first tick, and were planned again for ever (12765 give-ups in one test run). Now the start is worked out from the body: its wall, its floor only if it is really there, else the floor of the cell beside it that it stands on (`AntNavigator.currentFoothold`, `floorBeside`).
9. **A pellet once buried the queen.** Pellets are never placed on a living creature (players and animals still block it).

**Nest shape and soil**
10. **Ants stopped once the shaft was dug** in a world where only the top 3 blocks were dirt and the room layer was full of stone. Now the layout is planned before the first block is dug (`chooseLayout`), with fewer levels or one block higher where the soil is thin. Ants never dig stone.
11. **A sealed-off pocket next to a block stopped the dig.** `DigGoal` now tries every open side of a block as a place to stand.
12. **Holes in the nest walls** (caves, cracks, a gap between two rooms) and **a hole right next to the top of the shaft** that nobody could reach: fixed by `SealGoal` / `getLeaks` and `findPlugSpot` (floors first, then walls).
13. **Water and lava defence** got too complicated and was removed (commits ad0fcf4 to ec5253c). It is still in git history. Hazards stay a later item.

**Colony logic**
14. **A queen could have starved with a full bread store** (caught in design): her 5-day clock only runs while she wants food and gets none; it resets while she carries an egg, has her full meal, or the nursery holds 10 young.
15. **A full larva waiting for a worker slot must not starve** (same idea: the clock only runs for a larva that still has to eat).
16a. **Nest plan re-guessed after a reload** (code review): the layout was not saved, and seed/bread heaps made the old direction look blocked, so a second nest was started. Now saved. Lesson: anything decided once from the world must be saved, because the world changes. Game tests never reload unless a test does `Colony.load(colony.save())`.
16. **Old saves:** ants and colonies from before a change must still load. New saved fields use a "not set" marker (`Colony.NOT_SET`) and old keys are still read (`queenSeeds`). Existing nests keep their old shape; new layouts only apply to new colonies.
16b. **A "once a second" timer that never ran** (found in step 6b): timers that start at `Long.MIN_VALUE` and check `now - last >= 20` overflow on the first call (`now - Long.MIN_VALUE` is negative), so the check never happens. `ForagingHoles.isAnyoneOutside` always said "nobody" because of it. Start such a check with `last == Long.MIN_VALUE ||`, or test it.

**Test-only traps**
17. **A neighbouring test's leftover colony** was found by the `colony(helper)` helper (a colony with no living queen). The helper now requires a living queen. New tests shift the arena positions, so this can come back.
17b. **Test ants joined a neighbouring test's colony** (chimney animation session: `aLoneQueenFoundsHerColonyAndRaisesHerFirstWorkers` failed because 4 worker ants from walking tests nearby had joined her colony, so she was no longer founding alone and never sealed herself in). A worker with no colony joins the nearest one within 32 blocks on its first tick, and the tests run side by side; adding one test moved the arenas so the walking tests landed near her. Now every ant a test walks by hand is spawned with `TestAnts.loneAnt` (`WorkerAntEntity.keepOutOfColonies`). Use it for any new test ant that must not belong to a colony. The reload tests make their copy of the ant mindless and colonyless too (a copy with a mind wandered off the wall it was meant to hold).
18. **Timing flakes under load:** `antsOnBumpyGroundReachTheFoodStore`, `aStarvingWorkerSlowsDownAndDies`, `queenIsFedAndHerEggGoesToTheNursery` (a nurse can pick up the egg in the very tick it is laid). Re-run once before assuming a real bug.
18b. **`workersCarrySeedsToTheFoodStore` lost one seed of five** in 2 of 5 runs during the mound-block session (the counts ended at 4, nobody holding anything, no seed item in the test area); NOT explained yet. It started after the ceilings, so ceiling wandering is a suspect but not proven. Diagnostics added: the failure message lists every ant holding a seed or bread and every item within 64 blocks (`carriers`), `StoreSeedGoal` logs `[seed] ... dropped it` and the feeding goals log `[bread] ...`. Also fixed on the way: a nurse with bread it could take nowhere deleted it; now it puts it down where it stands as a bread heap (`Bakery.putDownBreadWhereItStands`). Next time it fails, read the message before guessing.
18c. **`termitesSealOpeningsInTheirNestWithMoundSoil` cut a "wall" that later became a doorway** (found in the step 6b full run): it picked a garden side wall that no garden dug *so far* used, but the middle of a garden's wall is where the doorway to a later garden goes, so the cut was later part of the nest and was never sealed. Now it cuts a wall cell beside a corner of the room, outside every garden the plan may ever have.
18d. **A colony's reach is bigger than a test arena** (the hay test was really bug 18f) (2026-10-13, food finding session): a neighbouring test's termites cut a test's grass (pieces went missing from its count), workers of other tests' colonies wandered in and kept its alarm at 2 (no foraging, a plant never cut), and a worker put down by a test joined the nearest colony, a neighbour's. Workarounds for now: `FoodMap.setTestArea` and `Alarm.setTestArea` (set by `TermiteMoundTests.colony`), `knowForTests` in tests not about finding, `joinColony` for a test's own new worker. The real fix is spacing the arenas (see Testing tips). `aLarvaThatIsNotFedForTwoDaysStarves` (an ant test) failed in 2 full runs this way and passes alone (again once on the 65 x 65 arenas, 2026-10-14, `0 bodies, expected 1`; passes alone). FIXED 2026-10-14: not a neighbour at all, the colony's own worker had picked the body up to carry it to the refuse chamber, and a carried body was not counted. Lesson: a test that counts things lying about must also count the ones being carried.
18f. **A test put its food outside its own arena** (2026-10-14): the hay runway test placed the hay 12 blocks out past the colony's first hole; when that hole faced north or west the hay was 1 block outside the arena, under the barrier wall, unseen. It looked like neighbouring tests interfering. Lesson: place test things by `TermiteSoldierTests.intoArena` or check `helper.getBounds()`, and make a failure message say *which* check said no (`ForagingHoles.whyNoRunway`).
18e. **A check that holds "for good" after an event** (chicken test, 2026-10-13): it asserted no foraging for the rest of the test once the alarm was up, but the alarm rightly calms after 30 seconds; when the test ran longer it "failed". Check a rule only while its cause still holds.
19. **A test that passes on the old code proves nothing** (`manyAntsWithPelletsAndOneGapDoNotJam` never failed before the hill-jam fix). Prefer a test that is first shown to fail.

20. **Termites crowding the middle hole after a chicken alarm** (user's report 2026-10-13, night, a young mound: they clung to the walls of the shaft over the hole and kept "trying to place a block" until the alarm calmed down, about 2 minutes). At alarm 2 the colony stays in, so the middle hole is plugged; two causes found in the code: (a) the mound's hollow shaft counted as inside even while a young mound has no roof over it, so foragers coming home stopped in it, above the plug; (b) `ForagingHoles.plug` refused while any creature stood in the hole, and the hole is where everybody coming home passes (the same jam as bug 8). Fixes: a mound shaft cell open to the sky is outside (`Nest.isInside`); coming-home termites never stop in a doorway (`GoInsideGoal`, `ForagingHoles.isDoorway`); nestmates in the hole are nudged down into the nest when it is plugged (anything else still stops it). The game test `TermiteAlarmTests.theMiddleHoleIsPluggedSoonAfterAChickenAlarm` could NOT recreate the jam (it passes on the old code too, see 19), so the user's retest is the real check. Lesson: a doorway must never count as a place to stop, and "inside" must mean out of the open air.

21. **Dead termites left no body in-game** (user's report 2026-10-14, punched in creative) though the game test passed: the body code needed a colony, and a termite of no colony (spawn egg far from a queen) died like a vanilla mob. Now every dead worker and soldier leaves a body. Lesson: a test spawns its insects next to a queen, the user often does not; test the colonyless case too.

22. **`theMiddleHoleIsPluggedSoonAfterAChickenAlarm` failed 1 run in 3** (first full run on the 65 x 65 arenas, 2026-10-14): not the termites but the test. It clears the plan's tunnel block over the middle hole to make a young open mound, and the builders sometimes put it back before the plug; a covered middle hole leads only into the mound's tunnels and by design is not plugged. Now the test accepts plugged or roofed over. Still a rare flake (1 in 13 alone): `the chicken is not dead yet`; the message now says whether the chicken ever came. Lesson: a test that undoes part of the plan must expect the builders to redo it. **2026-10-17 (long trails step 1, full run 110 of 111):** it failed with `no chicken yet: no forager reached the bush while a minor was outside` (the chicken only comes once a forager is at the bush while a minor is out); it fails the same way 1 run in 6 alone on the code without the long trails (and 1 in 5 with them), so it is this old flake, not the trails. Again in the full run after long trails steps 2 to 4 (2026-10-17, 111 of 112), then 3 of 4 alone. Still open: the scene depends on a forager and a minor being out at the same moment.
   - **Again 2026-11-05 (real sizes step 4, branch `claude/real-sizes-step4-072dcf`):** failed once in the full run on 6 servers ("no chicken yet: no forager reached the bush while a minor was outside"); 6 of 6 alone. Still the old flake.
22b. **Two more test races found the same night** (full runs on the 65 x 65 arenas): `aWorkerFindsAHayBale...` failed when the finder walked home before the colony's 5-second plant scan had seen the newly put down hay (the test now calls `FoodStore.rescanPlantsSoon`); `minorsBiteAChickenThatHunts...` asked every minor to feel the alarm, but the first one can kill the chicken before the drumming reaches the others (now at least one). Neither was a bug in the insects. Lesson: under a full run's load a test sees timings it never sees alone; check what it asks for is really what the insects must do.

23. **The ant queen's founding chamber open to a hillside** (full run, 2026-10-14): the nest plan only checked that each chamber cell was soil or air, never what lies beside it, so on a slope the founding chamber could run out under the lower ground with open sky over a corner. The side tried first comes from the colony's id, so the hill test passed or failed by luck, and an unrelated change seemed to break it. Fixed: the founding chamber must be under cover all round (`Nest.isCovered`). Lesson: a test that flips between pass and fail with no reason may depend on a random id; look for what the id chooses.

24. **Termites cased in with a branch and shut out by day** (user's report 2026-10-14, first in-game test of the mud over wood): four termites eating a fallen branch from outside at night stood in the spot beside it where the workers then put a plate of mud (casing the branch over its gallery, `NestWalls.wallFirst`). A cell with a plate is not open to the route planner, so every route from inside it failed at once (`[seed] ... 4 failed routes`, `[go-inside] ... found no way in`), though the hole was 2 blocks away; by day the holes were plugged with them still outside. Found from the user's `debug.log` (the stuck termites' cell, the branch's position) and the world save. Fixes: a plate is never put down over anyone: nestmates are nudged out to an open spot beside it first, away from the plate (`NestWalls.plate`, also for leaks patched with crust in `plugLeak`); and a safety net in the legs: an insect inside a block that is not open plans from the open spots beside it (`AntNavigator.plan`). Game test `aPlateOfMudIsNeverPutDownOnATermite` (fails without the safety net). Lesson: anything that puts a block down where insects walk must decide what happens to an insect already there (nudge, never bury, never "only if empty", see 8 and 9), and the legs must cope with a start cell that is not open, not only one in the air.

25. **A branch's gallery left open 2 blocks high on a step** (user's report 2026-10-14, big in-game test, savanna hill, at night). Could not be traced in the save (the branch was eaten and its gallery given up by then), but a game test of a branch on the edge of a step dug at night failed once in about 10 runs with the gallery open on the step side. Weak point found: when a wall plate could not go in (a creature in the spot that could not be nudged aside), `wallFirst` left it as a leak "patched later" while the digger opened the cell anyway. Fix: `wallFirst` says whether every plate went in, and `DigGoal` holds the last bite until it has; a refused plate is logged (`[walls] no plate yet at ...: ... in the way`), so a repeat in-game names what was in the way. The branch test now lies on a step with tall grass (green and dry) round it, and checks the branch's own casing every tick. Lesson: "patched later" breaks any rule of the form "never open, not even for a tick"; hold the action instead.

26. **The fungus comb kept layers and lost nodules after a colony came back** (found by the new game test `aTermiteColonyAwayAgesItsCombFeedsItsQueenAndEatsAsNumbers`, 2026-10-15, before any user saw it). Two causes. (a) On return the `NestLedger` took the eaten and crumbled layers off the comb *before* it turned the layers that had gone old while away into old ones; only old layers can be taken off, so it found too few, called the rest "taken already" and dropped them: the blocks had 20 layers where the colony counted 12. Now it works in the order things happened: layers put on top, then ripened and gone old, then taken off the bottom (`NestLedger.apply`, `addToStore`, `takeFromStore`). (b) A comb eaten or crumbled down to its last layer turns to air, and its nodules vanished with it (blocks 4, counted 7). First fixed by moving them onto other comb; then the user decided (2026-10-15) they are lost with the comb, as on a real comb eaten away: the brain counts nodules in the world, so its count simply has those fewer (`FungusGarden.loseNodules`, from `FoodStore.takeOneLayer` and the crumbling in `FungusGarden.grow`, counted for the test). Lesson: a ledger that settles several kinds of debt must settle them in the order they happened when one kind makes room for another; and a block that can disappear must say what happens to what lies on it.

27. **The termite eating test failed about 3 runs in 4** (`termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder`, 2026-10-15, found while building brain step 2c; it failed on the older code too). Not the insects: the comb kept ageing while the test looked at it. A fresh top layer ripened by itself during the 10 to 20 seconds the workers took to eat, and the test read that as "eaten from the top" (now the ageing stands still during that check). And `FungusGarden.grow` could turn a layer old and crumble it in the same second, so a sped-up test never saw the old stage (now one step a second per layer, a small real fix). Lesson: when a test checks one process (eating), stop the other processes that change the same thing (ageing), or check the effect itself rather than a snapshot; and a stage that takes time must never be skipped in one update.

28. **Founders never dug through grass, and founding stood still while away** (user's report 2026-10-15, seen twice). (a) A founder digs only while standing on its hole, and a dig target needs air over it for a stand spot (`Nest.findStandSpots`); a tuft of short grass or a flower in the entrance cell is not air, so the shaft's first block had no stand spot and the queen (or the royal pair) waited forever. Fixed: a plant in the entrance is the first thing bitten away (`Nest.isPlantInEntrance`). (b) The away building skipped founding colonies, and the founding eggs need the founders sealed in, so nothing happened while away. Fixed: the brain counts the founders' digging while away and seals them in once it is done (`NestLife.foundWhileAway`, `BuildLife.settleFounders`). Lesson: "needs air over it" is not "nothing over it": plants, snow layers and the like are not air; and every new away rule must ask what happens to a colony that is still founding.

29. **A founding ant queen's first workers starved while away** (user's report 2026-10-15: "the ant queen still didnt lay her eggs when the chunk was unloaded"). From the user's `latest.log` and world save: she did lay her 3 eggs while away and raised them into 3 workers, but they then starved (`[meals] ... 3 worker numbers starved`), so back home she sat alone, sealed in, with no eggs or young. The worker numbers forage only into room the brain has seen in the food store (`FoodStore.roomKnown`), and a nest that has only its founding chamber has no food store yet (`seenRoom` 0 in all three of the user's founding colonies), so they never went out, though the brain knew 280 to 457 plants. Fixed: while the nest is loaded the brain works out how much digging stands before the food room is open (`Nest.foodStoreDigLeft`, the bites and each bite's soil carried to the hill); while away the builder numbers work it off at the real pace (about a game day for a young nest's one builder) and then the brain counts the room as open (`NestLife.digStoreAway`, `FoodStore.storeDugAway`), so the foragers bring seeds in; the blocks are dug on return, rooms first. The founding test (`aFoundingAntQueenRaisesHerFirstWorkersWhileAway`) now stays away past the workers' starve time; it fails without the fix with the user's `3 starved`. Lesson: every away rule that needs a room must ask whether a young nest has dug it yet; count the digging of it, as for the founders' chamber (bug 28).

30. **The harvester ants stopped building their hill once they dug the food store** (user's report 2026-10-16, with screenshots: workers "stuck on the same block" at the top of the nest, walking back and forth to the food store, the hill not growing). From the user's world save: the soil dug while the surface was shut (night, and a 4-hour midday rest in a hot biome: shut about 71% of the day) went onto the night soil heap, a hidden count under the entrance; at most 4 builders carried it up, only in open hours, and only when they were not digging, so the heap grew without end (181 and 48 pellets in two colonies) and the hill starved. The "stuck" ants were carriers walking to an empty-looking cell under the entrance to pick up invisible soil, crowding that one cell. The game test missed it: its morning never ends, so the heap was always cleared. Fixed (the user's option A, "the morning clear-out"): the soil lies in visible piles of Loose Soil spread over the floors near the top of the nest (`colony/NightSoil`, `Nest.nightSoilCells`); builders carry the piles up before they dig again, one to a pile (`CarryNightSoilGoal`, `DigGoal.soilWaits`); while the surface is shut the diggers rest once as much waits as their builders can carry out in a morning; the builder numbers carry them first on return (`BuildLife.carryNightSoil`); an older save's heap is laid out as piles (the rest packed into the walls). The new test checks found two more traps at once: a pile on a stair still to be dug left the diggers no place to stand (a stand spot must be air), and once the hill was full nobody carried the piles; now no pile goes on a block still to be dug, and the piles are packed into the walls once the hill is finished. Lesson: a store that only ever grows needs a limit and a sink at least as strong as its source, worked out over a whole day (not only in the test's frozen hour); and anything ants walk to must be something the player can see.
31. **The harvester ant hill test on a step failed about 3 runs in 4** (`HillTests.antHillOnAStepKeepsItsRoundShape`, found in the test clock's full run, 2026-10-16; on `brain-offline` too). Not the hill: in the failing runs the hill was its planned shape by tick 20000, but the nest was never dug out before the time ran out (the message only printed the hill, so at first it looked like a hill bug). From debug lines: the colony had 4 workers (1 nurse, 1 or 2 builders), and the builders spent most of the run out on food searches with no food in the arena. A worker helps with other jobs once it has had nothing to do for 5 seconds (`isIdle`), and its own work goals look for work only one tick in 20 (checked every second tick), so a builder with blocks still to dig missed its work by pure chance for 5 seconds in about 1 pause in 13, counted as idle and took up a forager's job. Before brain step 4c that was a 30-second scouting walk; since 4c a lone search lasts until food is found or up to 5 minutes (`give_up_ticks` 6000), so each false idle cost the nest minutes of digging. Fix: in its last 2 seconds before it counts as idle, an ant looks for its own work at every chance (`AbstractAntEntity.looksForWork`, used by every goal that looks for a job's work, for ants and termites alike), so an ant is idle only when its own job truly has nothing for it. The test's message now says whether the hill or the digging is unfinished. Lesson: a random "think now and then" gate must never decide that an ant has nothing to do; and a change that makes one job's trips longer (4c) makes every "help when idle" rule costlier, so check who can be drafted into it.

32. **Tests that pass alone and fail side by side** (the quick run of 2026-10-16, `antnest,away,colony,hills`: 5 of 52 failed, every one passed alone). Three leaks between tests, all in how the tests ran, not in the colonies: (a) **shared dice**: every colony rolled the world's one random stream (`level.getRandom()`, 38 places), so a colony's luck changed with whatever its neighbours rolled; since brain step 4c the foragers search by chance, and in the crowd the 2c ant numbers found 3 seed spots where alone they found 13 in the same time. (b) **Leftover colonies**: Minecraft removes only the creatures inside a passed test's box (a forager out searching stays) and nothing of a failed test; the colony itself stayed in the colony list, living "away", through every later test. (c) **Counting boxes**: the nest sits in the north-west corner of its 65 x 65 box and a harvester can now search 36 blocks out (24 + 8 stretch + 4 wander), so a test counting "workers in my box" missed its own foragers (the jobs test saw 4 of its 6) and could count a neighbour's. Fixed (`gametest/TestIsolation`): each colony rolls its own dice (`Colony.random()`; in a game seeded from its id, on the test server from its test's name and spot, so a test rolls the same colony dice alone or in a crowd); when a test is done its colonies are removed with every insect, egg, larva and body of them, wherever they are; checks that count a colony's insects count its own (`TestIsolation.insectsOf`). Two checks were also too strict: the queen test now counts the bread the larva ate with the queen's (the nurses feed both from the last 5 pieces). Not explained: the seed test's one missing seed (34 of 35) in its long failed run; it passes since. After the fix: the same 52 tests all passed on 5 servers side by side in 9 minutes. Lesson: tests that share a world must share nothing else: own dice, clean up after themselves, count only their own colony.
33. **Harvester ant trunk trails faded a second after being laid** (the user's live game log, 2026-10-16: "laid a new trunk trail toward north", "the trunk trail toward north faded" a third of a second later, then the same east, west, north-west). A new trail started at strength 0 and the trip that laid it added 1, exactly the fade line (`fade_below` 1.0), so the next once-a-second look saw it decayed a hair below and forgot it. The 4a test sent all its trips in one tick, so the trail was strong before any look; in the game, once the door rule and lone searching made trips come one at a time, no trail lived to see its second trip. Fixed: a new trail starts as strong as the trips that laid it. Lesson: a thing that fades below a line must never start at that line; and a test that does everything in one tick never sees anything that happens between ticks: let the clock run (or age it) between the steps.
34. **The gaster messed up while climbing and went into the stone on top of a wall** (user's report 2026-10-16, branch `anim-engine`, the new waist bend). Three causes. (a) The live layer (`client/BodyLanguage`) adds its bend to a bone's turn each frame. GeckoLib clears every bone's "moved" mark after playing the clips, puts a bone back to its clip (or resting) pose next frame only if it is not marked, and `GeoBone.setRotX` marks it. So a bone the playing clip does not move (the postpetiole in every walk) kept this frame's bend and got the next one added on top, every frame; the model's bones are shared, so the next ant drawn got it too. Fixed: `BodyLanguage.turnBy` puts the marks back as they were. (b) The gaster stayed bent down for several ticks after the waist was on top (fixed by the averaging rule, `AbstractAntEntity.tickWaist`). (c) The body tipped over an outside edge only when its middle got there (fixed by `entity/body/EdgeOver`). Found with the in-game camera, `tools/film/climb.film`: the drawn bend was about twice the logged one, and the gaster stayed drooped while the log said 0.2 degrees, then snapped straight the moment the bend fell under 0.05 (when the live layer stops touching the bone). The film draws about one frame a tick, so it showed far less than the 60 frames a second the user saw. Lesson: in GeckoLib's `setCustomAnimations`, adding to a bone's turn is only safe on a bone the clip sets every frame; otherwise set it outright, or put the marks back. When a picture disagrees with the number behind it, trust neither: find what else moves the bone.

35. **Cleaning, naps and noticing lasted twice as long as their numbers** (2026-10-16, branch `anim-engine`; found when the `body` game test failed now and then: "the cleaning ant went on cleaning for 125 ticks"). The game ticks a running goal only every other tick unless the goal asks for every tick (`Mob.serverAiStep`; the same rule as in the notes on `GroomGoal` never starting, `docs/HISTORY.md`). `GroomGoal`, `NapGoal` and `NoticeGoal` count their time with `ticksLeft--` in `tick()`, so they ran at half pace: a both-antennae cleaning outlasted its clip, and the test failed only when the ant cleaned both antennae first (40%). Fixed: they return true from `requiresUpdateEveryTick`. Lesson: a goal that counts ticks in `tick()` must ask for every tick, or count from the game time instead; a test that passes or fails by a coin toss may be catching a real bug on one side of the toss. Many older goals count ticks the same way; not checked yet.

36. **An ant turning on the spot stuttered** (2026-10-16, branch `anim-engine`; found by the film checks). Minecraft's own `BodyRotationControl` lets a standing mob's head turn up to 75 degrees off its body and then, each time the head has gone 15 degrees on, snaps the body after it: with the head turning 10 degrees a tick the body jumped 20 degrees every other tick, and stopped 75 degrees behind the head. Fixed with our own `entity/body/BodyTurn` (`AbstractAntEntity.createBodyControl`). Lesson: vanilla body and head rules are made for big mobs; check them with the film log of both the server's and the client's body, yaw and head. Also learned while building the checks: GeckoLib 4.9.3's `GeoBone.getLocalSpaceMatrix` has a whole extra identity matrix added (its `RenderUtil.translateMatrix`), so subtract one before using it (`client/FilmChecks`).

37. **Two tests too strict, found by the first full run on `master`** (2026-10-16, after merging `brain-offline` and `anim-engine`: 108 of 111 passed). (a) `insectsNoticeMovingPlayersByTheirPersonality` (the animation work's noticing test) failed about 1 run in 5, alone too, on `anim-engine` before the merge as well: its mock players stood on fixed spots, and an insect sometimes strolled out of range before its first chance to notice (the shy ant ended 6.1 blocks from its player, the termite out of reach of the jump); the test then ran on to its time-out, and on a server sped up by other tests (tick rate 2,000) it got past the middling ant's 600-tick rest, so its second, rightful look failed "noticed again right after its look". Fixed in the test: each player keeps beside its insect until the insect notices, then stands still there; "again right after" means within the profile's rest time (`rest_ticks`). 10 of 10 alone. (b) `workersCarryTheDeadToTheRefuseChamber` killed "some worker within 20 blocks of the arena", and since step 4c a forager can be out on a search up to 32 blocks away: it died past the colony's 24-block reach, where nobody fetches a body (it crumbles there, as a real far-off death would), so 4 bodies came in, not 5. Fixed in the test: it kills the worker nearest the nest. No game change. Lesson: a test that picks "any insect" or puts things on fixed spots must allow for insects that move on their own; pick the one the check means.

38. **The test clock's sunset was 9 ticks late** (2026-10-16, the second full run on `master`: 109 of 111). `aFallenLogAndTheStumpOnItAreEatenFromBelowByDay` failed "foraging time" at 18:30 on day 1: the clock had sunset written in as 12550, but by the game's own sun (`Colony.isSunUp`, which the colonies go by) the sun sets at 12541, so for 9 ticks the test clock said day while the termites already counted it as night and came out. The check only trips when a test is still running right at dusk; this one was, because the stump on the log was still being nibbled (once the colony's store is full the termites eat slowly, so the last block can take till evening or the next night; 5 reruns of its server's 14 tests passed, finishing between 14:30 and the next dawn). Fixed in the test clock: sunset and sunrise are read from `Colony.isSunUp` itself (`TestClock.findSunEdges`). The stump's state is now in the test's hourly log line. No game change. Lesson: a test clock must take its edges from the same rule the game uses, never a rounded copy of it.

39. **The night soil waited hours in the morning** (2026-10-17, the `antnest,away,colony` run on the long-trails branch: `workersDigTheWholeNestAndBuildTheMound`, "carried up first thing in the morning, but it took 12936 ticks"; earlier runs 716 to 1907). The builders rest all night once the night soil piles are full, so at dawn they have long counted as idle (`isIdle`), and an idle builder may help forage. The food search (`ScoutGoal`, priority 7) came before carrying the night soil (`CarryNightSoilGoal`, priority 8), so at dawn some builders went off on searches of up to 5 minutes, two in a row, while 3 piles waited. Fixed: the night soil goal comes before searching (priority 6), so a builder carries it first, and one already out searching comes back for it (once on a search it is busy, not idle, and its own job's work takes over). Lesson: the BUGS 31 lesson again from another side: a night's rest is a long idle, so every "help when idle" job must rank below the ant's own job's waiting work.
40. **The ant numbers foraged one seed too many** (2026-10-17, the same run: `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember`, "9, was 0" where 8 were expected). An old race in `FieldLife`: each look first remembers the plants it sees (`look`), then shows the cuts made out of sight (`showCuts`). A tuft just cut bare out of sight was still standing when the look ran, so it was remembered again as fresh; then `showCuts` broke it, and the numbers cut one more piece off the remembered "fresh" tuft that was no longer there. Fixed: `showCuts` forgets a plant whose cuts turned it into something that is no plant, and the numbers never work a loaded spot that is no longer a plant. Lesson: when one step of a tick reads the world and a later step changes it, the memory from the first step must be checked again after the change.

41. **In game tests every harvester colony counted as young forever** (2026-10-17, found by the new far-meadow test: 26 searchers, none ever walked out the colony's 26-block trail, all spread out round the nest). The door (`Door`, Gordon's rule) works out once a second whether the colony is small (under 10 foragers), and a small colony's searchers spread out every way instead of following trails (real young colonies have none). But that count ran only while the door was on, and the door is on only with the patrol, which game tests leave off: the "small" mark stayed at its start value, true. In the real game the patrol is on, so only tests were hit, but every test colony with trails searched like a young one. Fixed: the foragers are counted door or no door. Lesson: a value worked out inside a switched-off part must not be read by parts that stay on; work it out where everyone reading it can rely on it.

42. **A termite worker walled in alive, and a king digging for ever** (the `AntNavigator` tidy-up's full run, 2026-10-16: 1,840 `[dig] ... found no way to` lines on one server, all 27 of its tests passing). Three insects stood still and asked for a route to the colony's next dig block about once a second for minutes, each failed search up to 6,000 steps. Found by rerunning that server's 27 tests with a dump of the blocks round a stuck insect (the far-hay test alone never showed it). (a) **Worker 104, far-hay tunnel test** (`aBigColonyDigsATunnelToFarHay...`, not the harvester queen test as first thought): it was in the feeding gallery under the hay when the hay went. A spent hole's gallery leaves the nest (`FoodWays.galleryCells`), so the gallery's mouth became a leak in the tunnel's wall, and a builder sealed it with soil from the tunnel side (`SealGoal`, `NestWalls.plugLeak`) while the roof (where the hay lay) had been plugged a few seconds before: a two-cell pocket closed all round, the worker inside, no way anywhere (`[dig]`, `[go-inside]`, `[plug]` all failing). (b) **The king in the user's-mound test**: a queen or king dug while the colony had no finished room, not only while founding, so with workers round him he tried to dig the shaft, never got there, and his searches took 29 of the mod's 34 ms a tick (worst search 235 ms). (c) `DigGoal`, unlike every other goal that walks to work, had no memory of a block it found no way to, so a cut-off insect asked again for ever. Fixes (the user's OK): a hole is never sealed while one of the colony's insects is in a small closed pocket behind it (`NestWalls.shutsInANestmate`: each side of the hole on its own, a pocket of more than 32 open cells counts as open to the world); the queens and the king dig only while founding (`QueenToRoomGoal.mayDig`, as real Pogonomyrmex queens and Macrotermes royals do); `DigGoal` leaves a block it found no way to alone for 20 seconds. Checks: the far-hay test keeps a worker in the gallery for 30 seconds when the hay goes (never walled in, gets out once let go); the user's-mound test fails if the king or queen digs once the colony has workers. The full run after (8 servers, 110 of 111) found one test that leaned on the old queen: in `workersDigTheWholeNestAndBuildTheMound` the workers came at once and the queen dug beside them (her soil never reaches the hill); with only workers digging, every bite went onto the hill, which was full before the test's midnight, so no night soil could pile up. The test now plays out a real founding: the queen digs the shaft and her founding chamber alone, then her 6 workers come (box 2, the behaviour changed on purpose). Lesson: anything that closes a space must ask who is in the space behind it (bugs 8, 9, 24: not only "who is in the cell"); and every goal that walks to work needs a "found no way, leave it alone" memory, or one cut-off insect costs a route search a second for ever.

43. **The game froze: a crash drawing an ant, then a save that never finished** (the user's game, 2026-10-17: night, rain, a harvester colony of about 100; the screen stuck on loading and the user closed it). `run/logs/latest.log` showed the real start: a crash on the render thread in `AntAnimationController.gearFor`, a `NullPointerException` from `walks.indexOf(...)`. The ants' list of walks is a `List.of` list, which throws when asked for the place of "nothing", and for a moment an ant can have nothing playing (no clip yet, e.g. just after a whole-body clip ends), so the first frame of such an ant crashed the game. Fixed: nothing playing counts as no walk yet (`gear -1`). After the crash the game tried to save and stopped at "Saving worlds" (a normal save of the same world took 0.8 s earlier in the same log); that part had no error and could not be found from the log (our code that runs when insects unload, `AwayLife.freeze`, never touches the world). If a freeze comes again, a thread dump taken while it is frozen (`jcmd <pid> Thread.print`) shows where it waits. Lesson: a `List.of` list's `indexOf` and `contains` throw on null; check for nothing before asking.

44. **Ants picking up dropped seeds and dropping them again** (the user's game, 2026-10-17: "can ants not pick up dropped seeds, maybe they're trying but it's not working"). `run/logs/latest.log` had 76 `[seed] ... dropped it` lines, the same few ants at the same few spots, each "0 failed routes": the seed went down again the moment it was picked up. Cause: a forager goes for seeds while the colony wants seeds, and with the food store full it still wants them for bread (`FoodStore.wantsSeeds`: a store spot, or `Bakery.needsBread`). Many foragers set out at once; the mill has places for only as many seeds as bread is wanted, and the first ones there took them. The rest picked up their seed anyway, found neither a store spot nor a mill place (`StoreSeedGoal.canUse` with no spot drops it), dropped it where they stood and, still told seeds were wanted, picked it up again. Fixed: an ant asks before it takes a seed off the ground or off a plant whether it has somewhere to take it right now (`Bakery.hasPlaceForASeed`: the store with room, or a mill place, claimed there and then); if not, it leaves the seed lying (as real harvester ants stop foraging once the granary is full) and fetches it once there is room. The first try of the fix still failed the new test every time: the nurses keep taking seeds out of a full store to mill, so a place or two opens now and then, and several foragers each saw the same free place, picked up a seed, and the later ones found it filled when they got home. So a seed on its way in now holds its place in the store (`FoodStore.claimStorePlace`, kept until the seed is put down or dropped, or 2 minutes), as the mill already held its places; `wantsSeeds` counts only places nobody holds. Game test `NestTests.seedsLyingByAFullStoreAreLeftThereAndFetchedOnceThereIsRoom` (the store full, bread wanted, 40 grama seeds lying round the nest, twice what the mill wants, 30 foragers: no seed is ever dropped; once 20 places are free every seed is fetched); it fails on the old code (1 dropped within 2 seconds). Lesson: a goal must check it can finish before it changes the world (picking a thing up), not only that the colony wants it done; "wanted" for the whole colony is not "a place for mine", and a free place seen by many must be held by the one that takes it.
45. **Termite tunnels on a hillside opened sideways to the air** (found 2026-10-17 by the new gallery proof, `GalleryProofTest`, before anyone saw it in a game: 5 of 379 made-up landscapes for tunnels to food, and 21 sides in 1,582 of the nest's own tunnels). A tunnel runs one block under the ground, so where it ran along the top of a drop of 2 or more (a terrace, a cliff edge beside its path) the block beside it was open air. `TunnelRoute` only checked the steps along the route, never the columns beside it. Now a tunnel block must have ground beside it on all four sides (`TunnelRoute.isWalledIn`): the route goes round, and the nest's own straight tunnels stop short before such a spot. Lesson: prove a planner on many made-up worlds before the insects use it; the proof found in a second what no game test had.
46. **Known hay near a hole got no tunnel and no runway** (the user's game, 2026-10-17, termites underground step 3: "they walked up to the hay to bite from it in the night time and it fast forwarded days and no hole", hay 15 to 25 blocks out). Two rules from before the galleries met: a new tunnel goes only to rich food more than 8 blocks from every hole (`new_holes.min_distance`), nearer food gets a mud runway instead, but only 3 runways are made at a time (`sheeting.max`). With holes every 4 blocks along the galleries almost all food lies near a hole, and the colony had made its 3 runways: the hay got neither and was only bitten from the surface. A failed runway route also left the food on the same "no route, wait" list the tunnels read. Fixed (galleries on): food near a hole waits for a runway only while one may still come (fewer than 3, a free hole near it, no failed runway route); else it gets a short gallery piece with its feeding gallery under it; runways keep their own list of failed routes. Check: the growth scene puts 4 known hay bales 2 to 4 blocks from 4 holes: 3 runways, the 4th a piece. Lesson: when a new system changes what is common (holes every 4 blocks), look again at every old rule that measured "far from a hole".
47. **Two gallery pieces ending at one hole** (found 2026-10-17 while checking 46: the growth scene failed now and then with the galleries counted 44 blocks longer right after a branch was filled in). A tip sometimes grew a piece whose hole was the tip's own hole (or another tip's), so two pieces ended at one hole; each piece's own start is kept by its hole (`newFrom`), so the second overwrote the first, and filling one in took the other's start away, which then counted its whole tunnel from the nest. The gallery proof, given a new check, found it in 217 places over its landscapes. Fixed: `grow` never ends a piece at its tip's hole or another tip's, the colony refuses any grown piece or piece to food ending at a hole it has, and a save holding such a pair is repaired on load (the longer piece kept, counted from the piece it really grew from). Lesson: anything kept "by hole" needs the holes to be one per piece; check that in the proof.
48. **Termite workers stuck on fungus comb in the far-hay test** (`aBigColonyDigsATunnelToFarHay...`, about 4 runs in 10 on `termites-underground` after step 3, the stuck watcher: "stuck under the ground ... for 401 ticks", motion 0, the next step one block over and one up). Two causes, both about the comb (4 layers, its top half a block up). (a) **The route planner let an insect step up off a slope with no room over it**: from a slope's cell up onto the top of the block beside, the body rises into the cell over the slope on the way, but only the cell it ends in was checked. A comb half a block high under a ceiling has room for an insect on it (`Foothold.LOW_SLOPE_PIXELS`), so a route went comb → onto the mound block beside, and the worker pushed against the block over the comb for ever. Fixed in the legs, species-neutral: that walk needs the cell over the slope open (`AntPathFinder.neighbours`), and `passesThrough` names it for a walk up or down a level, so `whatChanged` replans if it closes. Unit test `noStepUpOffALowCombUnderACeiling` (fails without the fix). (b) **The comb grew round a worker standing on it**: a new layer went on (`FoodStore.addLayerAt`, the foragers' forage) while a worker stood in the cell, which left it standing on the floor under the comb, inside it, with a whole block to climb to the next step. Fixed: anyone in the cell is lifted onto the new layer (`Mound.liftOnto`, as the mound's soil already did); for the harvester's seed heaps too. Check in `termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder` (a worker on the comb when forage goes on; fails without the fix). Why it showed up after step 3 is not proven: with the galleries off the step 3 code looks the same, but the scene differs from run to run (the tunnel 12 or 21 blocks; the colony's dice depend on the test's spot), and both causes need a worker at the wrong comb at the wrong moment. Lesson: a move between two cells checks every cell the body passes, not only where it ends (as `OVER_TOP` and `OVER_EDGE` already did); and the lesson of 6g, 8 and 24 again: anything that fills a cell, even by a layer, decides what happens to whoever is in it.
49. **Open: the full-store seed test fails beside others** (full run before merging `termites-underground`, 2026-10-17, 117 of 118 on 8 servers): `seedsLyingByAFullStoreAreLeftThereAndFetchedOnceThereIsRoom` failed 2 seconds in, `[seed] ant ... could not take its seed to the food store and dropped it (after 0 ticks, 4 failed routes)`: a route failure, not the full-store bug (44) the test guards. Passed 3 of 3 alone (box 4, a leak or timing between tests). Merged with the user's OK; not looked into yet. Next time: read the shard log for the ant's cell and the store spot it routed to.
50. **The away termite test checked the tuft eaten at a fixed moment** (found 2026-10-18 while building brain 4g step 3: `aTermiteColonyAwayForagesFromTheNightsHolesAndGrowsItsGalleriesOnReturn`, "the known tuft by the open hole should be eaten up: cut 3", 2 runs in 4 on the code before step 3 too). The test looked 400 ticks into the night away and wanted all 4 pieces of the tuft cut; the numbers graze at the pace of a living colony, and by then they had sometimes cut 3. Box 3 (too strict): the check now waits until the tuft is eaten, up to 2,000 ticks, as the test's later stage already did. Lesson: a check of "eaten by now" in a living colony waits for it within a fair time, never at one moment.
51. **Fixed 2026-10-20: the long walk test failed now and then early on** (was open) (2026-10-18, while building brain 4g step 4 on `brain-danger`, at the same time as another session ran its own game tests on the same PC): `foragersWalkALongGalleryOutAndBack` failed "no tunnel was planned to the hay: ... reach 24" before tick 1000 (the galleries had not grown out to the hay 38 blocks off yet; when it passes the reach is 42). 4 failures in 12 runs with step 4, all in one stretch of time; 10 of 10 without step 4, and the last 5 of 5 with it. Step 4's code does not run in that scene (no danger there; the numbers' foraging is off in game tests), so it looks like load on the PC or timing, not step 4. Not looked into yet. Next time: log the galleries' length allowed and grown each 200 ticks in the scene, and see what keeps them short. **Seen again 2026-10-20** after the errand helper was merged into `master`: full run on 8 servers 140 of 141, this test the only failure (same message, reach 24), then 2 of 3 alone, so it fails alone too, not only beside other tests. **Cause (found 2026-10-20 from the full run's log):** a real bug in the plant scan (`FoodStore.plants`), not the PC's load. The nest faces one of four ways by the colony's id (`NestLayout.choose`), not by the test's fixed dice; when its own foraging tunnel pointed east, at the hay, the galleries' first arm east was short (6 blocks, hole 14 out) and, planned before the tunnel to the hay, made the young web's range 24, exactly the termites' food radius. The scan only looks along the galleries and at known food when the range is bigger than that inner square, so the known hay 38 out fell out of the plant list and no tunnel was ever planned to it (in the passing runs the tunnel to the hay was planned before the arms, while the range was still the bigger one for the colony's size). In the game: a young colony whose galleries' range is still about 24 never saw known rich food past that (big dead wood that called it, far hay) until its galleries grew. **Fix:** with the galleries on, the scan along them and round known food always runs. **Test:** the long gallery scene now puts the hay down only once the arms are planned, the order that failed (without the fix it failed every run, "hay in the plant scan false"; with it 36 of 37 runs passed alone and beside the hidden trips test, and `termitefood,away,mound,soldiers,hills,trees` 79 of 79 on 8 servers). **Still open:** the one other run timed out at 40000 ticks: the tunnel to the hay was planned and dug, but no worker walked home through its middle (those at the far end stayed in the feeding gallery under the hay all day, then left by the far hole the next night); not seen again in 36 runs; the test now logs where those on their way home are and what they do every 1000 ticks, and each one that comes out under the sky. **Lessons:** a flake that "looks like load" can be a random layout: look for what differs between a passing and a failing log (here which was planned first); a scan limited to "past the inner square" must still run when the outer edge equals the inner one.
52. **Termite soldiers at the foot of a wall: a flip and jaws in the stone** (found 2026-10-18 by the new climbing guard films, `k_climb_minor`, `l_climb_major`, once each body climbs with its own measured feet, `docs/plans/climbing-every-body.md`). (a) The minor soldier's front feet reach only 0.08 blocks ahead of its middle, but its box stops it 0.15 from a wall, and the navigator puts it on the wall 0.12 off: for a tick no corner pose was found (`EdgeOver.findCorner`: the stretch starts when the front feet reach the wall) and the usual wall pose tipped it 30 degrees, then the corner pose took it back to 2 (0, 30, 2, 23, 54: the body flip-flopped and its legs and abdomen went into the grass). (b) The major soldier's head and jaws reach far past its front feet (7.95 units against 2.14), so walking up to a wall its jaws went 0.06 into the stone before the stretch began. Fixed in the corner pose, for every body: on the wall before its front feet get there it is drawn lying on the floor, as at the stretch's start; and the stretch starts when the front feet or the head (its front end, less the half of its box in front of the middle) reach the new surface, whichever is first. The harvester worker's feet reach farther than its head, so nothing changed for it (every ant guard film picture for picture the same). Lesson: a number measured from a model can be smaller than what the old shared number hid (the box, the navigator's own distances); film every body, not only the one the code was made for.
53. **Fixed 2026-10-18: coming head first down a wall onto the floor, insects stopped and turned round on the spot** (seen 2026-10-18 in the climbing guard films of the minor soldier and the king, also on the code before the spine): on the wall the insect's yaw was left facing into the wall, so when the navigator put it on the floor its yaw jumped 180 degrees and the drawn body swung round over 3 ticks (the king's yaw 90, 75, 45, -15, -90); and the walk off the wall started from a standstill with the game not yet knowing it stood on the floor (one tick "in the air", where a mob only creeps), so it stood 2 ticks, crept 0.005 a tick for 3, then dashed on (0.09 a tick for the minor) and the body jumped 0.2 to 0.3 blocks and tipped 36 degrees in a tick. **Fix (legs, every body):** (a) on a wall the yaw is the way the head points with the wall laid flat in front of it, wall yaw minus heading (into the wall going up, as before; out from it going down; along it sideways), so off the foot or over the top the body already faces the way it goes on (the drawn body on a wall is turned by the wall and the heading, so nothing changes there); (b) the walk straight out at the foot (56) is an exact motion at the climbing pace, as on the wall; (c) letting go onto a floor the insect keeps its pace along the floor and stands on it from that tick (`AbstractAntEntity`, `lastClimbMotion`). Walker logs after: the king's yaw stays 270 and it walks on at its pace (tilt 56, 42, 28, 16, 8, 3, 0); the minor tips 84 to 0 in 6 ticks, no spin, no dash. `legs` 21 of 21; guard films approved again (the foot of each climb down). **Lesson:** a body handed from one surface to another needs every number continuous across the hand-over: its yaw, its speed, and the game's own "on the ground" flag.
54. **Fixed 2026-10-18 (the user's OK): going head first over the top of a block the rear points up into the air** (seen 2026-10-18 in `m_climb_king` and `f_termite`, and the same for the harvester ant in `c_climb`, whose climbing the user approved in-game). The spine's rule makes each segment lie the way the thorax lay when the body's middle was where the segment is now; but the thorax tips over an edge from when its front feet reach it, so the path it remembers is tilted before the ground is, and segments still over the flat top lie tilted (the gaster or abdomen raised up to 45 degrees). The real rule: the parts behind the hind feet follow the ground the hind feet walked over. That changes the ant's approved look, so it waits for the user's OK. **Fix:** the spine remembers the ground under the hind feet, not the thorax's tilt (across an edge or a corner the thorax leans from the old surface to the new one; `EdgeOver.Pose.fromTilt` says which surface the hind feet stand on); the parts of a piece in front of the hind feet lie along the thorax, the parts behind them on the ground the hind feet walked over; and a piece is judged by its own body (`from` to `to`: the ant's waist piece by its gaster, not its petiole). `SpineTest.headFirstOverATopTheRearLiesOnTheGroundItWalked` checks it for a 6-segment abdomen and the ant, and that the old rule fails it. **Lesson:** a body part rests on the ground under it, not on where the body above it pointed earlier; a straight part spanning an outside corner still grazes it (the ant's gaster, up to 0.03 blocks, approved in `c_climb` and `d_ledge`).
55. **Neighbouring tests' termites met as neighbours** (2026-10-18, the first brain run after merging step 5 into `brain-danger`, `colony,antnest,away,termitefood` 74 of 76 on 8 servers; numbered 55 as 52 to 54 are on `climbing-every-body`): `aTermiteColonyAwayForagesFromTheNightsHolesAndGrowsItsGalleriesOnReturn` ("the grown piece's hole by known food should open") and `foragersWalkALongGalleryOutAndBack` ("reach 24", as in 51) failed side by side and passed alone (box 4). Likely cause: step 5 looks for other Macrotermes colonies within twice the galleries' reach (about 100 blocks), and the arenas are about 45 apart, so a colony could meet the next test's colony and stop or wall off its galleries. Fixed: a game test's colony only meets colonies of its own arena (`Neighbours.setTestFence`, set by `TestIsolation`, used by the galleries and by ants meeting ants and trails). **Not rerun yet** (the user's call, to save a 13-minute run); the next brain run shows whether both failures are gone (51 may still be timing). Lesson: every new reach between colonies needs the test fence, as the trails did. **2026-10-19:** the gallery test failed again side by side (`trees,termitefood,away` on 8 servers, the same message) and passed alone: the fence did not end it; still open. Later the same day (giant trees step 5e) it failed alone too, about 1 run in 3 (3 of 6 with the tree work, 1 of 4 on the commit before the fall, fire and lightning): not caused by the trees, and not only a leak between tests; still open. **2026-10-19 (the errand helper's full run, 8 servers):** the two neighbour tests themselves failed side by side (`neighbouringTermitesWallOffTheirGalleries`: the young colony never walled off a gallery; `oldColoniesYieldToNeighboursAndYoungOnesPushOn`: the workers never told their colony of the meeting) and passed 3 of 3 alone: still a leak, still open. **2026-10-22:** not the unticked ground round the arena (fixed then, `TestIsolation.GROUND_ROUND`): `neighbouringTermitesWallOffTheirGalleries` still failed 1 round in 5 side by side and once in the full run after. Two different ends: once the old colony walled off fast and the young one met it only 2.3 times (needs 3.4 at day 1.6), so the meetings stopped coming; once the young one's meeting was 49.8 strong and it yielded, yet walled nothing off. The second looks like a real gap (yielding without walling the piece off); still open. **Looked into 2026-10-22, then set aside at the user's call (minor):** it fails **alone** too, 5 of 15 runs (3 "yields but walls nothing", 2 "too few meetings"), so it is not a leak between tests. In the "yields but walls nothing" runs the young colony reaches its 4 meetings in a tenth of a day and yields, and its meeting strength keeps climbing to 49.8, yet `Galleries.wallOffNeighbours` never gets as far as the yielding piece for it: a log of every skip (not a leaf, one of the 4 arms, a way to food, `fillIn` refused and why) printed nothing for the young colony. The climbing strength comes from the old colony's upkeep, which tells both colonies of each meeting (`galleriesMet`). Lead for later: log, for the young colony, every piece `closestTo` finds near the old one and what `yieldsAt` says there (the young's nearest cell may lie outside the meeting's 30-degree slice, or its piece may not be a leaf); the in-game cost is only a young colony that gives way but leaves its tunnel open. **Seen again 2026-11-04** (`neighbouringTermitesWallOffTheirGalleries`, "the young colony never walled off a gallery", 1 run of `raids,away,soldiers,colony` on 6 servers during the raid code review's first fixes; 6 of 6 alone right after; the raid changes do not touch termites).
   **Fixed 2026-10-23 (Apex part 3, T5, branch `claude/apex-termites`):** the neighbours test repeated 6 at once (a new switch, `ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`, every server runs every chosen test) showed both ends. (a) "Yields but walls nothing", a real gap (box 1): the young colony remembered the meeting where it first met the old one's galleries; its own tip later stopped a block or two short of there (the piece planned on toward the old colony was never dug), so its nearest cell lay 4 blocks nearer home than the meeting, past the 3-block slack of the yield check (`numbers.within`), while meetings merge over 4 (`merge_within`): it yielded, but not "there". Now a leaf piece is walled off when the colony yields at its own cell there or where the neighbour's gallery it senses lies (`GalleryUpkeep.wallOffNeighbours`); that is the agreed design, a young colony that gives way seals its tunnel. (b) "Too few meetings" is the design working (box 3): the old colony yields and walls its own pieces off first, the meetings stop, and the young one, never yielding, pushes on and walls nothing off; the test now passes that end at its time limit and still fails a young colony that yields and walls nothing off. After: 6 of 6 (2 of them the second end). Lesson: when places are merged with one slack, every check of "is this the same place" must allow at least that slack.
57. **Fixed 2026-10-18: going over the side edge of a ridge after walking along it, the worker's tilt wobbled** (found 2026-10-18 by the new guard film `n_down_turn`): the body tipped 16, then 46, back to 36, then 48 degrees in 4 ticks, the antennae and legs jumping up to 0.23 blocks. Found from a server log of the walker: on the last tick of the move out over the edge, the step onto the wall was across the wall with a drift of 0.00004 blocks sideways in it (from the walk along the ridge), and the wall heading was worked out from any motion over 0.00001, so the head pointed sideways (-90) for one tick; the edge pose (`EdgeOver`) only draws a body going straight up or down, so for that tick the usual pose drew it (46 degrees) and then the edge pose again (36). **Fix (legs):** only a motion mostly along the wall (more than half of it) turns the head, and a hair's correction (`moveAttached`'s square-up) never does (`AntNavigator.faceTheWayItGoes`, `squaring`; the ceiling too). After: tilt 0.6, 7, 16, 25, 36, 48, 59, 70, 79, 86, 90, the antennae and leg jumps gone (20 jumps to 2). Still there, as approved in `c_climb` and `d_ledge` (54): the gaster grazes the corner 0.03 and swings 0.13 a tick as it passes the edge (the waist straightening from its 60-degree reach). **Lesson:** a heading worked out from a motion must ignore the parts of the motion that are not along the surface; the body layer trusted it, and one wrong tick showed as a wobble.
58. **Fallen giants on a slope stood on walls of wood or floated** (the user's report 2026-10-19, with pictures of trees in new land: "they either float in the air or become super thick"). The fallen log's shape is made for flat ground, and the placer's rule for a trunk's foot ("where the ground dips under it, the trunk goes down to meet it", up to 8 blocks) was given to every wood block at or under the foot's height: the whole lying trunk and the broken limbs round a dead tree. Down a slope each column of the log grew a post of wood down to the ground (the walls); where the ground fell more than 8, the log floated; up a slope it went into the hill. **Fix** (`TreePlacer.rest`, used for new land and for trees falling in play): the stump stands; the trunk and each of its limb stubs lie as rigid logs, 1-block slices along each one resting on the highest ground under its middle line, never more steeply than 1 in 1 (where the ground drops faster it bridges, as a real log does); the loose pieces (broken limbs, fallen branches, marked by the shape maker) lie on the ground under them; nothing reaches down. New land reads the land's height from the world's shape itself (the same for every chunk); in play from the blocks, a tree's own wood and leaves not counted as ground. The falling animation now swings down (or up) to where the log will lie. New game tests `aFallenTreeLiesOnTheSlopeDownhill` (over made-up land down, up, across and over a cliff, for 8 trees; then a real fall down a hill, and the broken limbs round the standing dead tree on it) and `...Uphill` (a fall into a bowl); the hill test caught two more problems on the way (the limb stubs fitted to the middle one's ground, and broken limbs touching the log taken for part of it). **Lesson:** a rule for one part of a shape (the trunk's foot) must not be keyed on height alone; and test shapes on land that is not flat.
    **Part 2 (the user's test 2026-10-19: "so much better", but a log down a steep hill still lay as a stiff staircase with parts floating; the user's rule: every block touches the ground, the log keeping its shape and the thick hollow base staying thick for the Matabele ants).** The rigid 1-in-1 log bridged steep drops and rested each slice on its highest point. **Fix:** each 1-block slice lies with its underside on the ground, as one piece where the ground under it is even, else each column on its own ground; every column keeps all its blocks (full thickness, the hollow open); no slope limit (a real hollow giant cracks and settles along the land). A first try let a slice bed 1 block into the soil; the placer never puts wood into soil, so the hollow's floor was skipped and its roof hung over a gap: no bedding in. Fallen trees in new land now lie on fairly flat ground (`GiantTreeStructure.flatFall`: the land under the log within 3 of the foot, up to 4 falls tried, else the tree stands shed; the user's wish). The slope tests now check every column touching and keeping its thickness, and on a real hill and bowl that no column of the log has air under it. **Lesson:** a block that lands inside the ground is not placed, so a fit must never put the underside below the ground.
59. **The termite galleries away test fails about half its runs, alone too** (found 2026-10-19 by the full run before merging `claude/tree-death-8ad727`, 137 of 139 on 6 servers; the other failure, `foragersWalkALongGalleryOutAndBack`, "reach 24", passed alone as in 51 and 55). `aTermiteColonyAwayForagesFromTheNightsHolesAndGrowsItsGalleriesOnReturn` fails alone on the code before step 5 too (3 of 5 runs), so the tree work did not cause it; the runs differ from each other (13 or 19 gallery pieces grown on return), so something in it is not the same every run. Two checks fail: (a) "the grown piece's hole by known food should open": the test picked the farthest leaf piece, which can be the one piece still not dug (the test allows one), and a hole not dug never opens: **fixed in the test** (it picks a dug piece, box 3); (b) "the piece to (-15, -29) led to no food for 11 days and was not filled in at once": still open; waiting up to 1200 ticks did not help, so it is not a termite standing in it. **Fixed 2026-10-19 (box 1, a real game bug):** (-15, -29) was the root of a dead branch on the west side. Just back, the builders' time grows the galleries (`BuildLife`, `growNow`) in the same second before the catch-up fill-in runs, and the even spreading picked the dead branch's tip and grew one more piece from it: the dead pieces were then no leaf any more, so never filled, and the new piece was fresh. What differs between runs: the colony's dice are seeded from its id, new every run, so the tips are shuffled in a different order (10 to 19 pieces grown on return); only some orders reached the dead tip first. Now a tip on a piece due to be filled in (no food for `fill_days` days) is never spread from at random, like a backfilled way (`Galleries.isDue`, used by the fill-in too). The test passes 6 of 6 alone (8 pieces left each time, 11 to 16 filled in), `termitefood,away` 37 of 38 on 6 servers (6.5 minutes; the one failure the old fallen tree up a slope flake, which passed 3 of 3 alone). Lesson: work done "at once on return" must happen in the order it would have happened while away (fill the dead ways, then grow).

56. **Coming down a block, the body slid sideways with its abdomen still raised before it turned** (the user's report 2026-10-18, put off since the first climbing: "they go down a block and slide to the left or right depending on where they wanted to go before actually turning"; "their abdomens are still raised when they turn"; ants and termites alike). The walking code (`AntNavigator.offWall`) ended the step at the foot of the wall the moment the feet touched the floor, so a route turning there turned at once: in the new probe film (`tools/film/guard/n_down_turn`, a ridge one block wide) the worker swung about 110 degrees in 2 ticks with its body still tipped 45 degrees and its waist bent 60 degrees up the wall, and then moved sideways while the drawn body was still lying back against the wall; the major soldier was still tipped for 15 ticks as it slid. **Fix (legs, every body):** after the body tips back onto the floor it walks straight out from the wall by its box's width (the worker 0.3 blocks, the major soldier 0.4, the most a cell allows) and only then goes on along its route, turning at once. Worker: straight 4 ticks, flat (tilt 0, waist 9 degrees), then the turn; major: level, then the turn. **Lesson:** a step that hands the body to the floor is not done when the feet land but when the whole body is on it; the old open bug 53 (termites stopping to turn round at the foot) is the same spot, see it there.

60. **Termites dug to a dead tree, then idled at the tunnel's end till night** (the user's report 2026-10-19, with a picture; branch `claude/tree-deadwood-1cf024`). The user's `run/logs/debug.log` showed every digger asking for the same gallery block under the tree and finding "no way" to stand next to it; the saved world (read block by block with a small script) showed the gallery stepping one block down from the tunnel there, and the route planner on a copy of those blocks found the way easily. The cause was not the legs but the day rule: by day a termite may only walk to a cell that counts as inside, and a feeding gallery cell counted as inside only with a solid block right over it, so the lower step of the gallery, under the upper step's open space, looked open to the sky. **Fix:** `Nest.isInside` takes a gallery cell with another inside cell over it as inside, from the top down. A second bug, found by putting the test's tree in a dip like the user's: `DeadWoodCall` could pick a root buried under the grass as the wood's foot, which the colony never lists as food, so no tunnel was planned; the foot is now wood lying open. **Lessons:** when diggers say "no way", check the rules for where an insect may end a walk (`mayBeAt`), not only the route planner; a scene on flat ground hid both bugs, so put test trees on land like the user's (here a dip a block deep); the user's saved world and debug log answer "what was different in my game" exactly.
61. **Termites ate a dead tree's foot and stopped** (2026-10-19, found by the dead tree test once it asked for the trunk eaten 3 blocks up; branch `claude/tree-deadwood-1cf024`). By day a forager only goes for food the colony knows of, and food is learnt by workers seeing it from outside, at night. The wood over a block eaten into a mud cast is inside the tree: nobody ever saw it, so the colony ate the blocks it knew and stopped. **Fix:** eating a block through teaches the colony the wood touching it, a worker inside feels the wood in its reach, and wood over a cast is listed. **Lessons:** food reached only from inside needs its own way of being known; a test that stops at the first bite hides what happens after it; switch a fix off once to prove the test catches it.
