62. **Open: the fallen tree up a bowl fails beside other tests** (found 2026-10-19 by the full run before merging `claude/tree-deadwood-1cf024`: 139 of 141 on 6 servers, the other failure the gallery away flake, 59). `aFallenTreeLiesOnTheSlopeUphill` said 3 of 319 log columns hung a block over the ground, then passed 3 of 3 runs alone (box 4, a leak between tests). Best guess, not checked: the trees live in one book for the whole world (`Forest`), and a neighbouring test's giant (a crown 40 to 50 wide, fallen logs over 30 long) can reach into the 30-block square this test looks at. Next: fence the test's look to its own tree's blocks, or keep other tests' trees out of its square.
   **Looked into 2026-10-23 (Apex part 3, T5), still open:** every failure since 2026-10-22, on every branch, names the same three columns (5 and 6 east, 19 and 20 south of the trunk), and under them the bowl's ground is gone, air 5 blocks down, so the log is fine and the ground went. Not the test's own clean-up (it runs only after the checks pass), not the trees alone (12 of 12 alone, a watch on those columns saw nothing), and not the long tunnel dug at once by `hiddenTripsGoUnseen...` in the arrangements tried (its tunnel stays in its arena); the failing server's 24 tests rerun together passed 3 of 3 (the tests' places differ run to run). Now the test watches those columns until the log lands and, if the ground goes, logs the tick and every test within 40 blocks with its arena's corner (`[bowl-ground]`, `TestIsolation.describeTestsNear`), so the next failure in a full run names the neighbour. Nothing in-game is touched by this test's failure: its trees lie right alone.

63. **Open (tools, not the game): rerunning `tools/grama_art.py` would undo the big blue grama meadows** (found 2026-10-23 by the split's step S8, which reran every art tool to prove the paths helper writes each file where it was). The game's `desert/.../worldgen/configured_feature/blue_grama.json` holds the bigger meadows the user asked for on 2026-10-17 (a weighted radius: 7 in 8 patches 2 to 4 blocks, 1 in 8 patches 7 to 10), written by hand; the tool still writes the old single radius (2 to 4). Nothing changes in-game until someone reruns the tool. Every other file the tools rewrote came out the same. Fix later: put the weighted radius into `grama_art.py`'s config for blue grama. **Lesson:** a game file changed by hand must be changed in the tool that writes it too, or the next rerun undoes it.

64. **Fixed 2026-10-23: a game could fail to start, "Ant & Termite Colonies has failed to load correctly" (ConcurrentModificationException)** (found by the split's step S8 full run: 1 test server of 6 never started, so its 24 tests did not run). Since S6 and S7 there are three mods, and the game builds its mods side by side on several threads. The savanna and the desert mod both sign their rows up in the framework while they are built (creative menu rows, test categories, test fences and save checks, each species' bodies), into plain lists that only one writer at a time may touch; now and then the two wrote at the same moment and the list broke. A start-up race, so about 1 launch in many, in the real game too. **Fix:** the five sign-ups (`ModEvents.addToCreativeTab`, `TestFilter.addCategory`, `AwayKit.addPartsCheck`, `TestIsolation.addFence`, `SpeciesBodies.register`) take turns (`synchronized`, as `ColonyParts.register` and `BodyPlans.signUp` already did). Nothing else changes: within one mod the order is as before, and nothing depends on which species signs up first (the menu rows are sorted by their places, the tests are split over the servers by name). **Lesson:** anything a species mod signs up into the framework while it loads must be safe for two mods at once.

**Open, quarantined (not blocking a merge, fix later; the 4-box rule):**
- **`aFallenTreeUpASlopeKeepsItsHoleWhenItsLowestBlockIsEaten` fails about 2 runs in 10 beside other tests** (seen in the full run on `master`, 2026-10-16, and in reruns of that server's 13 tests; passed alone each time tried). What is known: no alarm, foraging time, the hole may open; the tunnel (13 or 21 blocks: the plan differs from run to run) is dug, then the feeding gallery up under the tree stops at one block (2 of 7 dug; the colony's next dig target stays that block for the rest of the test, 13,000+ ticks), while the workers stand idle elsewhere (3 in one spot in the tunnel) and none ever gives up on it with "no way there" (a debug line in `DigGoal` showed none in passing runs; it did not catch a failing one). Because the colony hands out its first undug block, everything after it (the rest of the gallery, then the hole) waits. Suspects: the termites' walking or climbing up the slope to that block (the `AntNavigator` clean-up, roadmap 3a part 2, is the place), or workers that never ask for digging work. The test's progress line now logs the gallery, the next dig target, the hole and where the workers are. Since the navigator tidy-up (2026-10-16) it also logs the next dig block's stand spots and each worker's role and running goals, and `DigGoal` logs every give-up (`[dig] ... found no way to` / `gave up on ... out of reach`) to `debug.log`: the next failing run says which. The tidy-up's full run passed it (111 of 111). First suspect from reading the code, not yet seen: a digger that arrives at its stand spot but is still not within reach walks there 60 times, gives up and is handed the same block again.
- **`aHungryWorkerEatsOneSeedFromTheFoodStore` failed once** (2026-10-16, the `antnest,colony,away` run on 8 servers after the search fix, "nobody has eaten a seed yet" 48,000 ticks after the worker was made hungry), beside 4 neighbours; it then passed 4 of 4 alone and 3 of 3 with the same 4 neighbours. Not known whether the search fix plays a part (a hungry forager out on a 5-minute search eats only once home; ants were seen drifting to the test arena's walls and climbing them). The test now logs where the hungry worker is, whether it is searching and its goals every 2,000 ticks, so the next failure shows where it was.

**How to find the next one fast**
- Ask the user for F3 coordinates and `run\logs\latest.log`; add a one-line `[tag]` log where an ant gives up, with the spot, the ant's cell, the navigator state and what is in the spot. That found the hill jam in one round.
- Most pathing bugs come from one of: the start cell is in the air or inside a block, the goal cell is where traffic passes, the route has one step, or "done" is decided from a cell instead of from real progress.
65. **Alarm stuck on for ever after a chicken fell into a foraging hole** (the user's in-game test of the split, 2026-10-23). A young colony with no soldiers yet: a chicken waited by the exit; the termites kept trying to leave and sealed themselves in again, then dug up and under a dead tree round it (looked right). The alarm settled and a night forager set out for the tree; the chicken ran it down and fell into the hole the termite fled to. From then on the colony stayed on alarm, even after the user killed the chicken. Not caused by the split. **FIXED 2026-10-23** (branch `claude/alarm-stuck-65-c2133c`). **What the user's game log and save showed:** the alarm itself was not stuck: a living chicken sitting in an open hole keeps it at 2 (right: a predator in the doorway), and after the kill it fell to 0 in its calm time (2 game minutes at 2, half a minute at 1); at 2000 ticks a second that is about 25 seconds, and the night ended meanwhile (termites stay in by day), so no forager was ever seen. The real stuck part was the **danger memory**: the chicken pecked one worker to death (a worker has 3 health, a peck takes 1: 3 pecks) and a second worker once, all within 2 seconds, and every peck counted as a separate attack: hurt 2 + 2 + 2, killed 4, strength 10. Strength 6 is the line for "attacked again and again", so the hole by the dead tree was **left for good** (never opened again) and the tree, the colony's food, was kept off for about 3.3 days. **Fix:** (a) each member counts once an attack, at its worst wound (`DangerMap.hurt` with the member; the same member hurt again within `seen_every_ticks` adds only what its worse wound adds); (b) a place is left for good only after at least **2 separate attacks** (`DangerMap.Place.attacks`: hurts and deaths less than `seen_every_ticks` apart are one; saved). The user's case is now a death and a hurt, 6, one attack: the hole closes for about 2.5 days, then opens on its next turn. Test: `holesTakeTurnsNearFoodFirstAndMoreInTheRain` now has the chicken (2 pecks and a kill on one worker, a peck on another, then it sits in the hole and is killed): it failed on the old code with strength 10; it now checks the strength (at most a death and a hurt), one attack, the alarm at 2, the alarm back at 0 within its calm times after the kill, foraging time again, no hole left for good, and the hole back in the turns the night after the memory fades. **Lessons:** count the thing the rule is about (members attacked, raids), not the events that report it (pecks); and when the user says "stuck", read the log and the save first: here the alarm was fine and the stuck part was a rule two steps away.
66. **Fixed 2026-10-22: a termite missed a jump it had walked out of range of before it listened** (found by `termitesFeelNoSneakingStepsButFreezeAtAJump` failing once in the split's round 2 full run, 140 of 143 on 6 servers: "the termite has not frozen at the jump ... froze at -1"; it passed 62 of 62 runs alone and beside the same neighbours). `NoticeGoal.canUse` runs once a second, and a jump was remembered for a second (`GroundShakes.hardSince`), its distance measured at that check, not at the jump. A termite 5 blocks off strolling away (about 0.1 blocks a tick) was 6.3 blocks off when it listened, past its 6-block shake range, and never froze; the test jumps only once, so one miss failed it. Proved by making the termite walk away at every jump: each copy whose check fell more than about 10 ticks after the jump failed (5 of 31). Fixed (box 1, the user's pick A): the jump reaches every insect in range the moment it happens (`GroundShakes.hard` finds them and measures there; `AbstractAntEntity.feelShake`), and the goal freezes it on its next step without waiting for its once-a-second look; the test has the termite walking away as the player jumps and fails a freeze more than 3 ticks late. Lesson: something that happens in an instant (a jump, a hit) must be measured when it happens; a check that polls once a second, asking about the last second, measures it a second late. Real vibrations reach a termite at once.
67. **Fixed 2026-10-23: a test's ground round its arena stopped running when a neighbouring test finished** (found 2026-10-22 while fixing 63, branch `claude/test-ground-tickets`; test harness only, nothing changes in-game). `TestIsolation` keeps the chunks within `GROUND_ROUND` (32) blocks of each running arena running with a loading ticket per chunk, given back when the test is done. Neighbouring tests' grounds overlap, and Minecraft keeps equal tickets (same kind, level and chunk) as one with no count (`DistanceManager.addTicket` folds them with `addOrGet`, `removeTicket` drops it), so the first of two tests to finish took the shared chunk's only ticket away and the other test's insects there froze (the leak `GROUND_ROUND` was made against). Proved with a log in `termitefood,away` on 6 servers: before, 318 of the 1,073 shared chunks that were running stopped when a neighbour finished (the rest were still kept by the other arena's own tickets); after, 0 of 1,140. Fixed: each ground chunk counts the tests holding it (`GROUND_HOLDERS`), the first takes the ticket, the last gives it back. `termitefood,away,trees` 55 of 56 on 6 servers (the one failure the long walk timeout, 51, 3 of 3 alone). It may explain some of the side-by-side flakes (55, 62): watch whether they still fail beside others. Side note: in short tests (the tree tests, a few hundred ticks) the ground's outer chunks are often still being made and never run before the test ends; harmless for them. Lesson: Minecraft tickets are a set, not a count; whoever shares one must count the holders.
68. **The harvester neighbours test fails in the first batch, never alone** (found 2026-10-23 in Apex part 3's T5 full run, branch `claude/apex-termites`; an old flake, not from T3 or T4: the same failure on 2026-10-19 and twice on the morning of 2026-10-23, before the merge). `oldColoniesYieldToNeighboursAndYoungOnesPushOn` (desert) fails at tick 45 with neither colony holding a meeting, though its two workers stood a block apart halfway between the nests. Each time it ran in its server's first batch, about 30 seconds after the start, beside 20 or more other tests; alone it passes 6 of 6 (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`). Guess, not proved: the test fence (`Neighbours.canMeet`, set once from the arena `TestIsolation` reads) may be read wrong at the start, or the workers are not out on the surface. Looked into: the failure now prints whether each colony may meet the other, both nests' middles, each worker's place, sky and life, and the tests nearby, so the next failure tells which. Still open.
69. **A dead mound's spire piece crumbled from under a player's block** (found 2026-10-24 building M8 step 1a part 3, branch `claude/m8-dead-mound-holes-09e831`; part 2's own gap, not from part 3). The rule: a block a player put keeps the column under it. The columns kept to it, but the pieces (spire tips, mouths, ramps) crumbled from their own list without looking up, so a thin spire piece under a player's cobblestone went and the cobblestone floated. The dead mound scene's random mound shape put its cobblestone on a piece about 1 run in 18 (the scene asks for any column 7 blocks tall). Fixed: a piece with a player's block over it, straight or on the mound above it, stays (`DeadMounds.underPlayersBlock`), forgotten from the list so the mound still settles; the scene leaves out pieces under the player's block. Also found in the same scene: its "open to the sky" checks read the sky light, which lags behind a whole mound worn away in one tick, so they saw almost nothing; now they read the height map (`underTheSky`), and so does the game's "mud under the open sky" (`DeadWorks`). The dead mound scene 24 of 24 (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`, 4 runs on 6 servers). Lesson: a "never touch a player's block" rule must be checked by every path that removes blocks, not only the main one; and a test that asks the sky light right after many block changes asks a stale map.
71. **The game crashed in the fungus garden: "Cannot get property layers ... in air"** (the user's test of M8 step 1b, 2026-10-24, a new world with many termite colonies; `FungusGarden.grow`). Each second the garden lists its comb blocks, then ages them one by one. When a comb's last layer crumbled away it became air, and a comb lying on top of it (in a garden room with a stepped floor, or one the player dug under) lost its floor and dropped out at once. Later in the same loop the garden read that cell as comb and crashed the game. Fixed: a listed comb that is no longer comb is skipped, there and in the away catch-up's look for comb to ripen or age. Lesson: a list of blocks made at the start of a loop can go stale inside it, when one block's change knocks out a neighbour; check the block again before reading its properties.
72. **Matabele workers piled up at the top of the chimney while the queen walked in** (the user's report, 2026-10-24, a dead mound from `/antcolonies newmound`, a queen and spawn-egg workers; about 15 ants stacked in one cell of the hole). "Go inside" took a worker to the first cell that counts as inside the nest, and the top of the old chimney already does; the wander goal looks only 3 blocks up and down for a floor, and the rooms lie 7 to 8 blocks down the shaft, so nothing ever took them further. The queen has her own goal down to her room. The dead mound scene passed because it only checked "inside". Fixed: a species setting `worker.rest_in_rooms` (the Matabele's: real Megaponera crowd into their chamber with the queen and the brood); such a worker that stays in walks down into the nest's rooms and strolls only in them; the scene now checks every worker is in a room, 6 of 6. Lesson: "inside the nest" includes the shaft, so a check that an insect got home must ask where it settled, not only whether it is inside.
73. **The queued mound test failed every run alone: "not put in yet"** (2026-10-24, `aQueuedMoundIsGrownAsideThenPutInQuickly`, category `mound`). Not from the mound spacing or tree commits: it fails the same on the commit that added it (f376cf6). The game was right (box 3, the test): a queued mound waits until its shape is grown in the background, about a second of work, while the test server runs its ticks as fast as it can, so the test's 400 ticks were over in about a third of a second. It only passed when other tests slowed the server down. In play 400 ticks are 20 seconds. **Fix (test only, nothing changes in-game):** while the mound is not in yet, the test waits for the background growing (`MoundPlan.awaitGrowingForTests`), then checks again; putting it in must still take under 400 ms. 3 of 3 alone (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`). **Lesson:** a game test counts ticks, not seconds; anything done on another thread in real time must be waited for, or a fast test server outruns it.
70. **A hole into a fungus garden full of comb was never sealed** (the user's in-game test of M8 step 1b, 2026-10-24: a pit dug down into a grown termite nest stayed open; "the termites can't repair the ceiling when there's fungus under them"). Old, not from step 1b: new-land colonies just have gardens full of comb from the start, so it shows at once. **Cause:** a worker closes a hole from a nest cell beside it (`NestWalls.plugSpots`, `findPlugSpot`), and only an *empty* (air) cell counted. A garden's floor is all comb, a low heap the insects walk on (the legs allow it, `Foothold.LOW_SLOPE_PIXELS`), so a hole in a garden's ceiling or wall had nowhere to stand (`stand=null` in the leak list) and every worker skipped it, for ever. Not the unloading: the repair claims are not saved and the 20-second "leave alone" memory runs out. **Fix:** a cell with a heap no higher than half a block (comb, a low seed heap) is a place to stand and work from (`NestWalls.hasRoomToStand`), species-neutral. Test: `termitesSealOpeningsInTheirNestWithMoundSoil` now lays comb over the whole garden floor before cutting the holes; it failed 3 of 3 on the old code (both garden holes `stand=null`), passes 6 of 6 with the fix (the scene lifts any worker standing in the garden onto the new comb, as the game does, bug 48; without that, workers buried in the test's comb could not walk out and the test failed once beside others); `mound,termitefood,away` 53 of 53 on 6 servers. Lesson: a rule that asks "is this cell empty?" for where an insect can be must ask the legs' question instead ("can an insect stand here?"), or every heap the colony lays on its floors quietly turns into a wall.
74. **Some Matabele ants showed the purple-and-black missing-texture squares** (the user's report, 2026-10-24, after M8 step 1f: only ants from `/antcolonies matabele` or new land, never from spawn eggs). Young workers get a pale copy of their skin drawn over the body (`client/YoungPaleLayer`, real callows are paler and darken with age), looked up as `<texture>_young.png`; the Matabele never had one. Spawn-egg ants count as fully grown, but the command and new land give a colony workers of every age, so the youngest asked for the missing file. Fixed: `matabele_major_young.png` and `matabele_minor_young.png` (reddish brown, real young Megaponera are brown before they turn black; my pick, `tools/young_textures.py`), and the layer now draws nothing for a model with no pale copy. Lesson: a new model drawn by `AntRenderer` needs its `_young` texture (add it to `tools/young_textures.py`); the layer no longer shows the squares if one is forgotten, it just shows no youth.
75. **Open: the minors at the holes test failed once beside the fight tests** (found 2026-10-25 on branch `claude/m8-fights`, the run of `soldiers,matabelefighttests` on 6 servers, after reworking the Matabele attack clips; only clip files and the fight test changed since a full run in which it passed). `minorsGuardTheHolesAndLineTheForagersTrailThenGoInLast` said "a minor went in while a worker was still outside"; it then passed 6 of 6 alone (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`). Box 4 (passes alone, fails beside others): its neighbour on that server was a Matabele fight test (lone termites and Matabele, 65 blocks off); the leak between them not found yet. **Looked into 2026-10-25 (branch `claude/festive-mirzakhani-d70ff6`), still open: not a leak from the fight test.** The fight test was already finished and its arena gone seconds before the failure; 216 more runs of the pair on 6 servers failed 2 times (about 1 in 100), and no shared state (alarm, danger map, fight orders) reaches across. It is this test's own rare flake, two faces: (a) the original, the first minor in on the same tick the last worker came in; (b) caught twice with full logs: one worker stuck on the surface for 1,000+ ticks one block beside the nest's entrance, which the test turns to stone (its `GoInsideGoal` found a route but never moved off that cell; a `DigGoal` started there too), so the minors went in after their 300-tick escort while it was still out; it got in on its own about 1,300 ticks into the day. 36 runs logged every minor-in-while-minors-out tick: none, so the escort rule itself holds. The test now names the worker outside, what it is doing (`AbstractAntEntity.runningGoals`) and the holes, and logs every worker out by day. Next: find why a worker is wedged beside the stone entrance (legs, or the test's stone entrance being a dead end the game never makes). Lesson: when a test fails beside another, check first whether the neighbour was still running (the log's [+_] marks); a 1-in-100 flake can look like a leak in a small sample.
76. **Matabele ants camped one corner till a termite came by** (the user's in-game test of the fight polish, 2026-10-25, `/antcolonies skirmish`: about 6 ants piled in one corner, "until a termite came along and got instantly killed there"). Cause: the fight spot is the food itself (the hay bale, the dead wood), a solid block; an ant more than 6 blocks off planned a route into that block every tick, found none and never moved (only a termite coming within its 12 blocks woke it). A second, smaller one: the search round the spot could pick places so near the ant that it had already arrived, so it "searched" standing still. Fix (`FightGoal`): it walks to any foothold within 4 blocks of the spot, never into it; with no way there it searches round where it is and tries again a few seconds later; each search step goes somewhere new. The ten-ant fight test now fails if an ant stands in one block for half a minute while the fight is on (it caught the bug in 3 of 4 runs on the old code; 12 of 12 pass now); its "many escape" check is now a third of the foragers (with the ants all hunting, 1 of 4 out once escaped). Lesson: a goal handed a place to "go to" must ask whether that place can be stood on; a route search that fails every tick with nothing else happening is a stuck insect.
77. **Open: three Matabele fight tests flake about 1 run in 5 together** (found 2026-10-25 while building M8 step 2d, lost legs, on `claude/m8-fights`; `ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1 ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=matabelefighttests`, 6 servers a round): the same on the code before 2d (3 failures in 18 runs) and with it (4 in 18), so not caused by 2d. (a) `tenMatabelesAtAFeedingSpotTakeMinutesAndManyTermitesEscape`: "a Matabele has stood at ... for half a minute while the fight is on", 3 times, the ant about 15 blocks from the hay, across the arena (the BUGS 76 check: an ant still stuck somewhere, or a still ant that is not stuck, e.g. one that has given up but whose fight is not over); (b) `matabeleStingParalysesATermiteAndASoldierBitesBack`: "the termite was felled 4 (or 6) ticks into the blow; the sting goes in at 11", 3 times (something other than the blow's sting fells it early, or a second blow's clock is read); (c) `matabeleMajorBitesTheMudOpenOverATermite`: "the termite under the mud has not been stung still yet", once. Not looked into yet. **Seen again 2026-11-04** ((a), 1 run of `raids,away,soldiers,colony` on 6 servers, during the raid code review's first fixes; these ants have no colony, so the raid changes do not reach them).
78. **Fixed: a dead termite soldier's head floated over a Matabele's back** (the user, 2026-10-25, after `/antcolonies skirmish 10`: several majors had a big brown head with dark jaws high above the thorax, nowhere near a leg; M8 step 2c; branch `claude/floating-heads-bb9829`). Cause: `client/ClampedHeadLayer` moved and turned the pose onto the shin bone itself (`RenderUtil.translateAndRotateMatrixForBone`), but GeckoLib has already done that (`prepMatrixForBone`, parents and all) before it calls a layer for a bone; the shin's turn was applied twice, the second time round the model's middle, so as the leg swung in the walk the head was flung up over the back (filmed: the old code shows exactly the user's picture). Also, a Matabele major's knee is above its back anyway, so even placed right "at the knee" the head looked to float, and it sat on the inner side of the leg. Fix: no second move; the head locked low on the shin (70 percent of the way from the knee to the foot, between the shin's pivot and its child bone's, species-neutral), on the outside of the leg; size unchanged (half the soldier's body width, the same as the living soldier's head). At the user's ask the same day, the head's texture is plain (the soldier's own orange-brown, lighter on top, darker at the front, black jaws; `tools/clamped_head_art.py`, 64 x 32 so the head's faces no longer wrap into stripes). New guard film `q_clamped_head` (`/antcolonies clamp dead <leg>` now takes a leg, 1 to 6, for it). Lesson: in a GeckoLib `renderForBone`, the pose is already on the bone; only move to a point inside it. `CarriedLoadLayer` does the same double move on the hold bone, harmless while the hold bone does not turn.
79. **Fixed 2026-10-25 (branch `claude/m8-bug79-jumping`): jumping on a Matabele mound stopped the defenders** (found by the user in-game 2026-10-25, in-game test 3, branch `claude/m8-termites-2f-6a1046`; M8 step 2e, `fight/NestDefence`). What the user did: jumped again and again on top of the Matabele mound. What happened: the defenders stopped attacking and just stood there. What brings them back: walking on the mound instead of jumping, or another squeak (from the jumping's vibration, a new provocation). Not looked into yet; a guess, not checked: each landing counts as touching the nest anew, so the "stand on the entrance over 3 s" clock or the defenders' order keeps restarting before they sting. **Cause:** the nest looks once a second for players on the entrance, counting only those within 3 blocks of its height; a jumping player was over that line at some checks, which counted as stepping off: the lookers were called off and the 3 s before they may sting began again at the next landing, so they came out, looked and never stung (walking kept the feet down; a squeak came with a new, full provocation, which needs no wait). **Fix:** a player off the entrance for 2 s or less has not left it (`NestDefence.OFF_FOR`, my pick). `MatabeleDefenceTests` now has the player hop 5 blocks up 6 ticks in 13 while on the entrance: with the old rule it fails ("not stung"), with the fix it passes 3 of 3; `soldiers` 15 of 15 on 6 servers. Lesson: a check made now and then must not take one miss as leaving. Not tried in-game yet.
80. **The away termite test's tuft not eaten up again, past the 2,000-tick wait** (found 2026-10-26 by the full run on `master` after merging `claude/rot-looks-23cfbf` and `claude/crowd-research-96c2fb`, 165 of 166 on 6 servers, 11.5 minutes; shard 3). `aTermiteColonyAwayForagesFromTheNightsHolesAndGrowsItsGalleriesOnReturn` failed with "the known tuft by the open hole should be eaten up: cut 3", the same words as 50, which made the check wait up to 2,000 ticks: so either the numbers stop grazing that tuft with one piece left (something stops them, not their pace), or 2,000 ticks is still too short in a full run. Not looked into yet (the user's rule 2026-10-26: bugs found by the tests go on this list, not fixed on the spot); next: run it alone a few times (`ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=atermitecolonyawayforages ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`) and log how many pieces are cut over the wait. The merge touched trees and notes only, nothing in the termites' grazing, so it is likely old. **Seen again 2026-11-04** ("cut 3", the full run after the raid code review's first fixes, 170 of 171 on 6 servers, 13 minutes; the raid changes do not touch termites).
81. **Fixed 2026-11-03 (branch `claude/stoic-shirley-28a1d9`): most new-land termite mounds had no dead tree by them** (found in the user's game log of 2026-11-03 while diagnosing slow savanna loading: 16 of 21 new-land mounds said "no room for a dead tree by the mound", mostly "something in the way ... the last Grass Block"; dead wood for the raids, part 3, `colony/NewLandMounds`). **Cause:** two rules that only hold on dead flat land. (1) The tree's foot is the lowest ground round its trunk (as new land's giant trees), but every block of the tree from the foot up had to be open air, so where the ground beside the trunk rose even one block, the trunk's own base was "in the way": the allowed unevenness of 2 was in truth 0. New land's giant trees simply stand into the higher side (the placer leaves those blocks out). (2) A root running out over lower ground beside the trunk was in the air and counted as "a root in a tunnel or room". Also, a foot more than 4 above or below the mound's ground was refused, common across savanna hills. **Fix:** the trunk's (or a fallen tree's stump's) lowest 4 blocks may stand into the land itself (soil, sand, gravel, rock; never a mound, tree or player's block); roots in the open on the ground over a dip are fine (an exposed root), while roots in a hollow under the ground (a tunnel or room) or hanging 2 or more over a dip are still refused; the ground round the trunk may rise and fall 4 (was 2; the giants take 6) and the foot may be 8 above or below the mound (was 4; the galleries follow the land); a fallen log still lies on land within 3 of its foot and never over the mound's foot. The log's "no room" line now says why each place had no ground (steep, not soil, too far up or down, another tree's place, not loaded). In a test the fence now covers roots too (past a test arena is empty space). Test: `anOldHumpOfNewLandIsGrassyWithTreesAndABurrow` now stands on gently rolling land past 12 blocks from the hump (up and down 1 to 2): the old code fails it ("no dead tree by the mound"), the fix passes; a throwaway check of 12 mounds found a dead tree for 12 of 12 on that land and 9 of 12 on rougher land (up and down about 5, the misses all too steep), in an arena that cuts off most of the ring. Lesson: a rule that "every block must be in open air" quietly means "only on flat land"; check placing rules on rolling ground.
82. **A termite mound stage that cannot be built from inside: "stage 30 of a mound cannot be built from inside: [2, 4, 5 (SPIRE: no termite can get to it to piece)]"** (found in the user's game log 2026-11-03, 17:35:22, while diagnosing slow savanna loading; fixed 2026-11-03, branch `claude/quizzical-hermann-0ed0e4`). The mound's tunnels are worked out by growing its stages in a pretend world by the termites' own building rules (`MoundGrowth.grow`); when that gets stuck it logs this error and the stage is left part-built (the termites in the game then fall back on a longer reach). The unit tests checked 20 seeds, all fine; a sweep of 8,000 random seeds found about 1 mound in 80 stuck (about 100 of 8,000; the five looked at closely got stuck at stages 24 to 32), one of them (seed 6746626102635787809) at exactly the user's cell, though the user's own 16 colonies in the save all grow fine (the stuck one had gone); 4 more had an outside block no termite inside could mend if it were broken. **Two causes.** (1) A new side spire appears late where the dome had soil the stage before; its lowest spire piece is planned in a cell that already holds soil. The game and the pretend world both leave that soil alone (soil is never reshaped into a piece), but the plan still called the cell a spire piece, and a tunnel is only ever carved through cells the plan calls soil, so nothing could join the soil to the spire's channel above and the column was cut off. (2) A lump of two or more soil blocks holding each other up over a one-block air gap (the old hanging-soil rule only dropped single loose blocks), with a spire's top on it: nothing to build it on (seed 4619323497592152298, stage 24; 1 seed in 8,000). **Fix:** (1) when the termites are stuck on a spire piece, or could not mend one, soil standing in the column of planned pieces down from it is kept as soil in the plan from then on (`PretendMound.keepSoilUnder`), so a tunnel can be carved through it; only under such a spire, so every other mound keeps its pieces (keeping it everywhere was tried first: it swapped a spire's bottom piece for soil in 3,674 of the 8,000 mounds, which shows on mounds put down whole in new land). (2) A loose lump gets its one-block gap filled from the soil below, else it goes (`MoundPlan.holdUpLooseLumps`). The 8,000 seeds again: none stuck, none unmendable, and only 93 of them changed at all. Seven of the bad seeds joined `MoundGrowthTest`'s seed list (every mound check runs them), and a new check grows 200 more random seeds side by side (`manyMoreMoundsCanBeGrownFromInside`); `mound` game tests 14 of 14 on 6 servers. Not tried in-game yet. Lesson: a check over a few fixed seeds misses a 1-in-80 late-stage shape; sweep thousands once when a shape rule is in doubt, and keep the ones it finds.
83. **The Matabele "moves into a dead mound" test fails since the scouts (M8 step 3a): a major is out scouting, not down in the rooms** (found 2026-11-03 by the run `raids,soldiers,colony,away` on 6 servers for step 3b, 53 of 54; branch `claude/m8-raid-column-20c0fc`). `aMatabeleColonyMovesIntoADeadTermiteMound` (BUGS 72's check) failed with "workers not down in the rooms ... doing [MatabeleScoutGoal]". Alone it fails 3 of 4 on the 3b code and 4 of 4 on the 3a commit (`324704f`) without 3b, so it comes from 3a, not the column. Likely cause, not checked: a new colony has an empty prey pile, so it is hungry, and the test's still 7:00 is in the raid hours, so its majors go scouting as 3a means them to; the test's rule "every worker settles in the rooms" no longer holds for scouts (box 2: the behaviour changed on purpose, so the test should skip ants out scouting, or stock the prey pile). **Fixed 2026-11-03 (the user's ask, same branch; test only, nothing changes in-game):** the cause was as guessed; the test's "every worker is down in the rooms" now skips ants the colony has out scouting or raiding (`Raids.isScouting`, `isRaider`), real hungry colonies send their scouts out; the check that caught BUGS 72 is kept for every other worker. Alone 6 of 6 (it failed 3 of 4 before).
85. **Fixed 2026-11-03: the raid column walking home stalled on the dead mound's side, so nobody "came home"** (found 2026-11-03 checking the BUGS 83 fix, branch `claude/m8-raid-column-20c0fc`; my own step 3b code, the raid test `aScoutLeadsARaidColumnAlongItsTrailThatStopsShortAttacksTheWorkersAndComesHome` failing about 1 run in 3: "nobody came home with the column"). Every raider stood still 6 to 8 blocks from the entrance, about 4 below it, for the whole 3 minutes home. **Two causes.** (1) The way home ran through the chimney: the scent line's first point lay right over the entrance, and a dead mound's chimney is an open hole, so the ground's height there is the room at its foot, 8 blocks down; the column's places near the end were worked out down in the hump, inside the soil. (2) The column's last place was the chimney's mouth itself, an open hole with no floor, so the front never got there and the column waited for it. **Fix (`RaidColumn`):** points of the scent line right over the entrance are dropped (the route starts at the entrance); a place on the line with no foothold near its height takes the ground's top there instead of a cell inside the soil; a raider is home within 8 blocks of the entrance (any way up), or once inside the nest, and leaves the column there to go in by itself, down whatever hole it likes (my pick: real raiders break up at the nest and each runs in). The raid test 18 of 18 (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`, 3 rounds on 6 servers). Also seen once each in two of those runs before the fix, not since: "a raider stayed at the termites" (an ant out of the column still by the find when the raid was home); its message now says what the ant was doing, in case it comes back. Lesson: a dead mound's chimney fools the height map, so a line drawn on "the ground's height" must not run over a hole; and a place to walk to must be one an insect can stand in.
86. **The termite nurse fed the king "but not head to head": her head 0.96 blocks from his** (found 2026-11-03 by the run `raids,soldiers,colony,away` on 6 servers for M8 step 3c, branch `claude/m8-prey-home-44beec`, 54 of 55; shard 6). `termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder` failed with "the nurse fed the king, but not head to head: its head was 0.9592508406387862 from his". It passed 3 of 3 alone straight after (`ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=termiteworkerseatnoduleswhenyoungandoldcombwhenolder ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`). Step 3c touched only Matabele code (raids, the prey pile, the carry look) and one framework socket for the carry clip, nothing in termite feeding, so it is likely an old rare flake: the nurse feeding a little farther off than the check allows. Not looked into yet (the user's rule: bugs found by the tests go on this list, not fixed on the spot); next: log the nurse's and the king's head positions and how long she stood there, and see whether the check's reach or her stand spot is off.
87. **The raid column froze just out of the dead mound** (the user, in-game test 5, 2026-11-03, branch `claude/m8-prey-home-44beec`; `/antcolonies scout` on a 214-worker colony in a dead mound at about 19:00, at tick rate 2000). The scout found termites under mud sheeting and ran home, the raiders came out ready to raid, then stopped and stood still; the user thought night falling did it. **Read in the code 2026-11-04, not yet proven by a test:** nothing in the raid reacts to night (the scout command lets a raid leave at any hour, so it left after the raid hours and ran into the dark). The likely cause is the march (`RaidColumn.march`): it is the only stage with no time limit; the line starts at the nest's middle, which in a dead mound is the open chimney mouth (BUGS 85's hole, fixed then only at the walk home), so the raiders at the back are sent to the hole and either stand with no way there or drop down the chimney; an ant down the chimney still counts as "at the start of the line" (the line is measured flat), so the front waits for it for ever. Big raids (40 to 50 real ants) hit it; the tests send 10 along a hand-made trail, so they never did. The raid writes nothing to the log, so the log could not show it. To be shown by the whole-chain test (raid rework R2) and fixed by the march rebuilt to follow the trail (R3, `docs/plans/m8-matabele.md`, 3R). Lesson: every stage of a long job needs a time limit, and one stuck member must never hold the rest.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** its cause cannot come back: every stage of the new raid has a deadline, the march out too (a second a block and 30 s, `Raid.marchLimit`; then it turns home), and dusk turns a raid home at any stage (`Raid.turnHome`).

88. **A raider left standing at the termites after its raid was home (test flake, found 2026-11-04 by the raid tests, not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** In `aScoutLeadsARaidColumnAlongItsTrailThatStopsShortAttacksTheWorkersAndComesHome`, 1 run of the `raids` category on 6 servers: the raid ended "all home" with 10 home and 2 termites carried, yet one raider stood within 8 blocks of the termites, whole, not downed, health 10, with no goal running at all ("doing []"). Passed on the run before (3c). Likely the same family as 87: a raider the column counted home (or let go) while it was still out, and nothing then sends it in. To look at in the raid rework, R3 (only ants really out count; an ant with no headway goes home), with the raid diary's `[raid]` lines. **Lesson:** a member let go of a group job must be handed a way home, never left with nothing to do. **Fixed 2026-11-04 (likely; the raid code review, finding 2):** every ant a raid lets go is handed the one way home along the trail (`colony/WayHome`), and every 5 s a sweep sends home any Matabele left out in the field (more than 16 blocks off) with nothing to do, so a raider counted home while still out is walked in.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed, it cannot happen any more. A raider leaves the new raid (`colony/Raid`) in two ways only: `drop` (it is home, dead, or stayed in the nest) or `letGo`, which hands it to `WayHome` at once (kept by another fight over 30 s, hurt and not fetched, still out at the march home's deadline). The raid ends only when its list of members is empty, so nobody can be counted home while out. Every chain scene (`MatabeleRaidChainTests`) checks at its end that nobody stands outside or holds a fight order.

89. **The scout command's scout sometimes never sets out at dusk (found 2026-11-04 by the raid chain test, night trial; not fixed, the rule).** In 1 run of 6 of the whole-chain raid test started at 19:00, `/antcolonies scout`'s ant (`Raids.sendNow`) was picked but never started its scouting trip (no "sets out" line in the raid diary); nothing happened till the colony's own scouts went at 06:00. `Raids.plan` never returns nothing, so most likely another job at the same goal priority (2: feeding the queen or larvae, carrying eggs or remains) held the ant. To look at in the raid rework, R7 (the scout command). **Lesson:** a command that picks an ant must check the ant really took the job, and say so. **Fixed 2026-11-04 (the raid code review, finding 3):** the scout and raid goals are now priority 1, ahead of the nest chores, and the command prefers an ant not busy with a chore; 2 s after the command (and 1 s after a raid starts) the diary says if the ant has not taken its goal, and which goals it runs instead.

87, update 2026-11-04 (R3): one cause found and fixed: a raider checked whether it was at its place by flat distance only, so one down the chimney under its place stood still 8 blocks down; and the march rebuilt so no stuck raider can hold the column (a 15 s no-headway rule, a time limit on the march, the line starting beside the chimney). The freeze itself never showed in the flat test scene; to confirm in-game.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** the fix lives on in the new code: the scout command's check 2 s after it sends an ant is `Scouts.checkSent`, the raid's check 1 s after its start `Raids.checkRaiders`.

91. **Fixed 2026-11-04: a raid's big workers stood round the termites' log for the whole attack and bit nothing open** (found by the new raid test over a ridge, `aScoutsTrailOverARidgeAndRoundAPondLeadsTheRaidThereAndHome`, failing on 5 of 6 servers; branch `claude/m8-prey-home-44beec`, raid review batch 2). The log lay against the foot of a 1-block bank. A major (M8 step 2g) picks a breach (a block of soil beside the termites' gallery) and walks to a spot within reach of it. The spot it was given could be a foothold on the bank's wall right beside the breach: in reach, but on a wall, and a Matabele bites only standing on a floor (`standsToFight`). Its walk was "done", it was "in reach", so each tick it asked for the same walk again, got "already there", and waited for ever, holding its breach; the other majors keep off a breach another holds, so with two such ants no breach was left and the raid went home empty. Fix (`MatabeleEntity.breakIn`): the spot to bite from must be a floor foothold (`Foothold.surface() == DOWN`); and a major whose walk ended where it cannot stand to bite leaves that breach for a while (the same "no way" memory), so it never holds one for ever. The ridge test 6 of 6 after (1 of 6 before). **Lesson:** "in reach" is not "able to act": a walk's goal must be a place the act can happen from (here, a floor), and a goal that can be "reached" without the act ever happening needs a way out, or it holds a shared resource for ever.

93. **Open: the whole-chain raid test from a dead mound flakes about 1 run in 2 (found 2026-11-04 by the run `mound,termitefood,trees,colony,away,raids` on 6 servers for M8 step 3F, branch `claude/m8-feeding-spots-e975b9`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `abigcolonyinadeadmoundraidsfromthescoutsownfindtothepilewithnostagehanging` fails with "no carrier was out on the way home to kill" (most often) or "a raider stands about outside ... 8 from the nest, doing [MatabeleRaidGoal]". It is not 3F's doing: the scene is a hand-made colony in a dead mound with termites from no colony, none of the 3F code runs in it, and on the commit before 3F (3d06f35) it failed 2 of 4 the same way (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`, 4 servers). Likely the R7 faster walk home (ants near the nest walk in, the line breaks up): the carriers may all be home before the test looks for one out on the way, and one may stand by the entrance after its raid ended (like BUGS 88). Lesson to check: the test's moments ("a carrier on the way home", "nobody standing outside") must match the walk home as R7 made it.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** stays open. The "raider stands about outside" half cannot happen any more (88's line). The other half, "no carrier was out on the way home to kill", is the test's own moment: the check is still in `aBigColonyInADeadMoundRaidsFromTheScoutsOwnFindToThePileWithNoStageHanging` and now meets the new march home (`Raid.marchHome`); it passed in every run of the rewrite so far (parts b and c, steps 4 and 5). What would prove it gone: that one test on every server (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`), about 20 runs, none failing so.

94. **Open: the Matabele nest defence test once had no defenders out after the nest was broken (found 2026-11-04 by the full run for M8 step 3F, branch `claude/m8-feeding-spots-e975b9`; not fixed: the rule).** `matabeledefendtheirnestagainstaplayer` failed once, "only 0 defenders out after the nest was broken", in the full run on 6 servers, and passed 6 of 6 alone (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`). The scene uses none of 3F's code (a dead mound put without ants, the colony made by command), so it is a leak between tests or a timing race: box 4 of the 4-box rule. Next time: look at what ran beside it on that server (shard 5) and whether the nest's broken block was put back, or the ants were all out on a raid or a scout, when the check came.

95. **Fixed 2026-11-04: the game crashed when a termite put forage in its garden, "Cannot get property layers ... in air"** (the user's in-game test of the raids, 2026-11-04, `crash-2026-11-04_22.07.21-server.txt`; a termite worker of the new-land colony at -25, 72, 211, about 100 blocks from the raid, so not the raid's doing; branch `claude/m8-raid-fixes-31ac6f`). `FoodStore.depositSeed` put a layer of comb down (`addLayerAt`) and then played the "fresh comb" squelch (`FungusCombBlock.laid`), which reads the comb's height. The comb was gone by then. A comb heap needs a solid floor (`SeedPileBlock.canSurvive`), but the game only checks that when a block next to it changes. A garden cell whose floor had been changed by a block change that tells no neighbour (some building and wearing code does) keeps a comb floating there, or offers its empty cell as a place for forage; laying there set off the block updates, the floorless comb was taken away at once, and the squelch read air. Fix: a store cell counts as a place for forage only if a heap there would stand (`FoodStore.holds`, in `foodSpots`, the store's room and `addLayerAt`); `addLayerAt` checks the heap is really there afterwards before counting it; `laid` shows nothing if the comb is gone. Test: `termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder` ends by taking the floor from under two combs side by side and putting forage on one (the old code put it there; now nothing is put down, nothing counted, no crash). **Lesson:** a block that needs a floor can be left floating by a quiet block change; before building on one, ask whether it would stand (`canSurvive`), and never read a block's properties back after `setBlock` without checking it is still that block.

96. **Fixed 2026-11-04: the raid column moved in jerks, recruits were left home in a dead mound's pit, and attacks ran on with nobody fighting** (the user's in-game test of the raids, 2026-11-04, "column stuck or messy", "fighting looked wrong"; the raid and fight review 2, `docs/reviews/raids-and-fights-review-2.md`, findings 1 to 3; branch `claude/m8-raid-fixes-31ac6f`). (a) On the march each raider kept a gap behind "the ant ahead of it" in the order the raiders were called, never matched to where they were on the way out: an ant ahead on the trail of the ant it followed stood still till that one passed it, so the line moved in jerks and ants overtook ants. Now the line is sorted by where each ant is along the trail at every update, and each ant keeps its own lane. (b) The milling places round the entrance were looked for up to 3 blocks under the entrance; a dead mound's open chimney is a pit, so 5 recruits were sent down it, stood exactly at their places, and were left home as "not out" (the check wanted no more than a block under). Now a milling place is on open ground, at most a block under the entrance, never in the nest; else farther out or the trail's start. (c) The attack ends 30 s after the last termite about the spot, but it counted termites in their own nest and shut in under the logs, which no raider goes for: the attack ran from 18:20 to dusk. Now it counts only termites the fighters would go for (shut-in ones only while a major still breaks in). Test: the whole-chain raid test from a dead mound checks (b) and (c). **Lesson:** two pieces of code that judge the same thing (where an ant should stand vs whether it is out; which termites matter to the fighters vs to the column) must use one rule, or an ant obeying one is punished by the other.

97. **Open: the ten-Matabele skirmish test flakes, a Matabele standing half a minute in a foraging hole (found 2026-11-04 by the run `raids,soldiers,colony,away` on 6 servers for the raid and fight review 2, branch `claude/m8-raid-fixes-31ac6f`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `tenmatabelesatafeedingspottakeminutesandmanytermitesescape` fails with "a Matabele has stood at -4, 11, -12 for half a minute while the fight is on (the hay at -4, 12, -17)": the ant stands a block under the ground, in one of the test's opened foraging holes, 5 blocks from the hay. Alone (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`): 1 of 32 on the code before the branch (22745f5 and 23c5adb), 6 of 27 with the review's raid fixes, though none of the changed code runs in this scene (the ten ants have no colony and no column; nothing reads the clock or new dice). So it is an older fight-goal or legs problem (a fighter in a hole with no way on, `FightGoal`'s search round where it stands), seen more often by chance or by some timing not yet found. Lesson to check: what the ant runs while it stands there (`runningGoals`), and whether it is a minor gone in after the termites (step 2g) that cannot climb back out of the hole.
    - **2026-11-25:** again in the `soldiers` run after the chickens were taken out (branch `claude/flaky-fixes`): "a Matabele has stood ... for half a minute while the fight is on", failed alone 1 of 3. No chicken in its scene.

98. **Open: a Matabele scout test could not put its dead mound in, a neighbouring test's living mound within 40 blocks (found 2026-11-04 by the run `colony,away,raids,termitefood,antnest` on 4 servers for the savanna lag fix, branch `claude/perf-far-colonies`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `aHungryMatabeleColonySendsAScoutWhoFindsTermitesAndComesHomeWithAScentLine` failed at once with "no dead mound put in"; the log said "no termite mound at ...: another colony or dead mound within 40": the mound clue test (`aScoutRingsALivingMoundItComesNearAndFollowsATermiteTunnelOutToTheFeeding`) had just put a living mound 38 blocks away in its own arena. The arenas are 65 apart, but a mound near one arena's edge and another near the next one's can be closer than `NewLandMounds`' 40-block keep-apart (raised with step 3F, mounds 40 to 80 apart). **Lesson:** the keep-apart check reaches across test arenas; a test that puts a new-land mound should not be crowded out by another test's (the fix to look at: tests put their mounds with the keep-apart limited to their own arena, `TestIsolation`, or the two tests in one batch).
    - Failed again 2026-11-06 in the full run for the overnight fixes (3 servers): `ahalffedcolonysscoutspassoverlonetermitesandtakearicherspotfurtherout`, "no dead mound put in". Still open.
   - **Hit by a second test (2026-11-05 and 06, real sizes step 4, branch `claude/real-sizes-step4-072dcf`):** `aHalfFedColonysScoutsPassOverLoneTermitesAndTakeARicherSpotFurtherOut` failed at once with "no dead mound put in", twice (the full run, then the run `raids,soldiers,colony,away,legs`), the log the same "another colony or dead mound within 40". Any test that puts a new-land mound can be crowded out this way; the fix named above (the keep-apart limited to a test's own arena) would cover them all.

99. **Fixed 2026-11-05: a big lag spike when Matabeles raid a termite feeding spot, lingering after the raid (the user's report with `/antcolonies perf`, branch `claude/m8-away-raids-df9e17`).** The stopwatch said it all: 10 termite minor soldiers cost 73 ms a tick, 64 route searches a second, all failed, each the full 6,000 steps, every one asked by `DefendGoal`. Two faults stacked. (a) A minor sent at a Matabele it had no way to (the raider behind a seal, on a runway's top, up a wall, in its own nest) tried again at once: a failed search leaves the walk done, and "done" meant "plan again" the next tick; after 5 failures the goal stopped, and 5 ticks later it started again with the count at 0. So each soldier ran about 6 hopeless searches a second. (b) After the raid the alarm stays at "enemy at the foragers" for 2 minutes after its last raise (longer after a break-in) and sent the minors at the raider anywhere in the colony's ground, so they chased the Matabeles back to their own nest, where no way leads, for minutes. Fix: a soldier that finds no way to an enemy or a spot leaves it alone for 10 s and goes back to its post (`DefendGoal`, the shared `Errand` memory); and soldiers go at an enemy only while it is within 16 blocks of where the trouble was (`Alarm.CHASE_LEASH`, the same reach the Matabele defenders give up at). **Lesson:** a goal that retries a failed route search must wait before the next one; "the walk is done" is also true when the search found nothing.

100. **Fixed 2026-11-05 (test only): the Matabele nest defence test flaked, no defenders out after the nest was broken (found by the run `soldiers,raids` on 6 servers for the raid lag fix, BUGS 99, branch `claude/m8-away-raids-df9e17`).** `matabeledefendtheirnestagainstaplayer` failed about 1 run in 3 with "only 0 defenders out after the nest was broken", on the code before BUGS 99 too. The failure was made to explain itself (`NestDefence.explainForTests`: each real member and why it may or may not defend, the wave timer, the provocations): 9 of 10 ants could defend, but the colony's state was the same before and after the break, so the break was never a provocation. The test broke "the first solid block in a box 2 round the entrance", starting at a corner: when the worn dead mound had soil there, that block is 3.5 blocks from the entrance, past the nest's 3-block radius (`defence.nest_radius`), and the ants rightly ignored it; when the corner was air, a nearer block was picked and the test passed. (The hit before it, on a major 15 blocks off, never calls a wave, as designed.) Fix: the test breaks the nearest solid block the game counts as the nest (`NestDefence.isNest`). Nothing changes in-game. Alone 24 of 24; `soldiers,raids` 26 of 26. **Lesson:** a test that picks "a block of X" must ask the game's own rule for X, not guess it with a box.

101. **Fixed 2026-11-05: raiders milled on top of a dead mound, walking back and forth, until the scout set off, and ants still in the nest were left home (the user's report with a picture, in-game, branch `claude/m8-raid-bugs`).** The forming waited until every recruit counted as "out", up to its 30 s limit. In a tall dead mound the ants coming up from the rooms or the chimney did not count as out (the user's log: ants dropped at 5 and 7 blocks under the entrance, "0.0 off" their places), so the column milled for the whole limit and then left without them; the scout itself only walked among the millers, never into the nest. Meanwhile each ant's milling place was worked out from its number in the raiders' list, which shifted whenever an ant dropped out or went out of sight, so ants were sent to new places again and again: the back and forth. Fix: the scout runs into the nest to recruit (real Megaponera), the raiders pour out, the column leaves once all are out or most are and nobody new comes, latecomers follow it, and each ant keeps one place (numbered once, at the start). Test in `raids`. **Lesson:** a place handed out by a position in a list must be keyed by something that does not move (the ant itself), or every change to the list moves everyone.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** the fix lives on as the new muster's rule (`Raid.muster`): the bunch by the entrance is only as big as the level ground there, and an ant with no place waits in the nest and pours out behind the column as it leaves.

102. **Fixed 2026-11-05: Matabele sealed in by termite workers stood stuck for ever (the user's report with a picture, in-game, branch `claude/m8-raid-bugs`).** Termites seal a broken gallery behind them in a raid (`RaidDefence`), plug foraging holes and wall up leaks (`NestWalls`); the checks that wait while a nestmate would be shut in look only at their own colony's insects, so a raider in the gallery or pocket was walled in. The termites' plug is mound soil, which the Matabele may never bite (it keeps them off the mound), so the trapped ant had no way out, its route searches failing every second. Fix: a trapped Matabele bites its way out through soil, termite mud and fresh plugs (never the mound), the straight way with fewest blocks (`MatabeleBiteOutGoal`). The termites still seal over a foe, as real ones do. Test in `soldiers`. A first try dug a cave round the ant (each pick the nearest block, in any direction): a minor took 48 s or never got out; judging the whole straight way out fixed it. **Lesson:** a rule that keeps a body off something ("never the mound") needs a way out for when the world closes round it; and an escape must keep one heading, not pick afresh each block.

103. **Open: a raider left standing at the termites after the raid went home (found 2026-11-05 by the run `raids,away` on 6 servers while building M8 step 3S, branch `claude/m8-soldiers-fight`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `aScoutLeadsARaidColumnAlongItsTrailThatStopsShortAttacksTheWorkersAndComesHome` failed once with "a raider stayed at the termites ... fights no more false downed false health 10.0 doing []; the raid all home": a major at full health, no goal running, no fight spot, left within 8 blocks of the find after the column ended. Alone on every server (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`): 6 of 6 passed, so about 1 run in 7 at most. Like BUGS 88 (a raider left at the termites, fixed by `WayHome` for ants let go); this one was not let go and not in the column at the end, so it never got a way home. 3S changed who fights whom at the spot (majors now take on soldiers, soldiers bite back), which may make it more likely; not known. Lesson to check: whether the ending column counts every raider it ever held, and what happens to one that was off fighting a soldier (a fight spot cleared) the moment the raid ended.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed, it cannot happen any more. The case here (a raider off fighting a soldier at the moment the raid ended, never let go) has no way to arise: the attack's end ends every fight order of the raid at once (`Raid.endFights`), a raider held by another fight is kept 30 s and then let go with a way home (`Raid.rollCall`), and the raid ends only with its members list empty (see 88's line).

106. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (test): the ten-Matabele skirmish test now flakes about 1 run in 3, an ant standing on the open ground with a fight order and no goal running (found 2026-11-05 by the run `colony,raids,away,soldiers` on 6 servers while building M8 step 3Q, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `tenmatabelesatafeedingspottakeminutesandmanytermitesescape` fails with "a Matabele has stood at ... for half a minute while the fight is on": unlike BUGS 97 (an ant in a foraging hole), this ant stands on the surface 4 to 10 blocks from the hay, its fight spot set, idle, running no goal at all (the failure message now says what it runs, its activity, whether it fights no more, how badly wounded, its health). On the code before 3Q (a9a90e0, after 3S) alone on every server: 4 of 12 failed; with 3Q: 7 of 24, the same. So it came before 3Q, likely with 3S: `FightGoal` starts only while the ant still fights (`fightsNoMore`), and 3S made a Matabele at 40 percent health or less "badly wounded", fighting no more and going home; the skirmish's ants have no colony and no home, so such an ant keeps its fight order and stands, not known for sure (the run with the new message passed 6 of 6). 3Q found one cause of its own and fixed it before this entry: a Matabele with a fight order napped between bouts of its fight (`NapGoal`; now no nap, cleaning or grooming a nestmate with a fight order, and resting first is off while one is held). Lesson to check: what a badly wounded ant with no home does, and whether the test should count an ant that fights no more. **Seen again 2026-11-05** (run `raids,soldiers,away,colony,replays` on 4 servers, raid test bench round 3) with the new message: the ant stood at -4, 12, -13, "fights no more true (badly wounded true, health 2.0)", running no goal: the guess above holds, a badly wounded skirmisher with no colony has no home to go to. **Fix (2026-11-06, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`, box 3, the test too strict):** the guess held: a badly wounded skirmisher with no colony has no nest to limp to, and standing is right (a real hurt Megaponera waits to be carried home, step 5); the camping check is for ants that should fight, so it now skips one that fights no more.
   - **Rate on real sizes step 2 (2026-11-05, branch `claude/real-sizes-step2-2501ab`):** 7 of 19 failed alone on every server (6 with the badly wounded ant standing, health 2, as here; 1, in the full run, a climbing ant at a hole, BUGS 97), about 1 in 3 as before, so the new body reaches did not change it. Still open.
   - **Again 2026-11-06 (real sizes step 4, branch `claude/real-sizes-step4-072dcf`):** once in the run `raids,soldiers,colony,away,legs` on 6 servers, a badly wounded ant (fights no more, health 2) standing half a minute 8 blocks from the hay.

104. **Fixed 2026-11-05: dead termite mounds of new land with no Matabele colony (the user's report, in-game, branch `claude/matabele-newland-fix-29a340` from `claude/m8-soldiers-fight`).** In a new world the user looked at about 12 dead mounds in a savanna and none held a colony; the log said 29 dead mounds, 4 colonies, 12 turned away with "no living termite mound within 70 blocks" (the rule from step 3F, F4: the Matabele settle a dead mound only with termites in raiding reach), the nearest living mounds 82 to 181 blocks off. The rule and the world making agreed; the guess under them was wrong: 3F planned "about 2 in 3 places hold a mound" on flat ground (`tools/raid_balance.py`'s stand-in), but Minecraft's savanna is rolling and wet, and only about 1 place in 5 held one (in the user's world 92 of 427 savanna places: the rest on slopes steeper than 4 blocks up and down within 8, by water, or past the biome's edge). So mounds stood about 100 blocks apart, not 40 to 80, and most dead mounds had no living one in reach. Found with a new survey game test that makes real overworld land from the noise for 5 seeds (no chunks) and asks the pattern; tried and dropped: mounds shifting off their place's middle to dry level ground (4 times the cost, +7 percent mounds), a softer water rule (no change), a longer reach of 90 (15 of 24 turned away to 11). Fix: (1) a mound takes slopes up to 6 blocks up and down (was 4; the mound builds on a 6-block hillside in `hills`): 113 of 366 savanna places hold one, was 77; (2) a dead mound whose dice give it Matabele lives instead when no mound living by the dice stands within 70 blocks (`TermiteMoundStructure.pickAt`, `MoundPlaces.livingByDiceNear`; real: Matabele nests sit among the termites they raid, dead mounds among living ones), so none is turned away; (3) the dead dice 0.33 to 0.5, so about a quarter of mounds still end dead (27 percent in the survey, was 34). The survey: 30 dead mounds, 11 with Matabele (was 9 of 38), 0 turned away, no two mounds nearer than 40. Dead mounds left empty are the third the dice give no ants, anywhere. **Lesson:** a balance number guessed for flat test ground ("2 in 3 places hold a mound") must be measured on Minecraft's real land before other rules lean on it; a survey over the real noise, with no chunks, takes seconds.

108. **Open: the ridge-and-pond raid test's scent line once ran straight across the pond's edge (found 2026-11-05 by the run `colony,mound,raids,away` on 6 servers after merging BUGS 104 into `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `aScoutsTrailOverARidgeAndRoundAPondLeadsTheRaidThereAndHome` failed once with "the scent line does not bend round the pond: at most 2.9 blocks off the straight way". Alone on every server (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`) 6 of 6 passed, so it shows only beside other tests (box 4, a leak), like BUGS 98, which hit the half-fed scout test in the same run ("no dead mound put in": a neighbour's mound within 40 blocks). Lesson to check: what else in the arena (a neighbour's colony, its galleries or its trail) can change the scout's way home so it skirts the pond more tightly than the test expects.

111. **Open: a raid's attackers and the termite soldiers stuck together in a pit, cut off from the colony, the attack never ending (the user's report with a picture, in-game, 2026-11-05, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`; not fixed yet: the user asked to plan a raid test bench first, `docs/plans/raid-test-bench.md`, and fix this through it).** The picture: Matabele hanging on the roof of an open pit dug down to a gallery, termite soldiers (orange heads) on a ledge and the floor below. The diary (`run/logs/latest.log`): the raid from the nest at 363, 75, 376 went forming, marching, gathering, then "on to attacking" at 10:21 (tick 4364) with 49 real raiders, and then nothing for the 3 real minutes until the user quit, with time sped up. The attack should end 30 s after the last termite (`quiet_ticks` 600) and at the latest after `fight_ticks` plus 10 s (2,600 ticks), and `attackedAt` is set only once, so the cap alone should have ended it: the raid's update may have stopped running altogether, not only waited. Likely mix of features: majors bite a pit into the gallery (2g), the termites seal behind the break (2f), the soldiers wait by the seal (2f, 3S), and termite soldiers "about" the spot keep the quiet clock from running. To check through the bench: rules stage-limit, heartbeat, not-walled-in, soldiers-go-back.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed, it cannot happen any more. The attack has one rule and one deadline (`Raid.attack`): it ends 15 s after the last termite stung or gone for, and never later than `raids.column.fight_ticks` (2 minutes); termite soldiers standing about no longer keep it going (only work by the raiders counts, told by the fight itself, `Raid.fightNote`). At its end every fight order of the raid ends at once, and a raider more than `leash` (14) blocks from the spot is called back. The raid is worked out once a second by the colony whether or not a raider is loaded (`Raids.stepRaid`), and an update that fails gives the raid up and sends everyone home (`Raid.abandon`). Ants walled into a pit are the bite-out's job as before (`MatabeleBiteOutGoal`, BUGS 102, 113). Every chain scene checks that no stage runs past its deadline and nobody fights 3 s after the attack.

112. **Fixed 2026-11-05: termites in their nest stood still for half a minute and more with a route to walk (found 2026-11-05 by the raid test bench's not-stuck rule in the user's in-game raid test, new world, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`).** 22 not-stuck breaches in about 15 minutes, every one inside a termite nest of new land, never out in the open: colony 2f158835 (royal chamber round the queen at 789, 63, -645: 1 to 9 at a time, workers on `FeedQueenGoal`, `StandByQueenGoal`, `AllogroomGoal` and minor soldiers on `GuardPostGoal` heading for posts 20 to 25 blocks off, standing on the same spot from the world's load for 30 s and more), colony 1630ce19 (workers on `FeedQueenGoal` and `FeedLarvaGoal` 4 blocks from where they stand, still 30 to 73 s) and colony 16d3c3b8 (a minor soldier to its post). Reports `run/reports/2026-11-05_14-36-14-not-stuck`, `14-36-19`, `14-36-59`, `14-47-20` (diaries and the place saved). Not yet known: a crowded royal chamber where nobody gives way (no pushing), a route through a doorway the new-land mound's shape closed, or the route search failing quietly and the old route kept. Next: load a report's `place.nbt` into a test and watch one walker. **The cause (read in the reports' saved places, 2026-11-05):** every stuck termite stood inside fungus comb (3 layers, 6 pixels high) in a one-block-high garden room right under the grass. When the colony's numbers come out as real mobs (on load and whenever the real ones are topped up), `Population.comeOut` put each one at the bottom of its cell, so in a comb cell it stood inside the comb and could not walk out (the same trap as BUGS 48, from another door). **Fix:** the numbers now come out on top of a low floor block filling the cell (comb, a heap; `NestWalls.lowFloorTop`: one full-width box from the floor up to under a block, never ramps or stairs), and once a second any insect found sunk in one is lifted onto it (`AbstractAntEntity.liftIfSunk`, the safety net for anything else that puts one there). Test: the comb check in `termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder`. **Lesson:** every place that puts an insect into the world puts it on top of what fills the cell, not at the cell's floor.

113. **Fixed 2026-11-05: a Matabele that counted itself shut in but found nothing it may bite stood still for ever (found 2026-11-05 by the raid test bench's raider-stuck rule in the user's in-game raid test, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`).** A scout that gave up on its way home (1c048e6f, at 781, 65, -676, about 95 blocks from its nest) was under cover and shut in (`Fights.isShutIn`) away from its own rooms, so `MatabeleBiteOutGoal` took it; every block round its pocket was one it may not bite (wood, stone or the mound), so `pick` found nothing and the goal waited 'nothing to bite' over and over, holding the ant: still 60 s and more, its walk home never resumed. A wait without a limit (the test bench's rule, `docs/plans/raid-test-bench.md`). Likely fix: the goal lets go when it finds nothing to bite and does not take the ant again for a while, so the walk home (or a climb out) can try. Report `run/reports/2026-11-05_14-45-35-raider-stuck`. **The cause (read in the report's saved place):** the scout stood on open grass under a camel thorn's crown (leaves 18 blocks up), not in a pocket at all. Our tree leaves are solid blocks, not Minecraft's leaves, so the game's height map without leaves still counted the crown as the ground's top, and `Fights.isInTheOpen` said 'under cover'; the shut-in search then ran out of room and said 'shut in'. **Fix, both ways:** `isInTheOpen` looks down from that top and lets leaves (the `minecraft:leaves` tag, which ours are in) pass, so only wood, soil, stone or water count as a roof; and `MatabeleBiteOutGoal` lets go for 30 s when it finds nothing it may bite (my pick), so no ant is ever held by it for ever. Test: the open ground under a crown in `aMatabeleShutInUnderTheGroundBitesItsWayOutButNeverThroughTheMound`. Found while at it: BUGS 116.

114. **Open: the side-by-side test run hangs after every server has finished, one server stuck saving its world as it shuts down (found 2026-11-05, twice in a row, by `tools/run_game_tests.sh` on 6 and on 4 servers, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** Every server printed its "required tests failed/passed" line, then one (shard 2 the second time) logged "Saving worlds" and never "Game test server shutting down"; its java process stayed alive and Gradle, so the script, waited for ever (the user asked "why is it still going"). Killing the stuck java process lets the results be read by hand from `run-test-shards/<i>/logs/latest.log`. Not yet known: a colony or the test bench's background report writer (`watch/Reports`' pool, new in round 1) holding the save, or Minecraft's own chunk saving. Next: a thread dump of the stuck server (`jstack`), and a time limit in the script that reads the logs once every server has printed its result line.
   - **The thread dump (2026-11-05, branch `claude/real-sizes-step2-2501ab`, shard 6 of `raids,soldiers,termitefood,colony,away`, stuck 30 minutes):** not saving at all: the server thread was busy (32 minutes of processor time) in Minecraft's own shutdown, `MinecraftServer.stopServer` calling `ServerChunkCache.tick`, `ChunkMap.processUnloads` and `scheduleUnload` over and over, so chunks are being unloaded and something keeps giving them back (a chunk held or loaded again while the world shuts down: likely our code asking for a chunk from an unload or save event, or a ticket left by the tests' ground round the arenas, `TestIsolation.GROUND_ROUND`). Not the report writer. Still open.
   - **Fixed 2026-11-05 (branch `claude/real-sizes-step3-0f59d8`; the user: "we gotta fix this test its wasted me so much time"):** six more thread dumps of a stuck shard showed no mod code at all: Minecraft's own bug. On the way out `stopServer` unloads chunks with no time limit; a chunk still lent to a neighbour's generation (its generation count above 0) is put straight back on the unload queue, and the same loop takes it off again, for ever, on the server thread; the neighbour's generation needs that thread for its last steps (the full chunk), so the count never drops. Our tests load wide ground round every arena, so a chunk is often half made when the last test ends (about 1 run in 3 on 6 servers). The fix, `ShutdownChunks` (framework): as the server stops, with no player on, the chunk work runs in 10 ms slices with the server thread free between them until nothing is left (at most 60 s), so `stopServer` finds nothing to do; a test server is also stopped hard 3 minutes after its results, its result as the exit code, so a run can never wait for ever again. First runs: every server settled in about 1 s (6 of 6, then the category run). **Lesson:** a hang with only Minecraft on the stack is still worth a dump and a read of the loop's code: here the loop could never end once the thread it spins on was the one the work waited for.
   - **Held across whole runs (2026-11-05 and 06, real sizes step 4, branch `claude/real-sizes-step4-072dcf`):** the full run of 187 tests on 6 servers and 13 smaller runs after it: every server printed "Game test server shutting down" 1 to 4 s after its result line, none hung; "Chunks settled on the way out" (about 1 s, 40 to 55 rounds) where chunk work was left, as in 3 of the full run's 6 servers.

119. **Open: the termite eating test's new comb check flakes about 1 run in 4, a worker left sunk in the comb (found 2026-11-05 by the run `replays,raids,away,colony` on 4 servers while building the raid test bench round 2, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`; then 1 of 4 on every server alone; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `termiteworkerseatnoduleswhenyoungandoldcombwhenolder` fails with "a worker sunk in the comb was not lifted onto it: -51.0, its top at -50.5". The check came with BUGS 112's fix (`AbstractAntEntity.liftIfSunk`, run once a second on the ant's own staggered tick). Likely the check looks before the worker's own second comes round, or the comb grew under it after its lift; not looked into yet. Lesson to come.

120. **Fixed 2026-11-05: the raid test bench's rules cried "stuck" for insects that could not have moved (found by the first soak run, `tools/soak.sh 1 1`, while building the bench's round 2, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`).** Two false alarms. (a) The rules skip a colony while it is away, but kept each insect's "still since" time: when the land came back (the soak's unload poke, or a player walking away and back), an ant put back where it stood counted the whole time away as standing still, and `raider-stuck` broke 2 seconds after the land loaded. Now a colony not checked for over 5 seconds starts its stillness and its "lasting" counts afresh (`Watch.check`, `Watch.GAP`). (b) An ant out at the edge of the loaded land, where entities do not tick, stands frozen; `not-stuck` and `home-by-night` called it stuck. Now the rules only look at insects whose land runs (`Watch.insectsOf`, `isPositionEntityTicking`). The soak also kept only a patch round the place it searched loaded, so a scout walked out of it; now it keeps 8 chunks round every watched colony (`Soak.ROUND`). Lesson: a watcher must know when it was not watching, and where the world was not running, or it reports its own blind spots as bugs.
