121. **Fixed 2026-11-05: a raid scout that gives up on its way home stands still for hours (found by the first soak run 2026-11-05, `tools/soak.sh 2 2`, seed 8675309).** Matabele major fac2be53, "a scout that gave up on its way home", stood still at 2065, 81, 2030 from 21:02 to past 04:45 (raider-stuck twice, home-by-night); four more Matabele out after 22:00 not walking home the same two nights (home-by-night 5 times in all), and one termite worker with a route standing 30 s (not-stuck, may be the old flicker). The scouts were sent by the soak's "raid at any hour" poke. Reports in `run-soak/1/reports/` (raider-stuck 17-42-38, 17-43-34, 17-45-16; home-by-night 17-42-43, 17-45-11); make one into a test with `python tools/report_to_test.py <report> --known-bug`. Not fixed (test bugs go to the list). Also from that run: server 2's seed (20261105) found no Matabele colony with termites near in 6 places; the soak now tries 12. **The cause (read in the report's saved place):** the scout stood on a narrow ledge on the lip of a ravine about 20 blocks deep, its nest 10 blocks lower across it. An ant going home alone hops 14 blocks at a time toward home, and the hop's landing height was guessed from the way's two ends (here about 77) and looked for only 3 up or down from it: straight ahead lay the ravine (no ground), and every other way the hillside at 80 to 85 (no ground found 3 from 77). So every hop failed, every 20 s, all night. **Fix (`MatabeleRaidGoal.hopToward`):** a hop's landing is also looked for at the ant's own height and 6 over and under it, and an ant going home alone that cannot hop straight tries a half hop, then hops turned 45 and 90 degrees to each side (the way round the ravine), waiting 100 s between rounds of tries once all fail; behind it all, the new stray net (`colony/Strays`, B7) folds an ant stuck 3 minutes out of sight into the numbers. Test: `replay_bug121_scout_home` (the report replayed, 3 runs, counting only the Matabele's rules: the termites' own BUGS 122 shows there too), 3 of 3. **Lesson:** a landing guessed from the start and the goal fails on the slope between them; guess from where the walker stands, and when straight ahead fails, try round.

122. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (with BUGS 148: its replay passes 3 of 3, now required): a termite worker on a living mound stood 49 s with a route to a building spot (found 2026-11-05 by the BUGS 121 replay, `replay_bug121_scout_home`, run 3 of 3, while building the raid test bench round 3, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** Termite worker 7a42e5bf of colony c5f0408f stood at 2065, 91, 2061 for 11 to 20 s at a time, then took `BuildMoundGoal` to 2040, 107, 2058 (25 blocks across and 16 up, the mound's upper wall) and stood there walking it for 49 s: the not-stuck rule broke. Report `run-test-shards/1/reports/2026-11-05_18-10-24-not-stuck` (diary and the place; make it a test with `python tools/report_to_test.py <report> --species macrotermes --known-bug`). Not the scout's bug: the replay now counts only the Matabele's rules (`replay.json` `"species"`, new). Lesson to check: whether the route to a high mound job is planned and then never walked (a step it cannot take), or the job far out of reach, as with T4's skipped mound jobs (`AntNavigator.moveTo` with a reach); the general stray net (`colony/Strays`) would fold it in only after 3 minutes, and only out of the nest.

123. **Fixed 2026-11-06: Matabele scouts out at night break the home-by-night rule (found 2026-11-05 by the soak `tools/soak.sh 2 2`, seed 8675309, after the raid test bench round 3, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** Four breaches in two nights at 22:13 to 22:26, every one a major on `MatabeleScoutGoal`: 06b3eab5 still searching ("scouting", walking on its winding path) at 22:13; 784e468b had just come to the end of its trip time ("searched 200 s and found nothing worth a raid") and was heading home straight, and 6cb290a0 with it. Two things to sort out: (a) whether a scout should still be out searching after 19:30 at all (scouts search 6:00 to 19:30; the soak's "raid at any hour" poke may send them at night, `colony/RaidSoak`, which the rule does not know), and (b) the rule counts only ants "going home" along a trail as walking home, not a scout running home straight at the end of its trip. Reports `run-soak/1/reports/2026-11-05_18-28-04-home-by-night`, `18-31-04-home-by-night`. Also from that soak: server 2's seed (20261105) again found no Matabele colony with termites near in 12 places (the soak gives up on that server); and two not-stuck breaches, termite workers by their mound (BUGS 122). **Fix (2026-11-06, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`):** every one of the four was a scout the soak's "raid" poke sent "anytime" (`Raids.sendNow(level, true)`, logged as "the scout command sends"), and such a scout keeps no hours by design; the rule now excuses a scout sent anytime (`Raids.wasSentAnyHour`). Found with it: any scout the command sent, even one meant to keep the raid hours, searched on past 19:30 (one was out till 02:52); now only an "anytime" scout does, the plain command's scout goes home at 19:30 with the others (`MatabeleScoutGoal.searchOn`). **Lesson:** a rule must know every way the game is told to break its own hours (the soak's pokes too).

124. **Fixed: a small termite put inside the fungus comb was never lifted out (found 2026-11-05 by `termiteworkerseatnoduleswhenyoungandoldcombwhenolder`, 3 runs in 4, while building real sizes step 1, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`).** At its real size (a box 0.13 wide) a termite worker set at the bottom of a comb's cell read to its legs as holding on to the comb, and the BUGS 112 safety net (`AbstractAntEntity.liftIfSunk`) skipped every insect holding on to a wall, so it stood in the comb for ever. Fix: the net lifts any insect whose middle is in a low block's own cell below its top, holding on or not (a real climber of the block's side has its middle in the cell beside). **Lesson:** a size change moves which cell a body's middle is in and what its legs think they touch; a safety net must test the body's place, not the legs' state.

126. **Open: the Matabele break-in test's stop count runs close to its limit (found 2026-11-05 by the run `legs,raids,soldiers,termitefood,colony,away` on 6 servers, real sizes step 3, branch `claude/real-sizes-step3-0f59d8`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `matabelemajorsbreakintoafeedinggalleryunderalog` counted 251 stops against a limit of 240 once in that run. Alone, 18 runs on 6 servers all passed with 160 to 231 stops (before step 3, 164 to 207 in three runs). Waits for the crowd are not counted (40 to 130 a run); shuffles aside are only 1 to 7 a run, so they are not the cause. The count seems to rise on servers busy with other tests. To look at: what the remaining stops are (the end of a search step, a turn), and whether the limit of one stop every 5 s each is the rule the test means.
    - Failed again 2026-11-06 in the full run for the overnight fixes (3 servers, 185 of 188): 276 stops against 240. Still open; the overnight change that stuck insects plan again after standing too long (`AntNavigator`, BUGS 141) may add stops, to look at with the next run alone.
   - **Passed in the full run (2026-11-05, real sizes step 4, branch `claude/real-sizes-step4-072dcf`)** and in the run `raids,soldiers,colony,away,legs`; still open.

127. **Fixed: raiders stood inside one another round a tall dead mound's chimney (found 2026-11-05 by the full run for real sizes step 4, branch `claude/real-sizes-step4-072dcf`; caused by step 3, so fixed in step 4).** `araidfromatalldeadmoundsetsoutpromptlywitheveryrecruitthatcameout` failed its personal-space check (two raiders inside one another 20 ticks or more in a row): alone, 9 runs in 15 failed, the worst pairs 23 to 115 ticks, majors standing 0.1 to 0.3 apart on the hump while the column formed. Two causes. (a) The milling places (`RaidColumn.formSpot`) were handed out by block cells with no count of who already had one, so on a narrow hump top 4 to 7 majors were given the same cell; the legs' free spot search then found no free spot ("no free spot", every spot near taken or with no ground) and they stood stacked. (b) On the march, a late raider whose next place on the trail was the cell the one ahead stood in walked into it and stood there. Fix: (a) a cell takes milling places for at most one body length of ants (`FORM_CELL_LENGTHS` 1.0, my pick: a block holds two majors side by side), looked for out to 6 blocks round the place in finer steps, and each ant keeps the place it was given; (b) a marcher with less than 0.3 blocks of trail ahead of it to the one in front (`WAIT_ROOM`, my pick, half the column's 0.6 spacing) and nothing left to walk stands where it is until there is room. And the check itself: two insects walking past each other squeeze by (the design's "stepping round"), so a pair with one walking may now overlap up to 40 ticks; two standing still keep the old 20 (`CrowdWatch`, box 3). After: 36 of 36 alone (this test and the calm-nest one, 18 each), the worst standing pair 14 ticks, the worst passing pair 33. **Lesson:** at real sizes, places handed out by block cells must count the bodies already given each cell; a cell is no longer one ant's room.

129. **Termites floated up and down inside mud casts and spires** (2026-11-06, the user in-game, after real sizes; branch `claude/real-sizes-step4-072dcf`). Eating a dead tree from below, a worker that took the wood over it went up the mud cast's tube and then "kind of floated back down". The legs put a body going up or down a channel (a termite tunnel's 6-pixel tube: casts, tunnels, hollow spire pieces) on the channel's middle line, holding a wall in name only (`AntNavigator.channelPoint`); before real sizes a worker filled most of the tube, so it looked pressed to the sides, and the drawing only nudged it 0.08 toward the wall (`AntRenderer.CHANNEL_INTO_WALL`). At 0.15 blocks long and about 0.05 wide it hung in the air in the middle. And at the top it swapped to the opposite wall to come down, crossing the tube. **Fix:** going up or down a channel the body holds one of its side walls, pressed against it (`Channel.HALF_WIDTH`, 3/16, less half its width and a 0.01 gap); one already on a wall keeps to it and turns round on it at the top to come back down (the user's idea: crawl up the wall to the wood, then crawl down it); out of the top it goes over the rim of the wall it held; the drawing uses the normal wall pose; "is it in the channel" now counts a body on a channel wall (still telling it apart from one hugging the outside of the narrowest spire piece). Lying along a level tunnel is unchanged. The spire climbing test checks the body is off the middle line all the way up and down (switched off once: it fails, 0.0 from the middle). **Lesson:** anything placed "in the middle" because a body used to fill the space must be looked at again after real sizes.

130. **A raider could wait for ever beside a stung termite a step up or down** (2026-11-06, found reading the real-size code; branch `claude/real-sizes-step4-072dcf`). Real sizes step 2 made a raider step the last little bit to its prey before picking it up (`MatabeleRaidGoal.atPrey`), but a prey more than half a block up (on a step of comb, a ridge) or 1.5 down read as "not beside it", and with its walk over the raider stood there; before, it took any prey within 1.6 blocks. **Fix:** with its walk over and the prey within reach it takes it from where it stands, as before (as `Errand.at` already did). **Lesson:** every "step the last little bit" needs the old reach as its way out, for every answer the step can give.

131. **Termites looked round from deep inside a chimney and came down its outside floating off the face** (2026-11-06, found by the real-size sweep). The way out of a chimney mouth (`DoorwayMotion`) was a list of points made for a termite worker before real sizes, about 0.44 blocks long: how deep it peeks from, how far its drawn body is pushed into a wall and lifted, the grip on the rim. At real size (game scale 0.42) only the tips of its antennae came out for the look round, and on the spire's outside the hitbox stood where the old, wider push put the feet on the face, so the small body hung about 0.09 off it. **Fix:** every length of the body in the motion scales with the insect's game scale (`comeOut`/`goIn` take a size; at size 1 the motion is the approved one, checked); the spire's own lengths do not. New unit check for a real-size worker (neck just over the rim, feet on the face). **Lesson:** a motion tuned by hand for one body must name its body's lengths, so a size change finds them.

132. **Termites nibbled food from half a block away** (2026-11-06, found by the real-size sweep). A forager's walk to a plant or dead wood ends in any cell beside it (`ClipGrassGoal`, the 26 round it), which a big body filled; at real size a worker stood in the next cell's middle and the bits flew off the wood half a block ahead of it. **Fix:** a sized body steps the last little bit to the plant's nearest face before it nibbles (`atPlant`); getting no nearer (a gallery under the wood, a step), it nibbles from where it stands, as before. The harvester ant (no size yet) is unchanged.

133. **Eggs and bodies were put down half a block ahead of the termite carrying them** (2026-11-06, found by the real-size sweep). A carrier put its egg or body down in the middle of its spot's cell once it was within 0.7 of it (`CarryEggGoal`, `CarryCorpseGoal`); at real size that is nearly five body lengths. **Fix:** a sized carrier steps up until its head is over the spot (`Errand.atSpot`), then puts it down; getting no nearer, it puts it down from there, as before.

134. **Fixed 2026-11-06: a new-land dead camel thorn's worked-out death day moved it from mud-climbed to shed** (2026-11-06, found by the full run on 6 servers, test `adeadbaobabslumpsintoaheapandisgone`; nothing to do with real sizes). The test puts a new-land snag in the tree book at the MUD stage with its death day not worked out yet (`Forest.DIED_UNKNOWN`) and checks one `forest.update` keeps it at MUD; the run got "the new land snag's death day -6 moved it to shed". So the death day picked for a new-land snag can be too recent for the stage it already shows, and the update then winds it back to an earlier dead stage. Not fixed yet (test failures go on the list); the next step is to rerun the test alone to see how often it happens, then make the picked death day fit the stage shown. **Fix (2026-11-06, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`):** the cause: `TreeLife.daysDeadIn` picked whole days up to the stage's last (`ceil(end) - 1`), and the stage is then read from the days dead **plus the part of today gone by** (`Forest.update`, `max(today, time)`), so a roll on the last day in the afternoon passed the stage's end; now the last day picked is `floor(end) - 1`, a whole day short of the end.
   - **Failed again 2026-11-06 (the full run on 3 servers after the overnight fixes: "death day -6 moved it to shed"), fixed again:** when the finished part of the stage was under a whole day, the first whole day past it already ran over the stage's end. Now the death day is never past the stage's last whole day (a little before the shape is quite finished, the stage kept); the unit test `TreeLifeTest` reads every worked-out day on its last tick too, for the camel thorn as well.

135. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (its replay passes 3 of 3 on the overnight code, now required; likely by the BUGS 141 and 144 changes): termites climbing the mound's central shaft hang over the tunnel mouths in its wall** (2026-11-06, found by the new `holds-its-face` rule in the full run on 6 servers, replaying BUGS 113's report; real sizes). In a big mound the shaft's wall is a column of tunnel blocks, each with its 6-pixel opening facing the shaft (`termite_tunnel` with `west=true`, up and down open too). A termite going up or down that wall keeps to the middle of the block's face, which is exactly over the openings: a real-size worker (0.15 long) stands with nothing under its legs, 0.375 of hole round it, and the rule found workers resting there 5 s or more (at 794.935, 72.4, -644.5, holding east). Before real sizes the big body covered the hole. **Not fixed yet:** the fix is in the legs, to climb a face with an opening beside the opening (on its solid rim), or go into it. Its own replay, `replay_bug135_termites_over_tunnel_mouths`, is a known bug (listed in every run, does not fail it); the replay of BUGS 113 now counts only the Matabele's rules (`"species"`), the bug it is about.

136. **Open (flake): a fallen log on the edge of a step was not tunnelled under** (2026-11-06, the full run on 6 servers after the real-size review fixes; `afallenlogontheedgeofastepiseatenfrombelowbyday`: "the tunnel and the feeding gallery under the log were not dug"). Passed 3 of 3 alone on the same code straight after, and in the full run before; cause not known yet.

137. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (test tool): the two Matabele guard films change on every launch** (2026-11-06, found while checking the guard films after the real-size review fixes). `q_clamped_head` and `r_crowd_space` showed every picture changed (4 and 10 percent), and filmed again straight after approving them, changed again on the same code: each Matabele worker picks its own length from the real size spread when it is made (`MatabeleEntity`, `worker_sizes`), so the film's ants are a little bigger or smaller each launch. So these two scenes cannot catch a change picture for picture; their automatic checks (parts inside blocks, jumps) still work, and the pictures looked right. **Fix to make:** the film's Matabele get a fixed length (for example the size group's middle), then approve both once more. **Fix (2026-11-06, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`):** a film's "walker" line takes a sixth word of saved data (`FilmDirector.putWalker`), and both scenes give their Matabele `BodyLength:15f` (the major model's own length), so they are the same size every launch; approve both once more.

139. **Fixed 2026-11-06: half the soak servers found no colonies to watch (found 2026-11-05 by the soak `tools/soak.sh 2 2` and again 2026-11-06 by `tools/soak.sh 4 1`, seeds 20261105 and 424242: "no place with colonies to watch found in 12 tries").** Two causes. (1) The soak's colony pick (`RaidSoak.subjects`) took only Matabele colonies within 80 blocks of the place (`radius - 48`) though it held 128 blocks loaded, and the new-land Matabele there stood 83 to 112 blocks off; now up to 112 (once picked, the land round each watched colony is held by itself). (2) While finding, the game runs flat out, so the 2,400 ticks given to settle were 2 to 6 real seconds, while new-land mounds go in one at a time with their shapes grown in the background; now the soak also waits (at most 3 real minutes) until no mound place round it is still waiting (`Soak.Part.stillSettling`, `NewLandMounds.stillWaiting`), and tries 24 places, not 12. Also found: the 2-day soak's "Raids started: 0" was not "no raids": its one raid ran out of sight (the soak's unload poke), which the count leaves out. **Lesson:** a soak that finds nothing to watch tests nothing; read its "watching" line first.

140. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (the first fix was not enough, see the sub-bullet; its replay now passes 3 of 3 and is required): a scout going home alone stood all night by the same ravine as BUGS 121 (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak `tools/soak.sh 4 100`, server 1, seed 8675309, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`).** Matabele major 0e17b9c3 of colony 58845cca came back from scouting at 20:42 at 2054, 73, 2026, 33 blocks from its nest across the ravine, and was sent home along a trail by itself; it stood there till past 04:51 (raider-stuck at 20:43, 02:48, 04:51; home-by-night at 22:14). Report `run-soak/1/reports/2026-11-06_05-02-16-raider-stuck` (in the soak's copy `.claude/worktrees/soak-100`); replay `replay_bug140_scout_by_ravine` (known bug). **The cause (read in the code):** going home by itself it walks to a place 6 blocks along the trail ahead of it (`WayHome.orderFor`); with that place within a hop (14 blocks) the ant only tried a straight walk there, and on failing waited 5 s and tried the same again; BUGS 121's slanting hops ran only for places farther than a hop. Here the trail's next place lay where it could not walk (down the ravine's side). **Fix:** an ant going home alone that finds no way to a near place tries the slanting hops too (`MatabeleRaidGoal.walkToward`), and after 3 tries in a row with no way (15 s) it leaves the trail and makes straight for the entrance, hop by hop (`WayHome.leaveTrail`, `Raids.leaveTrailHome`, a `[raid]` diary line). **Lesson:** a fallback for "far" must also cover "near but unreachable".
    - **Fixed again 2026-11-06:** its replay still failed after the first fix (3 of 3). Read with a log line: the scout was already heading straight for the entrance, 33 blocks off across a lake in a pit, and every hop (straight, half, 45 and 90 degrees each side) had its landing in the lake, over the drop or inside the hill beside it, so `RaidGround.standAt` gave nothing and no route was ever even asked for. Now, last of all, an ant going home alone asks its legs for any floor some blocks nearer home (`MatabeleRaidGoal.walkToward`: a foothold up to half a hop nearer, the search heading toward home first); in the replay it walks round the lake and gets home. **Lesson:** guessing landing spots by straight lines fails on broken ground; let the route search find the way when the guesses run out.

141. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (not yet checked in a run): Matabele raiders stood 30 s and more on their dead mound's hump, walking a place a block or two off they never reached (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak, server 4, colony fd714cc2 at 933, 72, -13, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`).** At a raid's start, major 31071349 stood at 936.1, 70.5, -13.3 for 30 s "walking", its place flicking between 935, 72, -13, 936, 70, -13 and 938, 66, -13 (not-stuck); 3 minutes later 1848683c stood at the same spot 30 s, told to 942, 65, -13 (raider-stuck). The saved place shows the colony's refuse heap (`termite_remains`, 8 layers, a full block) right there beside the entrance, where the column's first places lie. Reports `run-soak/4/reports/2026-11-06_05-05-05-not-stuck` and `05-05-07-raider-stuck`. **The cause (read in the code):** each flick of its place gave the ant a new walk, so the legs' own stuck check (6 s a step, 3 tries) never ran out; and the column's "no headway" check (`RaidColumn.headway`) looks only at raiders sent more than `HEADWAY_FAR` blocks off. **Fix:** a raider (in a column or going home alone) that walks 8 s without moving half a block, not waiting for the crowd, stops and stands where it is for 5 s before trying again (`MatabeleRaidGoal.gotNowhere`). Not fixed: why a column's place falls where the heap is (the line starts beside the chimney, the heap is carried out beside the entrance); to look at if the soak shows it again. **Lesson:** a walk given again and again resets every stuck clock under it; the mind needs its own clock for "got nowhere".
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** the fix lives on as `entity/goal/Headway`, shared by `MatabeleRaidGoal` and `MatabeleWayHomeGoal`; the old column's own "no headway" rule that sent such an ant home is gone (166's line).

142. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (the rule, not the game; not yet checked in a run): a scout still running home at 22:13 broke the home-by-night rule (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak, server 1, colony 58845cca, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`; BUGS 123's part (b)).** Major 5e53d208's search ended with the scouting hours (19:30, "comes home with nothing") far out; it ran home straight on its own scout goal, round the ravine by the nest, and was still walking (at 2004, 72, 1996, 40 blocks from home, moving every second) at 22:13. Report `run-soak/1/reports/2026-11-06_05-09-45-home-by-night`. The rule counted as "walking home" only ants going home along a trail (`Raids.isGoingHome`). **Fix (`RaidRules.homeByNight`):** a Matabele on its scout goal and not standing counts as on its way home too (scouts search only in the raid hours, so at night that goal only runs home); one standing still is left to raider-stuck. **Lesson:** a rule about "going home" must know every way an ant goes home.

143. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (not yet checked in a run): Matabele going home stood for minutes a few blocks from their dead mound, "no route" (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak, servers 1 and 2, colonies 58845cca at 2044, 71, 1993 and 2abc44a7 at 22246, 78, 2163, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`).** Raider 2ce7e30e, "going home (left outside with nothing to do there)", stood 60 s on flat grass 11 blocks from its nest, its navigator saying "no route (last search failed)"; b8013ee4 the same 10 blocks out, and scouts that gave up on their way home (3f1be49f, 1c358b7e) too. Cause: an ant let go walks home by `WayHome.orderFor`, which gives a place along its trail and, for the last stretch or with no trail, the colony's entrance, `colony.getCenter()`. A dead mound's entrance is its chimney's open mouth, air over the shaft: no walk can end there, so every route search failed, and "home" (within 8 blocks) was never reached from 10 or 11 out. The scout's own walk home (`MatabeleScoutGoal.walkHome`) aimed at the same mouth. Fix: both now aim at ground an insect can stand on beside the entrance on its own side (`WayHome.nearEntrance`: 5, 3 or 6.5 blocks from it, turning up to about 115 degrees either way, for the way home; 1.5, 2 or 1 for a scout, who must come within 2.5 to tell its find), the entrance only if none is found. **Lesson:** a target cell must be a cell something can stand in; the colony's centre is a place to measure from, not to walk to (the column already started beside the chimney for the same reason, R3).

144. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (not yet checked in a run): termites "floating" by chimney mouths and under tunnel holes, BUGS 135's leftovers (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak, server 4, a termite mound near 994, 83, 12, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`, `holds-its-face` rule, 3 reports).** Two kinds. (1) A worker on the side of a full chimney mouth (`termite_mouth`, `slim=false`) at 994.500, 83.930, 14.009, pressed against it, was reported floating: the rule asked "is there anything this insect bumps into beside it?", and termites pass through a mouth block (their way in), so for a termite there was nothing; the rule's mistake, not the legs'. Now it looks at the face's own shape, as for any creature (`GeneralRules.floats`). (2) A worker resting upside down in an open cell under a tunnel block whose opening faces down (`termite_tunnel`, `down=true`) hung right under the hole, nothing over its legs: BUGS 135's fix held walls on the rim beside an opening, not ceilings. Now a ceiling hold goes to the solid rim the nearer way out of the opening too (`AntNavigator.ceilingPoint`). **Lesson:** a rule about what a body touches must ask about the block's shape, not about what that body may walk through.

145. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (see the last sub-bullet): a termite worker stood 31 s on a raid's fight spot, walking to grass (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak, server 2, termites 3199b021 raided by Matabele 2abc44a7, report `run-soak/2/reports/2026-11-06_05-17-49-not-stuck` in the soak worktree, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`).** Worker c0639a7c, on its way to clip grass at 22230, 65, 2089, stood still at 22234.1, 65.1, 2098.1 with its route for 31 s while two Matabele majors fought round the same cell (fight spot 22234, 65, 2099); it neither fled nor was taken. Not known yet why: the crowd wait gives up after its few seconds; a guess is that it was held (pinned) by a grab again and again, or stood in a gallery counted as its nest, where it does not flee (`FleeGoal` runs only outside the nest) and the hunters leave it alone (`isSheltered`). The report holds the place and the diary; a replay is the next step.
    - Again 2026-11-06 (soak server 4, report `run-soak/4/reports/2026-11-06_06-07-24-not-stuck`): termite worker 76dac8aa stood 30 s and more at 990, 69, -6, beside the Matabele fd714cc2's remembered good spot at 989, 69, -7 (a feeding place they raid).
    - Again and again 2026-11-06 on soak server 2 (old code), four or five termite workers at a time at 22230 to 22232, 64 to 65, 2099 to 2102, inside the mud casts of a feeding spot, days 40 to 46. Two known-bug replays made from the soak, `bug145_termite_in_feeding_cast` (server 4's place) and `soak_termite_under_mud_cast` (server 2's): on the night's newest code (BUGS 122, 135, 144, 148 fixed) both still break "not stuck" in most runs (on 3 servers: 4 of 9 runs and 6 of 9; the second one's first report, a termite floating under the cast, no longer happens: BUGS 144's fix). So a termite inside a feeding spot's mud cast still gets stuck; still open. Next: run either replay with the labels and read a stuck termite's diary (its route, the blocks round it in the report's `place.nbt`).
    - **The fix (2026-11-06):** every stuck termite in the replays' reports was a forager (`ClipGrassGoal`) going for wood one block up, standing in an open mud cast's top. Two loops: (1) its feet a hair under the cell beside the wood (at 64.9, so counted in the cell below, not beside the wood), its walk to the cell a tenth of a block up slipped back and began again for ever; (2) the last little step to the wood's face (BUGS 132) dropped it into the cast below, the walk back up wiped the step's own "no nearer" clock, and it stepped and walked for ever. Now a forager within a quarter block of a cell beside its food stops walking and nibbles, and the last little step has 3 s in all for one plant, over every try. Both replays pass 17 of 18 runs on 3 servers (were 6 of 18 and 3 of 9); the one left is a different insect, a nurse stuck in the cast walking home (BUGS 156). The first replay is now required; the second stays optional while BUGS 156 shows in it. **Lesson:** a small body's feet sit near a cell's edge; a "which cell am I in" check needs a little height slack, and a "can't get nearer" clock must not be reset by the walk it falls back to.

146. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (not yet checked in a run): termite colonies new land put in far from every player starved their queen and king while nobody looked (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak, servers 1 to 4: of the unvisited new-land colonies, cde2f500, 25c89667, a21cab51 and 447d4478 had their queens, some their kings too, starve "while away"; others, 1eba7f2f, 576bfbe0, b23f1885, 61bf6841, 4cd9ec11, 01bd3c1a, had their queens last fed at the world's start and were on the way; their saves showed `queenHome` 0, `gardenSeen` combs 0 and nodules 0, while the one colony the pretend player stood by, c5f0408f, had `queenHome` 1 and 54 combs).** The brain writes down what it needs for the time away (is the queen in her chamber, how much comb and how many nodules the garden has) once a second while the colony is here. Since the far rule (2026-11-04), a colony with no player within 64 blocks lives as numbers; new land puts its mounds in when the land round them is loaded, usually 100 or more blocks from the player, so the colony went away on its first second and was never "here": the brain thought the queen out of her chamber (no nurse feeds her there) and the garden empty (no nurse food at all, no nodules growing), and the royal pair starved after the queen's starve time. In a player's world this would have killed nearly every termite colony the player had not walked up to, and with them the raids' prey. Fix: as a colony goes away with its land still loaded, the brain takes one last look (`NestLife.watch` and the new hook `ColonyHooks.LOOK_ON_LEAVING`, which the fungus garden answers by counting its comb, `FungusGarden.look`). A colony away in unloaded land keeps what was seen last, as before. **Lesson:** anything the brain only learns "while here" needs a first look for colonies born out of sight; the game tests never saw it because the far rule is off on the test server.

147. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (watch rule only): "heartbeat broken, the raid (forming) was last worked out 4100 s ago while its colony is here" (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak, server 4, Matabele fd714cc2, report `run-soak/4/reports/2026-11-06_05-25-21-heartbeat`).** The colony had been away 0.8 hours with a raid forming; while away a raid column is not worked out (the away raids take over), and the rule looked in the colony's first second back, before the raid's own second. Not a game bug. Fix: the rule counts only from the colony's coming back (`AwayLife.backAt`).

148. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (see the last sub-bullet): a termite worker carrying an egg stood almost 6 minutes on top of its mound, on a thin mound layer over a closed tunnel block, "walking" to the nest 17 blocks down (found 2026-11-06 by the 100-day soak, server 2, colony 3199b021, report `run-soak/2/reports/2026-11-06_05-26-08-not-stuck`).** Worker a2aa5a29 at 22244, 83.3, 2109, `CarryEggGoal`, its route to 22245, 66, 2113, still 349 s; under it a tunnel block open only north and down, beside it air to the east and south (the mound's outside); two diggers in the spire's column just below. Odd too: the not-stuck rule (30 s) only spoke at 347 s. Not looked into yet: why an egg carrier was on the mound's outside top, and why its route does not move it (a climb down the outside from a 1-layer mound piece?). Replay: `replay_bug148_egg_carrier_on_mound_top` (category `replays`, not required until fixed).
    - Same colony again 2026-11-06 (soak server 2, report `run-soak/2/reports/2026-11-06_05-47-42-not-stuck`): a nurse, `FeedLarvaGoal`, stood 82 s at 22225, 65, 2104 walking to the nursery at 22249, 66, 2117; maybe one cause (a way into this nest cut off).
    - And again (soak server 2, report `run-soak/2/reports/2026-11-06_06-11-12-not-stuck`): termite worker d85a1764 stood 155 s at 22243, 87, 2111, up on the same mound, carrying a body (`CarryCorpseGoal`) to 22241, 66, 2118, 21 blocks below: so not only egg carriers, any carrier whose drop spot is deep under the mound top. Its diary shows how it got there: it climbed the mound as a builder (`BuildMoundGoal`, walking to 22243, 88, 2111, up from 70 inside), stood at 87 and placed its piece; from then on every job it picked (`DigGoal` down at 64 to 65, then the body) found no way and it stood: so the builder may close the very way it came up by. Next: look at the blocks round 22243, 87, 2111 in that report's `place.nbt`, and if so, keep a builder from placing the piece its own way down runs through (or let a stranded insect on the mound skin climb down its outside).
    - **The fix (2026-11-06):** the termite stood on a ramp of the mound (a slope block) and a builder laid a thin layer (`termite_mound`, 2 layers) on the block over it. A slope only counts as a floor with room over it (`Foothold.isValid`), so its own cell gave no place to start from; the walker then looked beside it only for floors within a drop, and on the face of a mound there were none within 12 blocks, so no route was ever found. And when a route did start beside it (BUGS 122: a termite inside a thin mud plate), the walker never stepped over to it: every tick it "landed somewhere else", planned again, and gave up after 3 tries. Now (`AntNavigator.plan`) a shut-in insect also takes the walls beside it as starts, and steps straight out onto the start spot it picked (`stepOutTo`: a short hop of at most a block, right against the wall it will hold; over a drop it steps out at its own height and falls onto the floor below). New test `TermiteClimbingTests.aTermiteOnARampLaidOverFindsItsWayDown` (a ramp 14 up a wall with a fresh layer laid over it: the termite climbs down to the ground; it failed before the fix). The replays of BUGS 148 and 122 pass 3 of 3 and are now required. **Lesson:** a "start beside" is only half the fix: the walker must also be able to get onto it.

151. **Open (test flake): the user's half-built stage 14 mound test (`TermiteCathedralTests.termitesFinishTheUsersHalfBuiltStageFourteen`) failed once in the overnight category run (2026-11-06, `legs,raids,soldiers,termitefood,colony,away,mound` on 3 servers beside the soak), "stage 14 is not finished", and passed 3 of 3 alone on the same code the same night (10 minutes).** It failed the same way once before, in the Apex step 10 full run (2026-10-22, 3 of 3 alone then too), so it is not from the night's `AntNavigator` changes (BUGS 141, 144). The test runs a living colony against a clock with the user's mound, so a slow night (several servers, the soak beside them) may leave a few pieces unbuilt at its end. Next time: look at its last `[user-mound-test]` line (what was left) and whether the builders were stuck or only slow; if only slow, give the test more time (box 3), if stuck, it is BUGS 148's kind (insects on a mound top).

152. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (test only): the half-fed scouts test failed "no dead mound put in" every time it ran beside the mound-ringing scout test (found by the overnight full run on 3 servers, branch `claude/overnight-bugs`; 3 of 3 on a rerun of the two together).** New land's mound placing keeps mounds 40 blocks apart (`NewLandMounds.crowded`), and it counted the living mound of the test in the arena next door (arenas are about 45 blocks apart). A leak between tests (box 4), not a game bug. **Fix:** the crowding check skips a colony or dead mound in another running test's arena (`TestIsolation.inDifferentTests`, always false outside the test server); the two tests then passed 3 of 3 together. **Lesson:** a "keep apart" distance bigger than the gap between arenas reaches into the next test.

153. **Open (test flake): the mound-ringing scout test (`ascoutringsalivingmounditcomesnearandfollowsatermitetunnelouttothefeeding`) failed once in the overnight full run (2026-11-06, 3 servers, the soak beside them): "the find was not made by following a tunnel from the mound (0 followed, 1 clue finds)"; it passed 3 of 3 right after on the same code.** Its scout found the feeding by ringing the mound and not by following the tunnel out. Same night the dead mound next door was failing (152), so its neighbour's colony may have been in play; next time it fails, look at the scout's `[raid]` diary lines: did it smell the tunnel at all?
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** again in step 5's runs (2 of 3 `raids,away,soldiers` runs, 3 servers), with a new message: "the find at ... is not at the four termites at the east tunnel's end" (the scout took a find 23 blocks out, worth 60, by ringing the mound, before it followed the tunnel: `mound clue: 1 mound(s) ringed, 0 tunnel(s) followed, 1 find(s)`). Passed alone 3 of 3 both times. Not from step 5 (the first time was after part 1, which only deleted code nothing called). Still open.

154. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (test only): the falling tree test ("the tree went before its creak was over: 282 of 382") failed every run after the night's second BUGS 134 fix.** That fix lets a new-land dead tree's picked death day fall a little before its stage's slow change is finished, when there is no whole day both finished and inside the stage; the test's shed tree then stood not quite shed, and the fall command (`fellNow`) first puts the tree into its finished shed shape, losing 100 high blocks before the creak. Not a game bug (the command reshaping a tree it fells is on purpose); the test counted the standing tree before the command. **Fix:** the test counts the standing wood again right after the command; it passed 3 of 3. **Lesson:** a test's "before" count must be taken after the last step that is allowed to change it.

156. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (branch `claude/soak-followups`; its replay passes 18 of 18 runs on 6 servers and is now required): a termite nurse stood 30 s inside a feeding spot's mud cast, walking to the nursery 23 blocks off (found 2026-11-06 by the replay `soak_termite_under_mud_cast`, 1 run in 18 after the BUGS 145 fix, report `run-test-shards/3/reports/2026-11-06_08-01-05-not-stuck`).** Worker 44cac6e7 (`FeedLarvaGoal`) at 22230.4, 64.3, 2102.6 inside a mud cast, "walking" to 22245, 66, 2120, its height going 64.3 to 64.6 and back, so it tries to climb and falls back. Probably the same as BUGS 148's sub-bullet of a nurse standing 82 s at 22225, 65, 2104 in the same colony. The legs inside a tube block of mud (`AntNavigator`, the tube walls of BUGS 129). Next: a replay made from that report, and the route's first steps from inside the cast.
    - Looked into 2026-11-06 (same night): replay `bug156_nurse_in_cast` made (known bug); it breaks "not stuck" 1 run in 9. Its report shows **seven** termites in that one cast cell (22230, 64, 2102), all going home through it (store, nursery, comb); the others came and went within 2 to 5 s, one stood 30 s, rising to 64.8 and falling back to 64.3 (the cast's floor), so it tries to climb out over the rim and slips. Insects in a channel do not take turns in the crowd grid (`Crowd`), so it is not a wait for room. A test of the same shape (four casts sunk level with the ground, open to each other and the sky, seven termites in one, `TermiteClimbingTests.aTermiteInASunkMudCastClimbsOutAndAway`) passes 3 of 3: the jam needs the real place. Next: in the replay, log one stuck termite's steps (the step kind, the wall it holds, why it lets go) round the cast's rim.

158. **Open: a raid column crashed the server as it set out from a scout's find right beside the nest (found 2026-11-06 by a test run on branch `claude/m8-termite-losses`, `raids,away,colony,soldiers`, shard 4; old code, not step 3d's).** `RaidColumn.<init>` line 360: `trail.subList(0, Math.max(1, gather) + 1)` asked for two points of a trail only one long. The trail drops every point nearer the entrance than the line's start (`trail.removeIf(...)`, then adds the start), so a find whose whole scent line lies within the first block or two of the entrance leaves a trail of only the start; `gather` is 0, and `Math.max(1, gather)` makes it 1, past the end. Crash report: `run-test-shards/4/crash-reports/crash-2026-11-06_14.39.34-server.txt` (`IndexOutOfBoundsException: toIndex = 2`, from `MatabeleScoutGoal.walkHome` through `Raids.found` and `startRaid`). Fix to make: cap the index at the trail's length, or give a one-point trail the find itself as its second point (and ask whether a find that near the nest should start a raid at all: real scouts recruit to spots metres away). In play a crash like this stops the game, so it goes first to the bug-fixing session.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed, it cannot happen any more. The march's line always has at least two points now: when every point of the scent line lies by the entrance and only the line's start is left, the find itself is its second point (`RaidMarch`'s constructor, which took over the old `RaidColumn.<init>` cut and still had it). Test: `aRaidToAFindRightBesideTheNestSetsOutWithoutACrash` (`MatabeleRaidChainTests`).

162. **Fixed: in a single-biome savanna world most dead mounds stood empty, Matabele rare (the user's report 2026-11-06, "I looked at 20 termite mounds and didn't find one"; branch `claude/m8-termite-losses`).** The user plays single-biome savanna worlds (`minecraft:fixed` savanna in `level.dat`); their world's log: 44 mounds, 11 dead, 4 with Matabele, the 7 empty ones all near spawn. Replayed on their seed through the same rules (a one-off game test on a single-biome savanna made from the noise): the game matched the replay mound for mound, so no difference between world making and the server. The cause was the rule from BUGS 104: a dead mound whose dice gave it Matabele lived instead when no mound *living by its dice* stood within 90; rugged single-biome land puts its mounds about 150 apart (only about 1 place in 4 or 5 holds one), so 2 in 3 of the would-be Matabele mounds turned living, while the empty dead mounds (the dice's no) all stayed: most dead mounds seen were empty, Matabele about 1 mound in 7 near spawn. The survey test that guards the share only read normal worlds, and so does the soak. Fix: `MoundPlaces.livesInstead`: every dead mound, Matabele or empty, with no mound living by the dice within reach lives instead unless a higher-ranked one within reach does (goes down the ranks only, so never round in a circle, like `isPlace`); so every dead mound has a living one within 90, and a dead mound's Matabele dice alone decide (about 85 in 100 dead mounds hold a colony, on any land). Measured: the user's world 19 to 25 percent of mounds Matabele, empty dead mounds 20 to 10 in 230 mounds; normal worlds 21 to 26 percent. The survey test now also reads single-biome savanna (the user's seed and seed 1) and fails outside 18 to 30 percent or with fewer than 3 in 4 dead mounds holding Matabele. **Lesson:** a rule leaning on how far apart things stand must be checked on the land the user really plays (here single-biome savanna, rugged), not only the land the tests make.

163. **Open: the termite eating test ran out of time once (found 2026-11-06 by the run `colony,raids,mound,away` on 6 servers while fixing BUGS 162, branch `claude/m8-termite-losses`; mound placement is not in this test, which builds its own nest).** `termiteworkerseatnoduleswhenyoungandoldcombwhenolder`: "Didn't succeed or fail within 4800 ticks" on shard 4, beside the Matabele and new-land tests; passed 3 of 3 alone on 3 servers just after. Unlike BUGS 119 (a worker sunk in the comb, a failed check) this is a timeout: some check never came true. Next: when it fails again, read the shard's log for what the test was still waiting on. The same run's other failure, the harvester neighbours test, is the old BUGS 68 (passed 3 of 3 alone).

164. **Open: a scout once never noticed the living mound in the mound-as-a-clue test (found 2026-11-06 by the run `raids` on 6 servers, branch `claude/raid-flow`; scouting code untouched by the raid flow change).** `ascoutringsalivingmounditcomesnearandfollowsatermitetunnelouttothefeeding` failed "no scout noticed the living mound yet" (its scouts reached 46 blocks, one good spot already known 38 out). It passed in two runs before and after, and 6 of 6 alone on every server. Not looked into yet. **Lesson:** none yet.

166. **Open: a raider on a tall dead mound's side walks but gets nowhere for 15 s and is sent home "stuck" (found 2026-11-06 by the tall mound test `araidfromatalldeadmoundsetsoutpromptlywitheveryrecruitthatcameout`, about 1 run in 6, branch `claude/raid-queue-openings-307d34`).** After the raid queue fix the column never sends a raider back up the hump and never calls one on the hump's side "down the chimney", yet now and then one raider two blocks under the hump's top and two off the trail (on a mound whose side drops 6 blocks at once) is told to a place a few blocks down the trail, its legs report a route ("walking true"), and it stays in its cell for 15 s until the column lets it go home along the trail. Likely the legs (`AntNavigator`, going over the edge of a tall drop beside the chimney), not the march: the order it held was sound. The test allows one such raider and logs it. Lesson: a raid's safety net (let go after 15 s) hides a legs problem; look at which cell and route when it fires.
    - **Cause (found 2026-11-06 from the replay's route lines):** a mud cast whose channel opens up has a hole 6 pixels wide in the middle of its top, and a termite worker at real size (0.13 wide) fits through it, but the route planner counts the top as floor. A worker that came up out of a cast over one rim walked straight on across the hole to the next cell, dropped back into the channel (at 64.3 while its route said "on top at 65"), stood "stuck on walk", planned again, and did the same; others walked from one open-topped cast onto the next and fell in the same way, some on down through a cast open below too. And once inside a side arm of a cast (the hollow out to an open side), the legs still read the body as standing on the floor above, so the new route started on top again. **Fix (`AntNavigator`):** a floor step from or into a cell whose ground has such a hole keeps to the rim, as a real termite walks round a hole it is not going down (`roundHoles`): still in front of the hole in the cell it leaves, it steps sideways out of its way first; heading into a cell with a hole, it gets out onto the rim's line before the hole's band, as the straight way to the rim point cut the hole's near corner (half of those that still fell in with the first version did it that way, 2 of 6 servers). A body whose middle lies in a cast's side arm is read as in the channel (`inSideArm`), so a route from there crawls out. On the old code 5 of 6 servers broke the replay within 1 to 3 runs; with the first half of the fix 2 of 6; with both 0 of 6. The soak's termite stuck by casts at 989, 68, -6 (server 4) was the same kind (casts with air under them, open up and down): its replay `soak4_termite_in_cast_going_home` passes 18 of 18 runs on 6 servers on the new code (not run on the old), now required. **Lesson:** a floor with a hole in it is only a floor round the hole; a body small enough to fit the hole must be steered round it, and the legs must know where the body is when it falls in anyway.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed as written: nobody is "sent home stuck" any more. The per-ant "no headway for 15 s, go home" rule was not carried into `Raid` or `RaidMarch` (step 1 measured it emptying a column one ant at a time); a raider that falls behind is left to the stage's deadline and then walks the trail home alone. The legs fault under it (an ant that walks and gets nowhere on a steep side) is not shown gone: its entry now is BUGS 313.

159. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (watch rule only, branch `claude/soak-followups`): "heartbeat broken" for a raid just forming (found 2026-11-06 by the replay `soak4_raider_on_hump`, made from the 100-day soak's server 4, Matabele fd714cc2).** A raid column's "last worked out" clock starts below zero (`RaidColumn.updatedAt` is `-EVERY` until its first update, which is how the column knows it was never worked out), so in the second between a raid starting and its first update the heartbeat rule read a raid "last worked out" more than a game's age ago. The rule now counts from the latest of the last update, the raid's start and the colony's return (`RaidRules`). Nothing in the game changed. The soak's raider-stuck report at 936, 70, -13 did not come back in the replay's runs; the replay stays a known bug (optional) for now.
    - Lesson: a clock that starts below zero on purpose is a trap for anyone else reading it; a rule must also count from the thing's start.

160. **Fixed 2026-11-06 (not yet tried in-game; branch `claude/soak-followups`): a Matabele on open grass under a standing dead tree "is shut in" and bit at the grass every second (found 2026-11-06 by the replay `soak4_raider_going_home`, raider e31d457d at 980, 70, 6, near the living termite mound at 995, 70, 11; 119 "[raid] ... shut in ... bites its way out" diary lines in about 10 minutes across its runs).** Over it, 15 to 18 blocks up, was the bare bleached crown of a dead tree (980 to 990, 72 to 88, -4 to 7): wood, not leaves, so BUGS 113's leaf rule did not let the sky through, and the shut-in walk (40 cells) never got out from under a crown that wide. Fix: an ant is shut in only with a roof within 3 blocks over it (`MatabeleBiteOutGoal.roofed`; a seal of soil or mud is always right on top; 3 my pick). The termite-hunting side (`FightGoal`, `Fights.isShutIn`) was left as it is: a termite under a dead tree is still chased.
    - Lesson: "covered" has a height. A crown far up is shade, not a roof.

161. **Open: a Matabele going home along the trail stood more than 60 s by two fallen branches, "walking" to 935, 69, -17 (found 2026-11-06 by the replay `soak4_raider_going_home`, 1 run in 3; report `run-test-shards/2/reports/2026-11-06_15-13-45-raider-stuck`, with `15-12-49-not-stuck` and `15-16-50-home-by-night`).** Raider e31d457d, "going home (left outside with nothing to do there)", at 943.5, 65.0, -11.7: inside the cell of a fallen branch (`fallen_branch`, axis x, at 943 and 944, 65, -12, and 944, 65, -11), dry grass at 943, 65, -11, a hole at 942, 64, -13. No route lines for it in the debug log in that minute, so its walk neither moved nor planned again. Rules broken: not stuck, raider stuck, home by night. Likely the "shut in where it stands" kind (BUGS 122, 148: a body inside a thin block's cell given no place to start from) or BUGS 143's way home; not looked into further (the user's call: 156 first, then move on). The replay stays a known bug (optional).
    - The run after the fixes of 159 and 160 (2026-11-06, `raids` and the three soak replays on 6 servers, 19 minutes): 15 of 16 required passed. `soak4_raider_on_hump` and `soak2_termite_under_cast_hole` 3 runs each with no rule broken. In `soak4_raider_going_home` no "shut in" line any more (BUGS 160), but 161 came back in run 1 at 943, 65, -12 (report `run-test-shards/2/reports/2026-11-06_15-31-19-not-stuck`). The one required failure was the old whole-chain raid flake, BUGS 93 ("no carrier was out on the way home to kill").
116. **Fixed 2026-11-05 (branch `claude/bug116-ground-top-9145dc`): about 15 places that look for the ground's top found the top of a giant tree's crown instead (found 2026-11-05 while fixing BUGS 113, branch `claude/m8-still-when-idle-07d154`).** Our tree leaves are solid blocks (`TreeLeavesBlock`), not Minecraft's leaves, so the game's height map 'without leaves' (`MOTION_BLOCKING_NO_LEAVES`) stops at a crown's top. Places that use it for the ground: `GroundReader.surface`, `ScoutGoal`, `MatabeleScoutGoal.openGround`, `RaidColumn`, `Mushrooms`, `PreyPile`, `NewLandMounds` (two), `MatabeleColonies`, `DeadWoodCall`, `MatabeleCommands` (two). Under a crown they may treat the ground as roofed, or aim at a spot 15 to 20 blocks up in the leaves. Fix: one shared 'ground top' helper that looks down past leaves (as `Fights.isInTheOpen` now does), used by all of them, then a full run (it touches worldgen and the shared ground reader). **Fix:** one shared helper in the framework, `colony/GroundTop` (`y`, `cell`, `isRoofed`, `isGround`): it takes Minecraft's height map without leaves and looks down past anything in the `minecraft:leaves` tag (and the open air under a crown) to the first block that stops a walker, or water; wood still counts (a trunk or a limb, as Minecraft's own map counts it). It reads one column only and never loads land, so new-land mounds can use it while chunks load. Every caller moved onto it: the 12 listed, `Fights.isInTheOpen` (its BUGS 113 walk now the helper), the desert mod's `Hill`, `TrunkTrails` and `PatrolGoal`, and three "is it open to the sky" checks that used the map with leaves (`Nest.isEntranceCovered`, the mound's roofed shaft in `Mound.addInside`, `DeadWorks.underTheSky`), so a crown high over a young mound's shaft or a mud plate no longer counts as its roof. Left as they are on purpose: `FoodStore`'s column scan (it already looks under crowns, `scanUnderCrown`), `Forest.sproutSite` (its own walk notes the crown), world making's `_WG` maps (no trees yet then) and the game tests' flat arenas. Test: the ground under the camel thorn crown in `aMatabeleShutInUnderTheGroundBitesItsWayOutButNeverThroughTheMound` (the grass, the ground reader's surface, no roof, a log under the crown still counted). Lesson: never use a height map for "the ground" in this mod; ask `GroundTop`.

117. **Open: the test 'idle termites never nap nor clean their antennae' fails every run since step 3Q gave termite workers a cleaning clip (found 2026-11-05 by the full run on 6 servers for BUGS 116, branch `claude/bug116-ground-top-9145dc`; fails alone too; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `TermiteBodyTests.idleTermitesNeverNapNorCleanTheirAntennae` still says a termite worker never cleans its antennae ("its species has no cleaning"), but M8 step 3Q (still when idle, 2026-11-05) gave the termite worker and king their own antenna-cleaning clip on purpose (only the soldiers have none, the workers clean them). So the test is out of date (box 2 of the 4-box rule: the behaviour changed on purpose), not the game. Likely fix: the test keeps 'never naps' for termites and checks cleaning on the termite soldier instead (or that a worker's cleaning ends), every check kept.

118. **Open: the dead baobab test's new-land snag once got a death day that moved it out of its mud-climbed stage (found 2026-11-05 by the full run on 6 servers for BUGS 116, branch `claude/bug116-ground-top-9145dc`; passed alone right after; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `adeadbaobabslumpsintoaheapandisgone` failed at its last check: "the new land snag's death day -6 moved it to shed" (a camel thorn snag made at the mud stage, `Forest.update` worked out a death day 6 days back, which put it in the stage before, shedding). The death day inside a stage is a dice roll, so some roll lands outside the stage asked for; the dice may be shared with a neighbouring test (the BUGS 32 kind) or the roll's range is a little too wide. Not touched by BUGS 116 (no ground height in it). Next: run it many times alone (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`) and read how `Forest.update` picks the death day of a new-land snag.

167. **Open: the test `thegalleriesgrowtipbytipwiththecolony` once found a 38-worker termite colony's galleries longer than it allows, 70 blocks against 58 (5 pieces, 1 out to food, 4 tips, reach 19, 13 holes; found 2026-11-07 by the full run on 6 servers after the three side branches were folded into `claude/raid-queue-openings-307d34`; passed 3 of 3 alone right after; not fixed: the rule, found bugs go on the list).** The merged branches touched no gallery code, so it likely fails only beside other tests (box 4, a leak between tests) or when a piece out to food is counted against the budget; a gallery piece grown toward food is meant to come from the same budget (termites underground, step 3). Look at whether the piece to food or a neighbour's known food pushed it over.

168. **Open: a raid that reaches an empty feeding spot paces up and down for about a minute, then leaves (the user's in-game raid, 2026-11-07, report `run/reports/2026-11-07_00-46-33-player`; looked into, not fixed: the user asked for no code yet).** Why it was empty: two other Matabele colonies about 90 blocks off (`0bbb0912`, `d625ded4`), out of the player's 64-block reach, raided the same termite mound as numbers in the minutes before (600 raiders took 825 termites at 538, 74, 228; another raid at 527, 73, 235, 9 blocks from the user's spot); each away raid marks danger for the termites (`RaidsAway`, `danger().hurt`), so the termites kept off the spots and plugged 10 holes, and by day they stay inside anyway, so every real termite was 20 blocks off in the mound's core; the scout had seen 12 there some minutes earlier. What the raiders did: the attack stage sent each one to the spot's fight search with nothing to find until 30 s of quiet (`RaidColumn.attack`, `quiet_ticks`), then the spreading stage stood them on a ring round the spot for `spread_ticks` (`spreadOut`), then home: about 60 s of milling that reads as pacing. They never went deeper because, by the rules built so far, majors break only termite mud and fresh plugs, termites more than 2 cells from an opening are safe, and the mound is never broken. Two findings for the user's call: (1) an away colony's raid hits a termite colony the player is standing beside, invisibly, and empties the spots the player's own colony is heading for; (2) with Matabele in about 85 in 100 dead mounds and mounds 40 to 80 apart, one termite mound now feeds about 3 Matabele colonies, each sending up to 600 raiders, 2 or 3 raids a day. Lesson: an empty raid needs a short, purposeful end (a look round that clearly ends, or digging along the smelt gallery), not two stages of waiting.
    **168, update 2026-11-09: FIXED for the scouts (branch `claude/scout-skips-empty-830783`; the user's raid of 2026-11-09, report `run/reports/2026-11-09_00-01-41-player`: raiders walking back and forth at the termites).** The second report showed the bigger cause: termite colony `4255ac54` had been raided at the branch pile near 1953, 71, -2750 again and again, remembered the attacks, had 16 of its 23 holes shut and kept off the spot (the raid's numbers said so: "the termites keep off the spot"), yet scout `77a96bf8`, rechecking the remembered spot, scored it worth 45: its look counted every by-day feeding place there as 5 hidden termites (`MatabeleScoutGoal.inspect`), and the list it counted from (`FeedingSpots.byDayPlaces`) judged by the structure only (a finished gallery under wood), never whether the termites still fed there; so a 141-ant raid marched to an empty spot and paced (BUGS 191's fix of 2026-11-08, 5 termites a place and the whole patch counted, made such spots score even higher). Fix, no new rule or number: `FeedingSpots.byDayPlaces` and `FeedingSpots.waysOut` (only the scouts use them) leave out every place the termite colony keeps off (`Colony.keepsOff`, the same question the number raiders already ask in `RaidColumn.takeHidden` and the away raids in `RaidsAway`): a sealed-off spot has nobody feeding there to smell, so a recheck finds it empty (marked as such, as before) and the scout searches on elsewhere; once the danger fades (about 2 to 3 days) the place counts again. Real Macrotermes seal and abandon a raided site for a while, and real scouts find termites by detecting them. Termites really out in the open there are still counted live. New check at the end of the mound-clue scout test (`aScoutRingsALivingMoundItComesNearAndFollowsATermiteTunnelOutToTheFeeding`): attacked at the east feeding place, the termites keep off it and it drops out of both lists, with every way to food within the danger's 6-block reach, while ways farther off stay (4 of 4 alone; `raids,away` 30 of 31 on 4 servers, the one failure the old flake 153). Still possible (the 2026-11-07 case, narrower now): a spot found full and then emptied before the raid arrives (by other colonies' hidden raids); the raid then ends after its 30 s of quiet and the spread, as before. Not tried in-game yet.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed for the pacing. No raid sets out to a spot the one judge finds empty (`Raid.termitesThere`, asked by `Raids.startRaid` before anyone is called; a spot the termites keep off counts as empty), and the judge is asked again at the halt: nobody there, the front three look round 10 s (`look_ticks`), the raid turns home and the find is forgotten. The attack and spreading stages that made the minute of pacing are gone. Chain scenes `noRaidSetsOutToASpotWhereNoTermiteIsNowAndTheFindIsForgotten` and `aRaidWhoseTermitesLeftOnTheWayLooksRoundBrieflyAndComesHomeEmpty`. The two balance findings (colonies out of sight emptying the spots a watched colony heads for; one termite mound feeding about three Matabele colonies) are untouched: for step 6's soak.

169. **Open: the low-mound ribbon test jammed once at the fallen camel thorn, 21 of 80 looks 4 abreast (found 2026-11-07 by the run `raids,away,soldiers,colony,legs,replays` on 6 servers while building the ribbon march, branch `claude/raid-queue-openings-307d34`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `araidfromalowdeadmoundmarchesasaribbonofthreesandtwos` failed late in a 25-minute shard, run in one batch with the ridge-and-pond raid test. The scene's dead mound always gets the same fallen dead camel thorn, 22 blocks off its middle (seed 5, try 6), and its limbs lie by the trail about 17 to 22 blocks along. That run's front stood at 17.0 blocks along for 5 s and later at 22.1 for 5 s, once 2 blocks up and 3 to the side (climbing the limbs), with raiders spread 2.4 to 3 blocks to one side, bunched behind it; the march took 80 looks in place of about 50. It passed 6 of 6 straight after with the test alone on all 6 servers, and in every earlier run (fronts at 40 s 28.8 to 31.6 blocks). The failing shard's log was overwritten by a rerun before the tree's timing could be read. Next: when it fails again, read when the camel thorn went in against the march, and how the front's route crosses its limbs; if the ribbon really jams at a fallen tree on its trail, that is a march fix (the formation adapting to logs and branches as it does to humps), not a test change.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed: this test and the ribbon it checked are gone since the stream march (BUGS 187); the low mound's march test is now `aRaidFromALowDeadMoundStreamsAlongItsTrail`.

170. **Open: the guard film `r_crowd_space` (eight Matabele majors in a pen) comes out different on every launch again (found 2026-11-07 while checking the ribbon march; two launches of the same code: 0 of 40 pictures the same).** BUGS 137 had made it repeat (`BodyLength:15f`); something since (the personal-space shuffle's dice, or a clock) varies again. The scene does not use the raid lanes (`setSideShift` stays off), so the ribbon is not the cause. `q_clamped_head` repeats (67 of 70 pictures byte for byte the same; its change from the approved pictures came with code merged after their approval, and the pictures look right: the head on the shin). Both approved again 2026-11-07, `r_crowd_space` keeping its known-bug note. Lesson: a guard scene with a crowd must roll fixed dice, or it can only guard the body checks, not the pictures. Next: find the varying dice (the shuffle in `AntNavigator` or `Crowd`, likely `level.getRandom()`) and give the scene fixed ones.

171. **Open: the whole-chain raid test's march check fails about 1 run in 6 to 8: "on the march others ran ahead of the scout" 45 to 62 ticks of 190 to 220, the limit a fifth** (found 2026-11-07 by the step 5 run `soldiers,raids,colony,away` on 6 servers; then 1 in 6 and 1 in 6 more on the step 5 code, and 1 in 12 on the code before step 5, so it comes from the ribbon march, not the rescue). The march in this test has a stone dropped on the middle of its scent trail; my guess: while the front casts about at the break, raiders behind it walk on in their lanes past the scout. Box 3 or a real bug: to look into (whether raiders should hold back while the front casts about, or the check should leave out the cast).
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** stays open. The march was kept as it was (`RaidMarch`, moved out of the old column unchanged) and the check is still in `aScoutLeadsARaidColumnAlongItsTrailThatStopsShortAttacksTheWorkersAndComesHome`. What would prove it gone: that test alone on every server, about 20 runs, none failing so.

172. **Fixed 2026-11-07: raids set out by a command counted among the colony's own raids of the day** (found by the step 3e and 5 code review). The notes said commands do not count, but `Raids.startRaid` counted every raid, so a tester's `/antcolonies raid` or scout command could use up the colony's day and stop it raiding by itself, which would look like a bug in-game. Now `startRaid` takes `ownRaid`: the colony's own raids (a scout's find, a waiting find) count, the raid command's and the scout command's do not (`raidForTests` stands for the colony's own raid and counts). New check in the dusk and rain raid test. Lesson: when the notes state a rule, the code path for every caller must follow it; check each caller of a shared start.

173. **Fixed 2026-11-07: a dead termite head locked on a leg counted as "needs rescue"** (the step 5 code review). `needsRescue` used `Clamp.isOn()`, true for a dead head too, so a raider with a dead head (step 2c's rule, confirmed in-game: a little slower, may fight on) left the fight at the spot and stood calling, and out of the nest stopped 10 s about every 40 s with a nestmate near. Now only a living foe clamped on (`isLiving()`) or an open wound with legs gone calls; dead heads are bitten off in the nest as before (`ClampCareGoal`). Lesson: a new rule on a shared state must keep what the user already confirmed for its other cases.

174. **Fixed 2026-11-07: a raid that only brought home hurt raiders counted the spot as empty** (the step 5 code review). `foundNothing` and the spread-out choice looked only at termites carried, so a raid where soldiers maimed raiders but no termite was carried spread out 30 s round the spot (its rescuers carrying the hurt) and then marked the spot an empty check. Now a raider hurt at the spot (`RaidColumn.hurtAtSpot`) means the termites were there: no spreading out, no empty check.

175. **Fixed 2026-11-07: a hurt ant whose carry to the hurt room failed was picked up again and again** (the step 5 code review). After 2 minutes `RescueGoal` set it down where it was; outside the nest it called again 30 s later and the next nestmate tried the same carry, for as long as the wound stayed open (a day). Now an ant set down outside the nest calls no more for that wound (`AbstractAntEntity.holdHelpCalls`, a new wound lifts it).

176. **Open: a carried hurt nestmate is placed by the carrier's facing only** (the step 5 code review; already noted as a known limit in `docs/HISTORY.md`). `positionRider` hangs it under the carrier's head as on flat ground, so on a wall (a dead mound's chimney, the way into every Matabele nest) it may sit inside the wall or in the air. New guard film `s_carry_down` (a major carrying a minor down a chimney, near wall glass) and test command `/antcolonies carryhurt` to see it. Filmed 2026-11-07: the carried minor is not inside the shaft's walls (a body part 0.03 blocks in, once), but it stays level, sticking straight out from the major's head into the shaft as it walks down the wall; it should lie along the wall under the carrier. Approved as a known bug; the fix is in the carried ant's drawing (turn it with its carrier's surface), for M8.5's polish.

178. **Open: the low-mound ribbon march test failed once beside other tests: "not a ribbon: only 31 percent of the raiders had a row-mate beside them (single file?)"** (found 2026-11-07 by the `mound,colony,raids` run on 6 servers for the spread-out dead mounds, shard 2; the change touches only where new land puts dead mounds, nothing this test builds). `araidfromalowdeadmoundmarchesasaribbonofthreesandtwos` passed 4 of 4 alone right after, so it is a box-4 flake (passes alone, fails beside others) or a rare march shape; not looked into yet (the user's rule: bugs found by tests go on this list). Next: run it on every shard with its batch mates and log the rows when it fails.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed with the ribbon, as 169.

181. **Open: caves within 16 blocks under the flat savanna's plain, 16 to 20 spots of 600** (found 2026-11-07 by `savannaGroundIsGentle` in the savanna rivers run, `worldgen,mound,colony,raids` on 6 servers, branch `claude/savanna-rivers-a9c96a`). The check (air within 16 blocks under the ground, at most 1 spot in 100) failed with 16. Not from the rivers: the same test on the flat-savanna terrain files of `145390fb` gave 20, the same spots in both, all on the open plain (ground at 70 to 73, `minecraft:savanna`, flat round them). The 5 of 600 measured when the caves were filled (`145390fb`) was a lucky sample: the new floodplain and river biomes changed which map cells count as savanna, so the test's shuffle picks other spots. What they are is not known yet: cheese or spaghetti caves starting 8 to 16 blocks down (the entrances and noodle caves are already filled). Next: look at one in a world (for example seed 1, 2560 -576) to see whether it reaches the grass or stays a closed cave under it, then either fill the shallow caves under the plain in data or loosen the check to what is seen (box 3).
   **Fixed 2026-11-07 (box 3, the test too strict; branch `claude/bug181-plain-caves-1d82ea`):** a probe of the noise at every flagged spot showed what they are: Minecraft's deep cheese caves (the big round caverns), every one with its roof 14 to 16 blocks under the grass, below the sea, closed over by solid ground. Minecraft lets the cave noise in only where the ground's own noise says "deep" (on the flat plain 9 to 16 blocks down, cheese caves opening fully a few blocks under that), so they cannot reach the grass; the entrances and noodle caves that could are already filled. Not something a player sees (the user's "deep caves far below are fine"). The check now asks for what it means: air within 8 blocks under the ground (a dip, pit or cave mouth, or a cave met in a shallow dig), still at most 1 spot in 100, and the report also counts closed cave roofs 9 to 16 down (12 of 600). The spot sample is now stable: every map cell in one fixed order per seed, savanna ones read in that order, so a change of the biome map no longer swings which spots are read. `worldgen,mound` 20 of 20 on 6 servers: 0 of 600 spots with air within 8 blocks. Lesson: before filling caves, measure how deep they start; a count of "air within N" needs N to match what a player meets.

182. **Open: the low-mound ribbon march test failed once beside other tests: "6 of 50 looks had more than 3 side by side"** (found 2026-11-07 by the savanna rivers run, `worldgen,mound,colony,raids` on 6 servers, shard 2; the rivers change only world generation, which the raid arenas never use). `araidfromalowdeadmoundmarchesasaribbonofthreesandtwos`: in 6 of 50 looks along the march more than 3 raiders walked abreast (rows are 3 and 2 by turns). Kin to 178 (the same test, the other way: too few row-mates), so maybe the same cause, raiders bunching where the lanes pass something. Not rerun alone yet.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed with the ribbon, as 169.

183. **Open (Minecraft's own): flying fast over new land, the game froze 8 to 13 s when a trial chamber's vault block reached into a chunk not made yet** (found 2026-11-08 by the flight run, `tools/flight.sh`, savanna loading round 2). A vault block's update (`VaultBlockEntity$Server.tick` → `setChanged` → `updateNeighbourForOutputSignal`) reads the block next to it; at the edge of the land being made that block's chunk is wanted but still being built, so the server waits for it. None of our code is in it; the wait is long because the world-makers are busy with the land ahead. It happens in vanilla too (only shorter where the land is quicker to make). **Lesson (the same as our own stalls this round):** Minecraft's "is this chunk loaded" (`hasChunk`, `isLoaded`) says yes while a chunk is still being made; only `LandReady` (the chunk really in memory) is safe to read without freezing. A fix would be a small mixin making the vault's neighbour update skip unmade chunks; not done (Minecraft's code, not ours).

184. **Open: the harvester test `eggsbecomelarvaeandnursesfeedthemintoworkers` failed once beside other tests: "2 larvae are still there"** (found 2026-11-08 by the savanna loading round 2 full run on 6 servers, shard 1, stopped early at the user's ask; not rerun alone yet). The round's only change a running colony could feel is `LandReady` in place of Minecraft's "is this chunk loaded" check, which gives the same answer for every chunk already made (all of a test's arena), so most likely a flake of the larvae's timing; to rerun alone (`ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=eggsbecomelarvae tools/run_game_tests.sh 6`, `ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`) before it is called one.
179. **Open: the harvester larva test found no body, "0 bodies (0 carried), expected 1"** (found 2026-11-07 by the full run on 6 servers for the raid tidy-up, branch `claude/raid-tidy`, shard 5; the branch touches no desert-mod code). `aLarvaThatIsNotFedForTwoDaysStarves`: the unfed larva starved, but no body lay in the refuse list and no worker carried one. Not looked into (the user's rule; the user calls it a flake). Next: run it alone a few times (`ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=alarvathatisnotfed ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`) and log where the body went (dropped and not counted, or folded away).

180. **Open: the harvester seed test's rich-patch check, "nobody laid a scent line home from the rich grama patch and called nestmates out"** (found 2026-11-07 by the same full run, shard 1; no desert-mod code changed). `workersCarrySeedsAndClipGrassIntoTheFoodStore`: 0 scent lines, 0 on their way, 0 called out. Not looked into (the user's rule; the user calls it a flake). Next: run it alone a few times and log whether a forager found the patch at all.


185. **Fixed: the scout came home from its find and no raid left** (the user's raid test, 2026-11-08, world `New World (169)`, with `/antcolonies scout`; branch `claude/scout-command-raid`). Not the command. The scout (a major) followed a termite tunnel it smelt (R6, the mound as a clue) to its end, the feeding place: a dead limb about 20 blocks up a giant baobab. Its walk's points came from `GroundTop`, which counts a tree's wood as ground, so it was sent onto the tree and climbed it. On the trunk under the baobab's bulge (wood 3 blocks over it) the quick "shut in?" look (`Fights.isShutIn`, 40 cells) never reached the open sky a few steps away, so the bite-out reflex (`MatabeleBiteOutGoal`, a higher goal) took its legs for a moment; the scouting goal's `stop()` ended the whole trip ("is back or gave up"), and the scout walked home by its trail with no find told. No find, no raid. Fixes: (1) scouts stand on the ground under limbs and are never sent up a trunk more than 3 blocks of wood tall (`RaidGround.groundCell`); (2) a scout pushed aside for up to 30 s by a higher goal takes its trip up again where it was (`MatabeleScoutGoal` pause and resume); (3) under tree wood the bite-out reflex looks 400 cells wide before it believes it is shut in (`Fights.isShutIn(level, cell, most)`); soil and mud seals are judged as before. Checks added to the first scout test (a limb and a trunk; a scout pushed aside keeps its trip). Side note: the plain `/antcolonies scout` keeps the raid hours (6 to 11, 16 to 19), so a midday find waits till 16:00 anyway; `/antcolonies scout anytime` raids at once. **Lesson:** a goal's `stop()` is also called when a higher goal only borrows the legs for a moment; a long errand (a scout's trip) must not treat that as the end. And "the ground" for a walker on the surface must never be a tree's wood high up. Tests: the mound-ringing scout test (BUGS 153) failed 2 times in 14 on this branch with its old message, 6 of 6 on the code before; no scout was pushed aside in it, so most likely the old flake, watched.

187. **Closed (the ribbon replaced by the stream, 2026-11-08): the two ribbon march tests fail their ribbon checks since the simple march** (2026-11-08, branch `claude/scout-command-raid`; `aRaidFromATallDeadMoundUnrollsBehindItsScoutAndNeverStops`: "too few looks at the ribbon (0 to 3)", `aRaidFromALowDeadMoundMarchesAsARibbonOfThreesAndTwos`: "only 25 to 33 percent of the raiders had a row-mate", once "7 of 11 looks had more than 3 side by side", the tail clumping). `raids,away` otherwise 28 of 30 on 6 servers.
