The front now walks at about 2 blocks a second; the raiders come out of the dead mound's one chimney over about 20 s (the first 10 s none at all: they climb 10 or more blocks up from the rooms) and down sheer walls at about 1.1 blocks a second, so on the test's 34-block trail the column is 27 to 30 blocks long when the front arrives and the tail never closes up into rows. Every other check passes (nobody told to stand, the front never stops and stays in front, no one sent home stuck, personal space kept). Waits for the user's call on the cause (the chimney; see the change log). **Lesson:** time the whole chain before building a fix for one part of it: the scattered way down was built for the hump, and the hold-up was the chimney before it. **Update 2026-11-08:** the raiders come out together (timed raider by raider); the tail was the scout's head start. The scout now waits at the hump's foot: the column 14 to 20 blocks at the end, 14 to 17 looks at the ribbon, 54 to 77 percent with a row-mate, both checks now pass. **Still open:** "more than 3 side by side" in 1 to 6 of 14 to 17 looks (limit 1 in 10), where raiders catching up pass a row in a side lane; the user's call whether that look is wrong or the check too strict. **Update 2026-11-08 (the scout first out, the fast climb):** the scout's wait at the hump's foot removed; the column 12.3 to 13.9 blocks 20 past the foot (a perfect one 11.4), 80 to 92 percent with a row-mate; "more than 3 side by side" now in 2 to 13 of 13 to 17 looks (worse: raiders catching up from the shaft pass rows faster in a side lane). Filmed in the real game (`run-film/raid_march.mp4`): on the flat the column reads as three even lanes; the passing is brief. Still the user's call: is the look wrong, or the check too strict. **Update 2026-11-08 (the stream):** the ribbon is gone (it piled raiders up on one-block steps on real savanna, BUGS 194; the user's OK to replace it); the raiders follow the scout's trail as a stream, side by side left to the legs. The row-mate check went with the rows; "within a block and a half of the trail" stays, now in the two march tests `aRaidFromATallDeadMoundStreamsBehindItsScoutAndNeverStops` and `aRaidFromALowDeadMoundStreamsAlongItsTrail`. "More than 3 side by side" is now counted per raider, not per look: at most 1 raider in 5 may stand in a bunch more than 3 wide (a stream 0.3 blocks apart bunches 4 wide for a moment somewhere in nearly every look, as real columns do, Longhurst and Howse 1979; the old "at most 1 look in 10" failed on 11 to 14 of 14 to 16 looks while the drawn march was a good stream 1 to 3 wide). On the stream 2 to 10 percent of the raiders, in 14 runs.

188. **Open: the low-mound ribbon test can lay its trail through its own mound's dead tree** (found 2026-11-08 by a `raids,away,legs` run, shard 6, branch `claude/scout-command-raid`; `aRaidFromALowDeadMoundMarchesAsARibbonOfThreesAndTwos`: "the column was NaN blocks long 20 blocks past the hump's foot"). The test lays the trail the way farthest from `NewLandMounds.lastDeadTreeForTests()`, the last dead tree placed in the whole level, not its own mound's: on a server running other tests that was another test's tree, 474 blocks off, so the trail could run into this mound's own dead tree. The raiders jammed about 11 blocks out for some 8 s, the "hump's foot" was found at 22 blocks out (the tree, not the hump), and the front never got 20 blocks past it, so the length was never measured. Not a game bug; the next run passed. Not fixed (the user's rule). Next: the test (and `/antcolonies raid demo`) should find its own mound's dead tree, say the dead tree nearest the entrance, or look along each way for anything standing on the ground.

189. **Fixed: the whole-chain raid test's raid now and then came home with no termites** (found 2026-11-08, branch `claude/scout-command-raid`; fixed 2026-11-08, branch `claude/empty-raid-189-001096`). Not the dice and not the rescue: a new fight diary (`fight/FightDiary`, every grab, dodge, sting, foe chosen, breach picked and mud bitten open written as a `[raid]` line) showed the termites dodging about 37 percent of grabs, as designed, and the rescue only starting after the attack. The cause: the test cut the legs off a raider at the spot, and now and then that raider was the major breaking the termites' mud open. A major claims its breach (`MatabeleEntity.breach`) and every other major keeps off covers next to a claimed one; the crippled major kept its claim while it called for help, was carried home and lay in the nest, so both mud casts over the termites stayed claimed and no major ever broke in again (on the next two raids either). In play the same happens whenever a termite soldier bites the breaking major. Fix: a claim counts only while its holder is still fighting at the spot (`holdsBreach()`: a fight spot, not done fighting, not needing rescue); another major takes the breach a tick or two later. The test now always cripples the major holding the breach (18 of 18 pass; with the old rule put back it fails 6 of 6, with the exact old message). Old code: about 2 runs in 30. **Lesson:** a claim on shared work must lapse the moment its holder can no longer do it; and test the case where the worker doing the job is the one that gets hurt.

191. **Fixed: the scout looked at a mud-covered branch or a feeding place up a tree, then walked off and went on searching round the termite nest** (the user's raid test, 2026-11-08, branch `claude/scout-command-raid`). Read in the `[raid]` diary: the scout smelt termites feeding 72 and 83 blocks out and passed both over, "worth 3, a raid that far needs 10.9" and "worth 6, needs 9.8", with the colony 92 percent fed. Two causes. (1) A feeding place fed at by day (termites eating out of sight under mud sheeting, a mud-cased branch, a gallery up a tree) counted as only 2 termites (`raids.choosing.by_day_termites`), worth 6, and a nearly fed colony needs 3 plus 0.1 a block, so every feeding place past 30 blocks was "too poor", while the nearest one is a median 40 blocks out (3F). A real Macrotermes feeding site under sheeting is a rich site that real Megaponera raid over their whole range (Frank and Linsenmair 2017), and our scouts out of sight already valued one 6 to 18. Now 5 termites, worth 15: worth a raid at the whole 96-block reach even fed. (2) A scout that first smelt a termite out in the open beside the place counted only feeding-place cells within 5 blocks of that termite; the place's open cells were a little farther, so it scored the patch worth 3, passed it over and would not smell anything within 10 blocks of it again that trip. Now the look counts every feeding place within those same 10 blocks (twice the smell, the patch it would skip). A check added to the scouts' choosing test: a feeding place's worth must reach a fed colony's need at the whole reach. Raid sizes do not change (a find worth 6 already counted as rich for the column). **Lesson:** a "too poor" rule must be checked against what the land really offers (here the 3F distances), and the area a scout skips after passing a find over must be the same area it counted.

192. **Open: the ridge-and-pond raid test's scent line once ran nearly straight, "the scent line does not bend round the pond: at most 2.9 blocks off the straight way"** (found 2026-11-08 by a `raids` run on 6 servers, shard 4, branch `claude/scout-command-raid`, with the BUGS 191 fix). `aScoutsTrailOverARidgeAndRoundAPondLeadsTheRaidThereAndHome`: the line had only 11 points over about 14 blocks, so the scout's run home was cut short or started near the pond. Alone 6 of 6 right after (lines 4.1 to 6.7 blocks off the straight way). Not from BUGS 191 (the test's termites are loose under logs, no termite colony's feeding places). Not looked into (the user's rule). Next: log the scout's whole run home when the line comes out short.

193. **Fixed: a raid could take as its leader a scout that had just set off on a new trip, and then never move** (found 2026-11-08 by the new lumpy-ground raid test, built from the user's 19:43 report; branch `claude/scout-command-raid`). The colony brain sent the find's own scout out on a fresh search one tick before the raid to that find set out. `Raids.startRaid` let the find's scout through even while it was counted among the scouts out searching (the rule was made for the scout coming home with its find, which is still counted out until its trip ends). So it was made the leader while its scouting goal held its legs: "called to the raid but is not walking with it a second later: it runs [MatabeleScoutGoal]". The front stayed 0.0 blocks along the trail, and 47 raiders stood at the trail's start until the march's time limit, 335 s. In play this can happen to a find that waited for the raid hours (its scout out searching again when they begin) or to the raid command. Now only the scout just home with this find may lead while still counted out (`startRaid`'s `homeFrom`); a find's scout out on a new trip stays at it, and the major nearest the entrance leads by the scent trail. **Lesson:** "this ant may lead" must ask what the ant is doing now, not only who it is.

194. **Closed (the ribbon's side lanes, gone with the stream; the legs are fine): an insect sent to a place up a one-block step, diagonally beside a one-block hole, can cut the corner into the hole, push at the step's side for a few seconds and try again** (found 2026-11-08 in the black box of the user's 19:43 raid, branch `claude/scout-command-raid`). In the ribbon march 15 raiders piled up at one grass bump at 2423.5, 69, -1378.1 this way (76 still records there, smaller piles at about six other bumps), while the front went on about 50 blocks. The ribbon is gone (the stream walks the scout's own trail, known to be walkable), and the lumpy-ground replay test (`aRaidMarchesOverTheUsersLumpySavannaWithoutPilingUp`, the same ground) now shows no raider stuck, so the march no longer meets it; but the legs (`AntNavigator`, `AntPathFinder`) were not changed, so any insect given a place like that may still do it. Not reproduced on its own yet. **Lesson:** a test on flat ground proves nothing about the legs on real blocky land; a black box from the user's own world, made into a test place, is what showed it. **Update 2026-11-08 (looked into while the user was away):** reproduced on its own and the cause found. The ground round the pile-up, copied block for block from the report as heights, is a diagonal staircase of one-block grass steps (low to the north-west), not a hole; the diary shows each stuck raider on the step's top, sent diagonally along its edge, sliding backward off it, away from where it was going, into the low corner below, then pushing at the step. New legs test `insectsWalkTheRaidsStaircaseOfGrassStepsWithoutSlidingOff` (`MatabeleTests`): a lone longest major, a lone shortest minor and a lone termite worker walk the raiders' moves (from the low corner up onto the step, along its edge, down the trail's slope and back up), and eight majors walk the trail down and back as a crowd; nobody may stand still 5 s, every walk must end where it was sent. Today's legs: 6 of 6 on 6 servers. With the ribbon's side lanes put back for a moment (`setSideShift`, the old navigator), a lane pushed north or west fails it the way the raid did: the minor stood still 5 s at the step's foot, the termite ended on the wrong block. So the side lane was the cause: it was a fixed offset from each cell's middle, up to 0.45 blocks, kept on diagonal steps too, which put the aim over the step's edge. The stream removed it; nothing in the legs needed changing; the test stays as a guard. **Lesson:** a lane offset the legs did not choose must never move the aim off a step, so a mind giving lanes again must leave steps and diagonals to the legs.

195. **Fixed (test only): the lumpy-ground replay test failed after other tests on the same server, "no raid set out: nobody fit to raid"** (found 2026-11-08 by `raids,away,legs` runs on 6 servers, branch `claude/scout-command-raid`; 5 of 5 servers that ran it after the two march tests, alone 1 of 1). The test put the user's savanna back (`watch/Replay.put`), moved the colony's ants into their rooms and called the raid 2 ticks later. The ants were alive and in the colony but hidden from the world, and the nest's chunk was held and fully loaded by the chunk system but its creatures neither loaded nor running. The game hands a chunk to its creature store a little after the chunk loads, as part of its own chunk work, and a test server at tick rate 2000 gets only a sliver of each tick for that work. After the march tests left some behind, it took 12 ticks, not the 2 or fewer it takes on a fresh server; an ant moved meanwhile into a chunk not yet running dropped out of sight. Now `Replay.running` says when every chunk of the place has its creatures loaded and running, and the test waits for it (at most 2400 ticks) before it touches the ants: 3 of 3 after the march tests on one server. `ReplayRun` (the `replays` category) still starts each run's clock right after the put; its runs last minutes, so a few ticks matter little, but it could wait the same way. **Lesson:** a place put back by code is not running the moment the code returns; wait until the game says its creatures run, and test a new test after others on the same server, not only alone.

197. **Open: the whole-chain raid test now and then finds a trail point still broken after the column crossed** (found 2026-11-08 by `raids,soldiers,colony,away` on 6 servers and by single-test reruns, branch `claude/empty-raid-189-001096`; about 1 run in 20; message "1 trail point(s) still broken after the column crossed"). In one log the trail broke a second time at tick 1333, on the dead mound's hump near the entrance, after the column had already passed the test's own break; likely the hump's wear (`MoundWear`) changing a block on the trail, not the march. Not looked into further (the user's rule).
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** stays open: not the raid's doing (a second break on the mound's hump, likely `MoundWear`), and nothing in the rewrite touches it.

198. **Open: the game crashes when a raid starts on a scent trail of one point** (found 2026-11-10 by a full run on 6 servers, branch `claude/far-colonies-speed-022804`: test server 6 stopped with "Ticking entity", `IndexOutOfBoundsException: toIndex = 2` in `RaidColumn.<init>` (`RaidColumn.java:447`, `trail.subList(0, Math.max(1, gather) + 1)`), called from `Raids.startRaid` when a scout walked home with a find; once in one run, the 15 tests that server had not reached all passed when run again). Read in the code, not tried: the raid's line is the scout's trail with every point near the nest taken off and the start put in front; when the find lies so near the nest that every point is taken off, the line is the start alone, and the "at least two points" cut reaches past its end. A real crash in play is possible (a feeding spot right beside a Matabele nest). Not this branch's work (the raid code is untouched here). **Not fixed: for its own session.** The fix is likely one line (the cut never past the line's end, and a one-point line needing no march), with a test of a find beside the nest.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed with 158 (see its line).
200. **Fixed 2026-11-10: Matabele scouts on a tall dead mound walked up and down the hump, turning back at the same height every time** (the user's report, an old bug reported before and not fixed; their report `run/reports/2026-11-10_05-27-44-player`, colony 1cc3d94a, nest -1428, 84, 532; branch `claude/far-colonies-speed-022804`). The `[raid]` diary said each scout "starts searching 4 (6) blocks out ... (no way farther out: it searches where it is)", and its winding search then stayed on the hump. Cause: a scout's step had to land on ground within 12 blocks of the height of the nest's middle (`MatabeleScoutGoal.openGround`, a rule from the first scouts, 3a, with no reason written). A dead mound's middle is its chimney top: on a tall one, 84, the land round it at 71 was 13 below, so every cell off the hump was refused and the scout could only walk the hump down to y 72 and back up. Every scout test stood on mounds less than 12 over their land, so none showed it. **Fix:** the height rule removed (fewer rules: the raiders' own stand check and the route planner already judge cliffs and water). New test `aScoutFromATallDeadMoundGetsOffTheHumpOntoTheLand` (a 6-day dead mound on a gentle 4-block rise, 13 over the land, like the report): failed 3 of 3 on the old code (the scout got 14 blocks out), passes 3 of 3 with the fix; `raids,away` 32 of 32 on 6 servers. **Lesson:** a height measured from the nest's middle is wrong for nests on mounds: the middle of a dead mound's colony is its chimney top, many blocks over the land.

201. **Fixed 2026-11-11: a Matabele scout home in a tall dead mound never told of its find, "never got home to tell of its find: the find is lost"** (found by the 8-day soak of 2026-11-11, server 2, nest 12120, 85, 691, branch `claude/brain-away-raids`: 19 finds lost in 8 days, the colony of 95 got two raids and starved). A scout counted as home only within 3 blocks of the height of the nest's middle, which on a tall dead mound is the chimney top (y 85); the scouts walked into the rooms (y 68) and the trip ended untold. Now a scout inside the nest is home (`Nest.isInside` in `MatabeleScoutGoal.walkHome`). Lesson (BUGS 87 again, the other way round): "home" is the whole nest, not a height near its middle.

202. **Open: a raid marches to a feeding place where no termite is, and comes home empty** (found by the same soak, nest 12186, 77, 695: 5 raids of 25 to 30 real ants, all "0 termite(s) carried"; diary "no termite about the spot for 30 s: the attack is over"). The scout scored the spot 15 (three feeding places at 5 each, BUGS 191's rule, no termite seen); the termite colony 60 blocks off, 221 strong, had no real termite at that place when the raid came, and with honest sizes no raider goes as a number any more, so nothing is caught under the mud either (`RaidCatch`'s hidden catch was all a raid of numbers brought). The spot is not marked empty, so the scouts send the next raid there too. To plan: a termite colony's numbers feeding at a place should stand there as real termites when a raid comes (a counted termite is one termite), or the scout should only count feeding places with termites at them.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** half closed. A raid to a spot the termites keep off, or have left, cannot set out any more (168's line). Still possible: the termite colony feeds there under the mud as numbers, so the judge says yes (`Raid.termitesThere`, the feeding places under cover), and a watched raid of real ants finds nobody to sting and comes home empty (only raiders going as numbers take termites from under the mud, `Raid.takeHidden`); the find then counts one empty check and is forgotten after two (`Raids.raidHome`), so at most two such raids, not five. What would prove it gone: step 6's soak showing no watched raid home empty from a spot the judge called "under cover"; if it shows them, the fix is on the termites' side (who comes out at a break), as step 4's notes say.

203. **Fixed 2026-11-11: a termite colony far from every player never foraged, ate its comb in three days and starved, queen and king too** (found by the soaks of 2026-11-11, `soak-others.txt`: every far termite colony, "137 starved" on day 5 with 864 known plants and not one piece of food brought in; the 100-day soak of 2026-11-06 shows the same on the old code). The forager numbers go to the plants the brain remembers (`FieldLife.known`), and the brain only looked at the plants while the colony was "here"; a colony new land puts in far from a player is never here. Now the colony takes that look as it goes away with its land loaded (`FieldLife.lookOnLeaving`, beside BUGS 146's look at the queen and garden), picks up known plants whenever their land is loaded (`lookAway`), and where it still finds no plant to work it forages by the land (`forageByTheLand`: no hole is laid out for a colony nobody has been near, so remembered plants alone did not save it). Lesson: everything the numbers need to know must be learned in the last look on leaving, not only while near.

204. **Fixed 2026-11-11: a Matabele colony far from every player found no termites and starved in four days** (the same soaks: far colonies with no raid and no find at all). The number scouts picked from the termites' recorded tunnels and runways to food, which are only planned near a player. Now a termite colony with none on record offers the dead wood it knows, one place per 8-block square (`RaidsAway.scout`).

205. **Fixed 2026-11-12: Matabele walked into the feeding galleries without breaking any mud** (the user's report: "the ants go in most of the time they dont have to break a mud caste because the branches dont get fully covered"; branch `claude/termite-alarm-rework`). A fallen branch was cased in mud only on the block right over a dug gallery cell (`NestWalls.casingGaps`), and the next block of the branch counted as "closed" though it stood open to the air: the uncovered end of a branch (no gallery dug under it yet, or none possible there) was a door into the covered part and the gallery. Fix: the gaps are looked for round the whole branch (every block of walk-through food joined to the one over the gallery, up to 12), each with the side its plate goes on, so the whole branch is plastered as the first gallery block under it is dug; the same list is what "closed round" (fed from by day) and the leaks read. Test: `aFallenBranchOnTheEdgeOfAStepIsCasedInMudAndEatenFromBelowByDay` now checks every block of the branch from the moment a gallery block under it is dug; it failed 3 of 3 on the old code. Known limit: a plate knocked off a far end of a branch (no gallery cell within a block of it) is not mended by the sealers; the gallery is then not fed from by day until it is. **Lesson:** a check that asks "is my neighbour one of ours?" must also ask whether that neighbour is closed; and the old test only looked where the code looked.
206. **Open: the harvester numbers test failed once beside others, "a tuft the brain never saw should not be foraged while away: cut 0"** (found 2026-11-12 by the run for BUGS 205, `termitefood,away,raids,soldiers,hills` on 6 servers, shard 5: `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember`; passed alone 3 of 3; a harvester test, nothing of the change touches it; the message names a count of 0, so the check that failed may be another than its words say). Not looked into.
207. **Open: the two chicken alarm tests fail often, the chicken sunk deep under the hay** (found 2026-11-12 by the runs for the alarm rework's step 5, branch `claude/termite-alarm-rework`). `minorsBiteAChickenThatHuntsTheForagersWhoHurryHome` ("no minor has bitten the chicken") failed alone 3 of 6 on the commit before step 5's second part (8a10a7e7) and 5 of 6 with it; `theMiddleHoleIsPluggedSoonAfterAChickenAlarm` (the old flake BUGS 22) failed 2 of 3 alone with it ("no forager reached the bush while a minor was outside", "the workers where the foe was felt took 653 ticks to get in"); not measured on the earlier commit. Seen in the logs: the chicken, put on top of the hay, is 6 to 12 blocks under the ground some hundred ticks later, losing health, and the foe trouble follows it down, so no minor reaches it. Not known: what takes the chicken down (a guess, not checked: the gallery dug to the hay under it), and since which step. Not looked into further.
    - **Looked into 2026-11-25 (overnight flaky-test session 4, branch `claude/flaky-fixes`), diagnose only (soldier fighting is the user's call):** alone 2 of 6 failed (then 1 of 3 and 3 of 3 in the reruns, so REAL). Both failures were this entry's first finding, not the triage's "kept place" reading: the chicken, hurt and panicking, **dropped down an open foraging hole into the galleries** (the test opens every hole): in one it sat 3 blocks under the ground in a tunnel about 8.5 blocks from the three minors, who stood at nest cells for 7,000 ticks without reaching it ("no minor has bitten"); in the other it fell 12 blocks, below the nest, after one bite (3.0 health), and the alarm faded to calm with it alive ("the chicken is still alive"). **What real Macrotermes do:** a foraging hole is a few millimetres wide (a tenth of a block at the mod's scale), so a chicken can never get into the galleries; the workers run in, the soldiers stand in the openings jaws out and bite what pokes at them, and a bird pecking at a hole is bitten on the beak and face and usually gives up (blind soldiers do not chase). **Recommendation (one question for the user):** make the open foraging holes too small for any animal bigger than an insect to fall into (a chicken, a player's pet, a hyrax walks over them as over a crack), so the fight stays at the hole as in real life. The other ideas (soldiers lunging farther at a vertebrate, places handed out again when the foe moves) make the blind soldiers hunt, which they do not do. Not built.
    - **2026-11-25, the user's call:** `minorsBiteAChickenThatHuntsTheForagersWhoHurryHome` deleted ("I'm gonna remake that code at some point or just might delete it"); the other chicken test, `theMiddleHoleIsPluggedSoonAfterAChickenAlarm`, is kept. The recommendation above (open foraging holes too small for anything bigger than an insect to fall into) waits for that remake. This entry stays open for the kept test.

The kept test is now `foragersHurryInThroughAYoungMoundsOpenShaftAtAFoe`, with a lone Matabele major standing still at the bush (no mind of its own: an enemy at the foragers) in place of the chicken. It keeps the old flake with the same two messages: 5 failures in 16 runs (2 of 6 alone, 1 of 3 and 1 of 3 in reruns, once beside the other soldier tests and 1 of 3 in its rerun): "no foe yet: no forager reached the bush while a minor was outside" and "the workers where the foe was felt took 702 / 858 ticks to get in, more than 30 seconds" (the chicken version: 2 of 3 alone, the same two messages). So the chicken sinking under the hay was not its only cause. Not looked into further (the rule: bugs found by test runs go on the list). The danger memory test (`holesTakeTurnsNearFoodFirstAndMoreInTheRain`, BUGS 65's chicken kept only as the attacker whose damage the test applies by hand) passed 6 of 6 with the body now left.

208. **Fixed 2026-11-13: a third of new land's dead mounds could never hold Matabele** (the user's report: only 3 nests found; branch `claude/termite-alarm-rework`). The user's log: 18 dead mounds, 11 with a colony, 6 "no way in", every one of the 6 dead under 3 days (0.3 to 1.5), every one with a colony dead 3.0 or more. A dead mound's chimney stays capped until its tips crumble (day 3, `MoundWear.PIECES_TO`), the colony is put in once, when the mound is, and the wear mix had been retuned to 30 in 100 freshly dead (2026-11-07) so mounds look dead: two rules meeting. The survey test counted the Matabele dice only, never the way in, so it kept passing. Fix: a freshly dead mound whose dice give it Matabele has been dead 3.1 to 4.9 days (`TermiteMoundStructure.pick`): just open, spires still up. The survey now fails on a Matabele mound dead under 3 days. Lesson: a survey must count what the game really does, not the dice before it; and a "no" with three causes behind one log line hides which rule said it.
209. **Fixed 2026-11-13: the spawn landed in a small savanna** (the user's report, "the biomes are broken"; branch `claude/termite-alarm-rework`). The log: spawn moved into `minecraft:windswept_savanna`, "about 32 blocks in from the savanna's edge". The biomes were not broken: `SavannaSpawn` went to the nearest savanna of any size, and the nearest was one of Minecraft's own small ones (its zones, about 1 in 4, keep Minecraft's table). The world test accepted any spawn 96 blocks in. Fix: the spawn goes to the nearest place with savanna all round out to 500 blocks (`SavannaSpawn.bigSavanna`, a 128 grid out to the search radius), the old rule only when there is none; the world test fails when a seed's spawn is in no big savanna. Lesson: "nearest" and "big" are different questions; the test checked the rule as written, not what the user wanted from it.
  (209, more: the first try found no big savanna on any seed, as a savanna's own river biome counted as its edge; `SavannaSpawn.savannaAt` now counts `savanna_river` as inside. On the 4 test seeds the spawn is 931 to 3,764 blocks from Minecraft's own.)
210. **Fixed 2026-11-13 (not tried in-game yet): termite major soldiers did not attack the Matabele** (the user's in-game test of the alarm rework: "the major soldier werent attacking the matabele ants", everything else worked; branch `claude/termite-alarm-rework`). Cause, read from the code (fairly sure, not proved in-game): against insects the minors took the places nearest the opening and the majors the ones behind them (`Alarm.places`), and a soldier at its place bit only what touched its own box (`DefendGoal`), while the raiders keep clear of a soldier that is a wall; so a major deep in the gallery never met an ant. Fix: against insects the majors take the places nearest the opening (the big heads are the plug, as in real Macrotermes), minors behind and beside; and a soldier standing at its place snaps at an insect within 1 block of it without leaving its cover, once a second (`DefendGoal.snapsAt`, `SNAP`, my picks). Measured on the measuring scene, 18 raids of 20 on a full colony, the 8 that broke in: ants dead 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 3, 5, 7, hurt 1 to 9; soldiers lost 1 to 8 (mostly clamped on to a leg and dragged out); workers stung 4 to 7 (one raid 28). The user wants the war feeling ("its okay if more ants get injured and die"), so the old aim of 0 to 2 ants dead no longer binds. Run `soldiers,raids,away` on 6 servers: 5 failures, none from the fix: BUGS 97, 207, 153 (old), 211 and 212 (below). Lesson: when a rule makes a body untouchable at its place, check that the body can still do its own job from there.
211. **Open: the gallery seal test flakes, "the soldiers by the break should be shut out by the seal"** (found 2026-11-13 by the run for BUGS 210, `soldiers,raids,away` on 6 servers; `aRaidBreakingIntoAGalleryIsSealedBehindTheSoldiers`). Alone on every server: 1 of 9 on the commit before the BUGS 210 fix (9cb5fdfc), 3 of 13 with it, so an older flake, likely since the alarm rework's steps 4 and 5 moved the soldiers' places. `RaidDefence.shutOut` counts a soldier as shut out only if it stands nearer the break than the plug (plus half a block) at the moment the seal is packed; one of the test's two minors is probably standing at a place just behind the plug's cell then. Not looked into further: what place each soldier holds when the seal goes in.
    - **Looked into 2026-11-25 (overnight flaky-test session 4, branch `claude/flaky-fixes`), cause found, not fixed (the user's call):** alone 2 of 18 failed (3 launches of 6). The test's message now gives each soldier's place, distance, alarm task and running goals when the seal goes in. Both failures: the soldier `beside` (put one cell from the break) had **no alarm task** and was walking its beat (`WalkBeatGoal`) 13.5 and 14.1 blocks back inside the nest, so the seal shut it in, not out. Why: it is put down idle, starts its beat walk inward at once, and the drumming spreads from the break at only `drum_speed` 1.3 blocks a second (`Alarm.reached`), about as fast as a soldier walks; so it stays just ahead of the wave. In a pass (traced tick by tick) the same soldier walked 6 blocks in before the wave caught it at tick 136; the seal goes in near tick 230, when the wave has spread 13.45 blocks, and both failing soldiers stood just past that. **Not the triage's guess** (a ring place past the plug): the tunnel was straight and the ring places all lay outside the plug. **The deeper cause is a unit slip:** DESIGN says the drumming is relayed at about 1.3 *metres* a second (Hager and Kirchner 2013), but at the mod's scale (1 block = 30 mm, real sizes 2026-11-05) that is about 43 blocks a second, so in a real colony every soldier within the 16-block reach would feel it in under half a second, and a soldier 1 block (3 cm) from a drumming nestmate feels it at once. Fix options (not built, it changes how fast every soldier answers an alarm, the user's call): (a) `drum_speed` to the real scale (about 43; the alarm then reaches the whole 16 blocks almost at once, no visible spreading); (b) keep the visible spread but let a soldier feel a drumming nestmate within a block or two at once (the real relay, soldier to soldier). Not the new raid code: the scene has no raid machine, only the termites' seal.
    - **Fixed 2026-11-25 (the user's call, option (a), branch `claude/flaky-fixes`):** the drumming now runs at the real speed, 43.3 blocks a second (1.3 m a second at 1 block = 30 mm; `drum_speed` in `macrotermes.json`, the code's fallback and the unit test), so the soldier beside the break feels it within a tick or two and takes its place before its beat walk carries it in. Alone 18 of 18 passed (3 launches of 6; 2 of 18 failed the night before), and every `soldiers` test passed in the `soldiers,raids` run. Lesson: a real speed in metres must be turned into blocks at the mod's scale (1 block = 30 mm), never copied as blocks.

The raid did carry termites home; the test's hook, which kills one carrier while the column is homing, found no carrier of this column out at that stage (maybe the carriers were home before the stage turned to homing). 213. **Fixed 2026-11-13 (not tried in-game yet): the mud plate over a fallen branch, once bitten open by a raid, was never put back** (the user, 2026-11-13: mud casing not all round the branch, then "make sure they repair it after a raid ... if they just keep it open the matabele will always go there"; branch `claude/termite-alarm-rework`). The user's own save (New World 184), read block by block: every branch over a gallery closed on all sides but three, all at the spot raided two minutes before the save. A gap in a branch's casing is a leak the builders close (`NestWalls.getLeaks`, `SealGoal`), but a leak is worked on from a nest cell within one block of it (`plugSpots`, `findPlugSpot`), and the plate over a branch lies two blocks over the gallery, so it had no place to work from: side plates were repaired within minutes, the top plate never (the new test: still open two game days after the raid, counted as a leak all that time). Fix: a hole is also worked on from round the walk-through food beside it (`NestWalls.workedFrom`): the termite stands in the gallery under the branch and plasters through the branch it eats its way into. No new rule or number. Test: the branch test (`aFallenBranchOnTheEdgeOfAStepIsCasedInMudAndEatenFromBelowByDay`) now ends with a raid: a Matabele major bites the top plate and the end plate open, a worker is hurt, the raider leaves, and the casing must be whole again within two game days with the branch still there; 6 of 6, whole again 10 to 17 seconds after the raid. Not checked: a colony living as numbers (no player within 64 blocks) repairs nothing until a player comes near; and whether workers re-plaster a break while the raiders still stand at it (in a real raid the workers there go deeper and the gallery is sealed behind them, so probably not). Run `termitefood,away,raids,soldiers,mound` on 6 servers: 4 failures, all on the list (BUGS 97, 206, 207, 211; 206, the harvester numbers test, failed alone 2 of 3 this time, more than before, a harvester test this change does not reach). Lesson: a repair rule that looks one block round the hole misses holes the insects reach through something they can walk into.
214. **Fixed 2026-11-14 (not tried in-game yet): a 1.3-second freeze when a new-land mound's dead tree went in** (found in the user's own log while looking into a frame-rate question, `[stall] a server tick took 1349 ms`, `NewLandMounds.deadTree` -> `Mound.getParts` -> `MoundGrowth.grow`; once in that session; on `master`). The tree's place is measured from the living mound's own foot, which needs the mound's shape. The shape is grown in the background before the mound goes in, but only the last 24 shapes are kept (`MoundPlan.KEPT`), and the tree goes in later, once more land round it is loaded: flying over new land, more than 24 other mounds can be grown in between, the shape is dropped, and it was grown again on the spot, on the server's own thread. Fix: the waiting tree asks for the shape first (`MoundPlan.growthSoon` in `NewLandMounds.tick`); if it is gone it is grown in the background and the tree waits for a later look, as the mound itself already did. No new rule or number. `mound` on 6 servers: every test passed but one flake, passed alone 3 of 3 (BUGS 215). Not measured in a flight run (the freeze was rare). **Lesson:** "it was ready when we checked" does not hold for work put off to a later tick when the thing checked lives in a small keep-the-last-few store; check again where it is used.
215. **Open: the young cathedral test failed once beside others, "stage 5 is not finished: {carve=3, piece=10, reshape=9, soil=3}"** (found 2026-11-14 by the `mound` run for BUGS 214 on 6 servers, on `master`: `termitesBuildTheYoungCathedralFromInside`, 213 s; passed alone 3 of 3; the BUGS 214 change only touches when a new-land mound's dead tree goes in, which this test has none of). Not looked into.
216. **Fixed 2026-11-14 (not tried in-game yet): after the catfish struck at them, the tilapia huddled in a corner and stood still** (the user's report with a picture, watching in spectator mode). Not the catfish: Minecraft lets a mob wander (`RandomStrollGoal`, the fish's `FishSwimGoal`) only while its idle clock (`noActionTime`) is under 100 ticks, and winds the clock back only while a player who is **not a spectator** is within 32 blocks (`Mob.checkDespawn` asks `getNearestPlayer` with spectators left out). With only a spectator near, the school's leader stopped wandering 5 s after anything last happened and its followers sat beside it; the strikes' scatter had just pushed them into the corner. Fix: tilapia and catfish answer `getNoActionTime()` with 0, so they always wander (only the wandering reads it; despawning reads the real clock). New test `theSchoolSwimsOnAfterAHunt` (`river`): wild fish (made by hand, as `helper.spawn` marks a mob kept for ever, which also winds the clock back every tick, why the old tests never saw it), a school of 8 and a hungry catfish; 0 of 6 before, 6 of 6 after. **Lesson:** a game test's spawned mobs are kept for ever, so they never show what happens to wild mobs nobody counts as near; and Minecraft never counts a spectator as near, the same gap `colony/Watchers` closes for our colonies.
217. **Open: the catfish hunting test failed once beside others, "held in the mouth for 0 ticks"** (found 2026-11-15 by the `river` run for the crocodile's fish hunting on 6 servers, on `master`: `ahungrycatfishhuntsandswallowsatilapia`; passed alone 3 of 3, and the next full `river` run passed 7 of 7). Its server ran no crocodile test, so not caused by the crocodile's fish hunting. Not looked into; a guess, not a fact: the tilapia was gulped down or removed between two of the test's checks.
218. **Fixed 2026-11-15 (not tried in-game yet): crocodiles never spawned by themselves** (the user's report after the crocodile's own spawning group, "the crocodiles arent spawning in naturally"; on `master`). The crocodile is built on Minecraft's land-mob base (`PathfinderMob`), whose last check on a natural spawn, `Mob.checkSpawnObstruction`, refuses any spot with liquid in the mob's box; Minecraft's water animals (the tilapia and catfish among them) replace it with "only blocks and mobs in the way". So every natural spawn in the river, the only place the crocodile may spawn, was thrown out at the last step, since the crocodile was added; eggs and `/summon` skip that check, so it never showed in tests. The earlier explanation (the tilapia filling the shared fish group, 2026-11-15) was at most half of it. Fix: `CrocodileEntity.checkSpawnObstruction` answers `level.isUnobstructed(this)`, as a water animal's. Test: `aCrocodileClimbsOutToBaskAndGoesBackIn` now runs the world's own last checks (`checkSpawnRules` with `NATURAL`, `checkSpawnObstruction`) on a crocodile in the pond's deep water; failed with the old check, passes with the fix; `river` 8 of 8 on 6 servers. **Lesson:** a test that spawns with `helper.spawn` or an egg skips the world's spawn checks; a mob that should spawn somewhere unusual (water for a non-fish) needs those checks run in a test, and its base class's checks read.
219. **Open: the catfish hunting test failed once, "the catfish died"** (found 2026-11-15 by the `river` run for the remains on 6 servers, on `master`: `ahungrycatfishhuntsandswallowsatilapia`, alone on its server; passed alone 3 of 3). Its server ran no crocodile test, so not caused by the remains or the tear. Not looked into; a guess, not a fact: the catfish was stranded or suffocated in the test pond (perhaps the same root as 217).
220. **Open: the catfish hunting test failed once, "no scraps on the bed after the meal"** (found 2026-11-15 by the `river` run for the crab, on `master`: `ahungrycatfishhuntsandswallowsatilapia`; alone it failed 1 run of 3, then the next `river` run passed 9 of 9). The crab test runs in its own arena and the crab only takes scraps it reaches, so not caused by the crab (a guess, not checked: the scraps film may land outside the cells the test looks at when the catch is made near the pond's edge, perhaps with 217 and 219). Not looked into.
222. **Fixed 2026-11-16 (not tried in-game yet): every ground height the land worked out was kept in memory for good** (found by big savannas step 1, branch `claude/big-rivers`: the river width measure ran the test server out of memory, alone too, on a trial land with wide rivers). Before Minecraft works out a height for land not made yet it wires its land recipe afresh and throws the wiring away after. Our `antcolonies:shared` wrapper (savanna loading round 2, 2026-11-08, `FlatSavanna.Shared`) remembered the last wiring in a per-thread memory of its own, and the wired copies made their own such memories too; a thread's memory of a copy held the copy's own wiring, so Java could never let either go (a classic Java leak: a thread-local whose value reaches its own key). Measured with Java's heap count, not guessed: 5 minutes into the measure about 2.2 million kept memories and some 10 GB of wiring pieces, growing. In play the same path runs whenever the trees and mounds of new land ask for ground heights, so a long session flying over new land grew the game's memory without end (not measured in-game; the user had not reported it). My first guess, the slow-tick watcher keeping call pictures without limit, was wrong as the cause but real: it is capped too (`StallWatch.MAX_LOOKS`, 10,000 a tick, kept at random so the report still covers the whole tick). Fix: one per-thread memory for all the wrapper's parts, the wiring it is in and each part's answer, cleared when a new wiring starts (`Shared.WIRING`): at most one wiring per thread held; the same wired parts, so the same land. **Lessons:** a cache keyed per object must not live in a per-object `ThreadLocal` whose value points back at the object; and a test that runs long is the cheapest leak finder there is: when one runs out of memory, count the heap (`jcmd <pid> GC.class_histogram`) before guessing.
224. **Fixed 2026-11-16 (not tried in-game yet): crabs and snails never spawned by themselves** (the parent session's ask: both had only been tried with spawn eggs; branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`). Measured with the world's own spawn checks on a channel 1 to 7 deep built at sea level, not guessed. Two causes. (1) They shared the river fish group (cap 60 round a player) with the tilapia, which are kept within 96 blocks and fill it for good (the same finding as the crocodile's, BUGS 218), and by the group's weights the tilapia took about 9 picks in 10. (2) Minecraft's natural spawner draws one random height for a try in a column and keeps it for the whole group; a crab or snail could spawn only at the one water block just over the bed, so it passed 64 spots of the channel against the tilapia's 336, and its group spawned one at a time (the next spot's bed lies at another height). Fix: their own spawning group `antcolonies:river_bed` (cap 24, removed past 128 blocks like the fish; `SavannaMobCategories.riverBed`); any water spot over a bed at most 4 blocks down counts, and a spawn by the world is set down on the bed under where it was drawn (`animal/RiverBed`, one rule for both); crab weight 1 in ones, snail weight 3 in twos to fours (my picks); the quiet-savanna gate unchanged (the placements already wrapped in it). After: 160 spots each (8, 16, 24, 32 for beds 1 to 4 deep, none deeper). Test `crabsAndSnailsSpawnOnShallowBedsByThemselves` (`river`). **Lesson:** a bed or floor dweller must be judged against the spawner's single random height per try, and a group whose mobs are kept near fills its cap for good, so a rarer kind needs its own group.

The catfish bit the tilapia in half but no fresh tilapia half was found by it. The same test as BUGS 217, 219 and 220, each failing with another message; BUGS 224 only moved the crab and snail out of the fish group, and this test spawns neither, so not caused by it (not checked on the old code). 227. **Open: the tilapia breeding test failed once beside others, "stuck at stage 1"** (found 2026-11-19 by the `life,river` runs for animal life cycle step 2, branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`: `tilapiabreedinanestpitandthemotherkeepsherfryinhermouth`; passed alone 3 of 3, then four whole `life,river` runs passed). Lessons already learned on the way: Minecraft's "go to" ends a block short (now `GoExactly`), and a stand-in player left in the world by another test (`makeMockServerPlayerInLevel`) turns on the spawning group caps and can fill "room to breed" (the remains test now removes its player; `TermiteAlarmTests` still leaves two, a leak for a full run).
   - **2026-11-25 (flaky-fixes session 3), partly fixed:** kinds 1 and 2 ("players 3, room false"; "a player beside her and her fry not taken into her mouth", the fry despawned) by the shared cause, stand-in players left in the world, fixed at its source in `TestIsolation` (`letStandInPlayersGo`): each stand-in player goes once every test that was running when it came in is done. Found on the way: every passing test using `MatabeleDefenceTests.survivalPlayer` (the shark, Matabele and river tests) left its player behind, not only failing ones; any player in the level makes Minecraft despawn far-off young that are not kept (baby crabs off their mother's back, hatchlings, fry) and turns on the spawning caps of room to breed. Stage 3 now asserts her fry are still there, so a despawn shows as itself. **Kind 3 still open** ("stuck at stage 1", seen again once in 4 `life,river` runs): measured, the female paired 2.6 blocks off and one block above the male down in his dug pit, her `SeekMateGoal` path never finishing (fnav done false, path length 4). Tried once: steering straight in with the move control within touch + 2 blocks, in the shared `SeekMateGoal`; it broke crocodile, bullfrog and tilapia courting even alone (their bodies do not come in on the move control alone), so it was taken back. Next idea, not tried: let the pit-holding male rise to meet her, or end her path at the pit's rim and court from there (a tilapia's own fix, not the shared goal's).
228. **Open: the catfish hunting test failed again, twice in one day** (found 2026-11-19 by the `life,river` runs for animal life cycle step 2, branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`: `ahungrycatfishhuntsandswallowsatilapia`, once "bitten off, 0 remains by the catfish" (failed alone 2 of 3) and once "held in the mouth for 0 ticks" (failed alone 1 of 3); the next four runs passed). The same test and messages as BUGS 217, 220 and 225. Not caused by step 2 as far as can be told: the test's fish are grown, with no nest, eggs or fry, so none of the breeding code acts in it. 230. **Fixed: a Nile monitor got stuck in the river from time to time, floating at the surface** (the user's report 2026-11-19, `run/reports/2026-11-19_03-55-18-player`, branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`). The report's place file showed it: the monitor hungry, afloat in water 4 deep, a snail on the bed 3.4 blocks under it and 1.9 across. The hunt's last steps only pushed it sideways (made for land) and the float rule held it at the surface, so its jaws never got down to the snail; after 20 s the hunt gave up and about a second later picked the same snail again, for ever (rare because it needs a hungry monitor in deep water over a snail or a sunk crab). Fix: swimming, the hunt steers its jaws onto the prey in all three directions (it dives to the bed), and the float rule stands aside while a hunt is on; a hunt that caught nothing rests a minute, as the catfish's does, so no unreachable prey can hold it again; the 20 s counts only the chase, not biting and eating. Test `aHungryMonitorDivesForASnailOnTheBed` (the report's scene; failed on the old code, passes now). Lesson: a goal that gives up must not be able to pick the same target again at once, or giving up is no way out; and the land hunt test caught a first try that judged "gave up" by hunger (the test makes it hungry the same tick it finishes eating): judge it by what the hunt itself caught.
232. **Fixed 2026-11-19 (BUGS 234, `SteadyController`): an animal's legs and tail jump when its walk or swim speeds up or slows down** (found 2026-11-19 by the new measuring course, `tools/body_course.py`, branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`). GeckoLib works out how far into a clip an animal is as "speed x time since the clip started", so a change of `setAnimationSpeed` rescales all the time already played and the clip jumps to another moment. Seen in the course's flat walk: the monitor's tail flipped about 0.4 blocks sideways in one frame as it slowed near its goal (the same tick in the step-up scene). Every animal that sets its clip speed from its own speed has it: the monitor, crocodile, crab and catfish (`setAnimationSpeed` in each `...Entity`). Not fixed (step 1 changes no looks); the fix belongs in the shared animal kit: keep the clip's own position and move it forward by speed each frame, so a speed change bends how fast it plays from now on, never where it is. Lesson: a clip's play speed must change its rate, never its position.
234. **The Nile monitor stuttered now and then while walking** (the user, 2026-11-19, in-game; branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`). Two causes in one place, both measured on the course (`run-film/course/course_monitor.txt`: 161 "FLICKER" lines, the walk dropping to idle for under one tick every 9 to 27 ticks). (1) `MonitorEntity.walking()` read one tick's movement: on a player's screen the monitor's place comes from the server in steps, and a late one left it standing for a tick, so the clip flicked to idle and the walk started over. Now it walks until it has stood still for `WALK_HOLD` ticks (3, my pick), and the walk's play speed follows its stride eased over ticks, not one tick's move. (2) BUGS 232: each play-speed change made the clip jump; fixed for every animal that sets its clip speed (the monitor, crocodile, crab, catfish, tilapia, snail) by the shared `entity/body/SteadyController`, which moves the clip on by speed x time passed. Lesson: never decide a drawing state from a single tick of a client-side position; and a clip's play speed must change its rate, never its position.
235. **After climbing a block, a hind foot of the Nile monitor twisted round and pointed backwards** (the user, 2026-11-19, in-game). Measured on the course: in `step_up` the left hind foot's long axis went from 38 deg forward-out to about -139 deg (backwards, upside down) for about 12 ticks after each step (39 moments on the step, 96 on the stairs, none on flat). Cause: after the box hops up, the old step code's whole-body lift (`GroundBend.lift`) eases off over a few ticks, so for a moment the body is drawn too high; from there the hind pair found no ground within its 1.3-block reach (the low ground behind the step), and `MonitorModel.legs` folded that pair toward the swimming pose, along the shortest turn, a foot twisting round. The fold is for water (walking into deep water, swimming), so a pair now folds only when the monitor is in water or water lies under that pair; on land over a drop the legs stay standing. The too-high body for a few ticks after a step is the old step code that stage B of `docs/plans/engine-step2-climb.md` replaces. Lesson: a pose made for one place (the swim fold for water) must be gated on that place, not on a stand-in for it (no ground in reach).
236. **Open: the catfish hunting test failed once more, "no scraps on the bed after the meal"** (found 2026-11-19 by the `river` run for the monitor's climb redo on 6 servers, branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`: `ahungrycatfishhuntsandswallowsatilapia`, beside two other tests; alone it passed 3 of 3). The same test and message as BUGS 220, the fifth time this test has failed (217, 219, 220, 225, 228). Not caused by the monitor: no monitor is in that test. Not looked into; it keeps coming back and is worth a look of its own.

Not the stage BUGS 227 stuck at: here the fry hatched and stayed round her, but in the 40 ticks after the stand-in player came within 2 blocks she took none in. Her danger check runs every 5 ticks and looks for a player within 6 blocks; a guess, not checked: under load beside 42 other tests she may have been mid-goal or the mock player's position not yet settled. Look at it with BUGS 227 (same test).
240. **Open: four known flakes came back in one full run** (found 2026-11-19 by the full run for animal life cycle step 3 on 6 servers, branch `claude/breeding-step3-crab-catfish`; that step changed only river animals and the animal kit, which no ant or termite test uses): `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember` "a tuft the brain never saw should not be foraged while away: cut 0" (BUGS 206; failed alone 1 of 3), `minorsBiteAChickenThatHuntsTheForagersWhoHurryHome` "the chicken is still alive" (BUGS 207; alone 1 of 3), `aBigColonyInADeadMoundRaidsFromTheScoutsOwnFindToThePileWithNoStageHanging` "no carrier was out on the way home to kill" (BUGS 93 and 212; alone 1 of 3), `termitesFinishTheUsersHalfBuiltStageFourteen` "stage 14 is not finished: {piece=2}" (BUGS 151; alone 3 of 3 passed). Each failing alone only some of the time means luck in the test itself (box 3 or a race), not a neighbour; recorded so their rate can be followed.
237. **Open: diving into the water off a bank two blocks high, the Nile monitor's dragged tail stands straight up the bank's face for about a second before it falls in** (found 2026-11-19 by the frame-by-frame film `tools/film/monitor_water_drop2_frames.film`, branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`; off a one-block bank it slants over the lip about 2.5 s and slides in, which looks right). The cause, measured: `TailDrag.floor`'s "ground rising behind" rule holds each joint near the face almost as high as the top (so the tail behind could slant up over it), and each piece climbs at its steepest toward that, building the column; the tail's tip just reaches the top (the tail is 2.25 blocks at size 1, the face 2.1 over the surface). Two tries on "count only ground the tail can still reach" did not fix it: fading by the distance along let the column stand, fading by the reach up and along put the climb down's tip inside the block (`TailDragTest`). Two strikes: the starting idea (reach decides) is wrong, a real tail on a lip stays by its weight on top, and slides off once more of it hangs below the lip than lies on top. **New idea, not built** (my recommendation, waits for the user): the rising-behind rule counts only while at least as much of the tail lies on the higher ground as hangs below it, so a draped tail slides off by itself; the climb down (most of the tail on top) keeps it. Lesson: a rule built for one case (coming down a block, tail on top) met a case it was not made for (the body dropping far below the tail); ask what physically holds the thing up.
238. **Open: the catfish hunting test failed again, a different way: "bitten off, 0 remains by the catfish (a bite-off leaves 1 fresh tilapia half, a swallow none)"** (found 2026-11-19 by the `river,life` run for the monitor's water moves on 6 servers, branch `claude/hopeful-kirch-74f4d5`: `ahungrycatfishhuntsandswallowsatilapia`; rerun alone it failed 1 of 3, so REAL, not only a neighbour leak). No catfish or remains code changed in this work. Likely kin of BUGS 236 (the same test, scraps missing after the meal): the bite-off's half may be eaten, carried off or despawned before the test looks. Not fixed on the spot (test failures go on the list).

   - **2026-11-25 (flaky-fixes session 3), measured, not fixed:** the stuck message now prints alive/removed, distance moved, room to breed, snails within 48 blocks, players, running goals, no-action time, in water and nav done (the logged failures had both snails 3590 ticks on their spawn blocks). It did not fail in 4 `life,river` runs after the leak fix (stand-in players left in the world, fixed at its source in `TestIsolation` (`letStandInPlayersGo`): each stand-in player goes once every test that was running when it came in is done. Found on the way: every passing test using `MatabeleDefenceTests.survivalPlayer` (the shark, Matabele and river tests) left its player behind, not only failing ones; any player in the level makes Minecraft despawn far-off young that are not kept (baby crabs off their mother's back, hatchlings, fry) and turns on the spawning caps of room to breed.) Read the new message the next time it fails.
243. **Open, low priority: no crocodile, of any size, can climb out onto a bank a full block over the water** (found 2026-11-20 while checking the young crocodiles, branch `claude/breeding-step4-crocodile`; first written down as "a hatchling sinks and sits on the bottom", which a game test showed was wrong). Measured in a game test: a hatchling swims across and climbs out onto a bank level with the water (as the mod's rivers are made) at its designed pace, and stopping in the water it rises and rests at the surface like a grown one. While it travels it swims low, a block under the surface, because Minecraft puts swimmers' route points in the lower water block; in the films' 3-deep pool that looked like sitting on the bottom. The real fault: from the water Minecraft's routes reach land only 1 level up (`floor(maxUpStep)`, and at most 1.125 blocks over a swimming spot's floor), and a bank a block over the water is 2 levels and 1.5 blocks up, so every route ends at its foot; the swim's hop (a push of 0.3 up wherever there is room 0.6 over it) also only clears level banks. Grown crocodiles fail the same way; the course's `climb_out` scene only got them out because the film camera keeps steering. Tried and thrown away (it broke basking on level banks, `crocodilesSpawnApartAndBaskTogether`): letting a swimming crocodile plan a step of 2 and haul out by a stepped lift. Not needed for now: the mod's rivers have level banks. Lesson: a test that answers the first question (the hatchling swims fine) is the moment to stop and re-ask whether the rest is needed.
244. **Open: the crocodile basking test failed once beside others, "the crocodile has not basked on the sand bank: sand spot none"** (found 2026-11-20 by the `river` run on 6 servers for the river's new prey (barb, fly clouds, bullfrog), branch `claude/breeding-step4-crocodile`: `acrocodileswimstoasandbanktobask`; passed alone 3 of 3, so a flake: box 4, something leaking from a neighbour test, or luck in the test). The new prey changed what crocodiles hunt (hatchlings and teens take barbs) but not basking; "sand spot none" says the crocodile found no sand spot to bask on at all. Not looked into yet.
245. **Fixed for the bullfrog, open for the other animals: the game draws each box's paint the other way round from the viewer** (found 2026-11-20 by the user: the bullfrog's in-game eyes were not their design; from the side a plain green panel). GeckoLib turns a model over left to right on loading, but not the paint: the face named east lands on the side the viewer draws west, and every face's picture is read back to front (sides and front left to right, top and bottom front to back). Invisible on a box painted the same on both sides; the frog's eyes have their art on the front and outer faces only. Fixed by `model_parts.game_faces` in the frog's export (the texture untouched); `tools/game_compare.py` proves it from the film's own cameras. **Still open for the others (not changed, the user's call):** the monitor's yellow back pattern and the crocodile's back shading are turned round box by box in the game, the flies' wings are drawn root to tip backwards, the catfish, crab and snail differ in faint mottling only, tilapia and barb not at all (each animal's game files drawn in the viewer with and without the rule). The fix for each is one call in its export plus its in-game films. Lesson: the viewer showing a look is not proof the game does: compare the in-game film with the approved viewer shot from the same camera before calling a look done. **Step C (2026-11-20, overnight): made automatic.** `model_parts.write_assets` passes every game model (geo/ file) through `game_faces` itself, once (bones a maker already turned by hand, the bullfrog's, are marked and left; its files byte for byte the same); an animal not yet proved passes `faces=False` with the reason beside it. Proved and on: the **monitor** (`tools/film/game_faces.film`, before and after beside its approved v1 from the same cameras, `references/workshop/monitor/faces_before_after.png`: with the rule its back and tail bars line up with the design, before they were shuffled). Kept off: the **crocodile** (filmed both ways, `references/workshop/crocodile/faces_before_after.png`: the game draws a white row of teeth along the lips that its approved look lacks with and without the rule, so a match cannot be shown; its mottling is too fine to judge from those cameras; to look into before turning it on), the **fly** (a cloud's flies are posed by FlyCloudRenderer and never stand still for `game_compare.py`; needs a still-fly film), and the catfish, tilapia, barb, crab, snail and remains (not filmed tonight). The monitor and crocodile now take `film:<clip>` names from the film camera (a clip they lack draws the rest pose).
246. **Fixed: a bullfrog sent anywhere stopped for good after about 2 blocks** (found 2026-11-20 by the measuring course, the polish's step 1: every scene, the frog hopped 4 times, 2.2 blocks, and sat; never seen in-game because the user had not tried the frog yet). Its legs crawled the last 0.8 blocks to a route's next point instead of hopping, but a hop covers only 0.55, so most hops landed inside that crawl zone, and the crawl was a small walking speed pushed through Minecraft's own walking sum, which squares a small speed to almost nothing (about 0.00002 blocks a tick): the frog sat a few tenths of a block from the point, never close enough for the route to move on. Fixed: it hops down to 0.3 blocks out and crawls the rest at the walk clip's own pace, moved straight (`BullfrogMoveControl`); found beside it: the landing slid on 8 ticks in the walk clip (feet jumping 18 px at both clip changes; now the hop keeps its landing 3 ticks and the slide is stopped), the frog's wander goal took over any route under way (now it waits for the route to end), and a step height of 1 let it glide up a block or out of the water without a hop (now Minecraft's 0.6: only a hop or the new climb out). Then the user, watching the course film: on the stairs it hopped straight up and down against the second step for good (Minecraft wipes a mob's speed into a block's side, and the hop's push was one kick): fixed by holding the hop's forward push through the flight. Tests `aBullfrogHopsWhereItIsSentAndUpAStep`, `aBullfrogClimbsOutOfTheWaterOntoTheBank`. Lesson: an animal's own move control needs the course run once before anyone tries it; a speed handed to Minecraft's walking push is not a pace.

Not looked into; it keeps coming back and is worth a look of its own.
248. **Open: the bullfrog's leap test failed once beside others, "the bullfrog never leapt (monitor 4.9 blocks off)"** (found 2026-11-20 by the `river` run for the bullfrog's hunt on 6 servers, branch `claude/bullfrog-polish`: `abullfrogleapsintothewaterfromamonitor`; passed alone 3 of 3, so a flake, box 4 or a timing edge). Not looked into yet.

    Fixed 2026-11-25 (branch `claude/flaky-fixes`, overnight flaky-test session 2): `aBullfrogLeapsIntoTheWaterFromAMonitor`: on its first tick the frog is still in the air (goals run before physics), and `EscapeGoal` then fell through to running away on foot instead of leaping. A frog in the air over land now decides only once it lands (`EscapeGoal.canUse`). 6 of 6 alone twice, and the `river` run.
249. **Open: the bullfrog's feet and hands spin round at four clip changes** (found 2026-11-20 by the new offline checker's blend check, `python tools/clip_check.py bullfrog --switches`, the Animal Maker Engine step A; not seen in the game yet). GeckoLib eases each angle straight from the old clip's number to the new clip's over the blend (3 ticks for the frog), so one pose written two ways turns the long way round: `climb_out` ends with each foot at 360 degrees and `idle` starts at about 1 (a whole spin in 3 ticks, up to 120 degrees a tick); `swim` holds the feet at about 196 and `float` at about -159 (the same turn, 355 apart, both ways); `leap` and `hop_dive` hand over to `swim` with the hands 165 degrees apart (55 a tick, a real move, maybe fine). The fix is in the clip maker (`tools/bullfrog_models.py`, the polish session's file tonight): write every angle in the same turn as the clips it meets (360 as 0, 196 as -164), then rerun the check. The monitor's `walk` to `swim` (both ways) moves a claw 12.8 px in a tick of its 4-tick blend: the course's baseline finding 2, now caught offline too. Lesson: an angle and the same angle plus 360 look alike in a still picture and only show at a clip change; the blend check catches them before the game.
250. **Open: the river snail flickers between `crawl` and `idle` as it goes** (found 2026-11-20 by the measuring course's first run of the snail, the Animal Maker Engine step A, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`: 15 to 92 flickers a scene, `idle` for a third of a tick between crawls, `bed_flat`, `bed_step_up`, `bed_step_down`, `bed_turn`, `shore_out`). Most likely its clip choice reads "is it moving" from a speed that drops to nothing between its slow steps (0.05 movement speed), so each tick it stops for a moment; with the 4-tick blend it may show as a small shudder, or not at all. Not looked into (no game code tonight); for step C: look at the films (`run-film/course/course_snail_*`), then give the choice a little hold.
251. **Open: the bullfrog's leap test fails alone now, "the bullfrog leapt but never came down in the water"** (found 2026-11-20 by the `river` run for the Animal Maker Engine step A on 6 servers, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`: `abullfrogleapsintothewaterfromamonitor` failed beside others and alone 2 of 3, so REAL).
