A frog in the air over land now decides only once it lands (`EscapeGoal.canUse`). 6 of 6 alone twice, and the `river` run.
253. **Open, for the bullfrog's own polish session (not changed tonight): what the hopper course and the one checker find on the frog** (found 2026-11-20/21 overnight by the Animal Maker Engine step C, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`; the first full run of the hopper scenes, `run-film/course/course_bullfrog.txt`, and `python tools/clip_check.py bullfrog --switches`). Worst first: (1) its feet jump 16 to 30 px in one frame at the start of hops in every scene (115 pops on the turn; very likely the foot spin of BUGS 249 at the clip change into and out of `hop`); (2) the hind feet are drawn up to 9 px (0.18 blocks) inside the ground in about a third of the moments on the flat and every land scene (sitting and crouching: the long foot's box under the ground); (3) the `walk` clip flickers in for 0.3 ticks between every hop and `idle` (the snail's BUGS 250 kind of flicker); (4) offline, the blends from `idle` into `walk`, `stalk` and `puff` and back jump 3 to 5 px (flagged), and the hops kick the hands and thighs 108 to 124 degrees in 0.05 s (a jump's kick: to judge on film, maybe right). A limit of the course, not the frog: its "planted foot slides" (5 to 8 times the body's speed) counts a foot near the ground through the push as planted, so it means little for a hopper; the hopper scenes should judge a foot only while the frog sits (a course fix for later). Lesson: the hopper course is the first look at the frog's land moves as the game draws them; the foot spin shows in every scene, so BUGS 249's fix comes first.
254. **Open, small (nothing fixed: none looked broken enough for a quick fix): the quick audit of the catfish, barb, crab and river snail** (found 2026-11-21 overnight by the Animal Maker Engine step C2, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`: `python tools/clip_check.py <animal> --switches` and `python tools/body_course.py run <animal> --quick`; reports `run-film/course/course_<animal>.txt`). **Crab:** walking out onto the shore every planted foot skates at about the crab's own speed (0.9 to 1.2 times). Cause (read from the code; step A's guess of a too-fast clip was wrong): its `walk` clip swings each leg fore and aft (`tools/crab_models.py`, `legs()`), but the game turns its body sideways to walk (`CrabEntity`), so a planted foot never strokes back along the way it goes: the fix is a sideways walk clip (the leading legs pulling, the trailing ones pushing, the tips moving along the body's length line), the crab's own polish. Its leg tips also go 2.8 px into the step's edge on the bed and the bank. **Catfish:** a chin barbel 3.9 px into the bed in the shallows (as step A); the blends `idle` to `court` (the tail 7 px in a tick) and `swim` to `flop` (5 px) flagged offline; seams open about 1.3 px with the bend. **Barb:** the blends `idle` to `nibble` and `swim` to `flop` flagged offline (4.6 and 6.1 px); seams 0.8 px; step A's tail-fin joint snaps did not show in the quick scenes. **River snail:** 18 clip flickers between `crawl` and `idle` walking out onto the shore (BUGS 250, still open). The fish's body waves (the new wave check) are right: growing to the tail and running back along it.
255. **Open: step B's new test `aMonitorWhoseStepIsBrokenMidClimbFallsBack` failed once beside others and once alone in 3, "the monitor never got its box on top of the step"** (found 2026-11-21 by the full run after the Animal Maker Engine steps A to C, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`, 6 servers; rerun alone: passed 2 of 3, REAL by the script's rule but only sometimes). The test sends a monitor (its size not fixed: 0.85 to 1.15 at random) straight at a one-block step and breaks the step once the move has its box over the step's top; in the failing runs that moment never came before the 900-tick time-out. The run's logs say nothing more (no positions). A guess, not checked: the move starts only while the monitor is within a 0.5-block window of its start (`MonitorEntity.climbAt`, `dist` against the move's `aheadPx`, which scales with its size), so on some runs it may pass the window and step up Minecraft's old way, or another of its goals (hunting, basking) takes it off the route. Which one matters: the first would be a real look bug (the old plank pop on a straight approach), the second only the test. **Measured after (same night):** the test alone on 6 servers at once failed 4 of 6; with a fuller failure message added (ticks on a move, the highest its box got on one, its size, where it ended) it failed 2 of 6, both at size 1.000 (so not its size): once it was on a move for 93 ticks but its box never rose above the floor (highest 0.00), once it never started a move in 900 ticks and stayed below the step (not even the old step up). Both point at the game, not the test: a monitor sent straight at a one-block step can stand at it without climbing, or play a climb that does not lift its box. Not fixed tonight (too big for the check run). To look at next: where it stands and faces each tick against `climbAt`'s 0.5-block start window and `BlockMoves.turnFor`, whether `BlockMoves.step` finds "a block in the way" and cuts the move each time (a cut move restarted would explain 93 ticks on moves with no rise), and whether the old step up still happens when no move fits. Worth checking in the game too: a monitor walking up to a one-block step. Its sister test `aMonitorHurtMidClimbIsSetDownAndWalksOn` (same scene) passed. The same run's other failures were old flakes: `tilapiaBreedInANestPitAndTheMotherKeepsHerFryInHerMouth` (BUGS 227 and 239; alone failed 1 of 3; no fish code changed tonight), and passing alone 3 of 3: `holesTakeTurnsNearFoodFirstAndMoreInTheRain`, `crabsCourtFaceToFaceAndTheBabiesRideOnTheirMother`, `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember` (BUGS 206), the replays `soak4_raider_going_home` (BUGS 161) and `soak4_raider_on_hump` (optional).

    Fixed 2026-11-25 (branch `claude/flaky-fixes`, overnight flaky-test session 2): the two monitor climb tests: a random stroll (or a mate search) took the monitor off the test's route, once to z 30.8 off the cleared ground, and it went up the step Minecraft's old way, never on a move; a bask or hunt starting at its random times could do the same. The scene (`monitorBeforeStep`) now keeps the monitor fed and sunned (`MonitorEntity.calmForTests`) and takes away its stroll and mate goals, so it walks only the test's route; the broken-step fail message tells what it was doing. 6 of 6 alone for each (after 5 of 6 with the calm hook alone), and the `river` run. Not fixed: a monitor that does come at a step at a bad angle still may step up the old way (the climb's narrow start window), a looks matter for the game, not these tests.
256. **Open: the young crocodiles draw their tails and heads deep inside blocks at steps and banks** (found 2026-11-21 by the first full course of the hatchling and the teen, `python tools/verify_animal.py crocodile`, reports `run-film/course/course_croc_hatchling.txt` and `course_croc_teen.txt`; nothing changed tonight in the crocodile's game files, so this is how they have been since they were made). Hatchling: tail5 45 to 47 px inside the block at a step down and both banks, the head 29 px inside on the stairs; teen: the tail 16 px inside blocks at a step down and the level bank, a 76 px tail pop in the turn, the neck snapping 67 degrees at a high bank. The grown crocodile's worst on the same scenes is 9 to 12 px pops. A guess, not checked: the old step rules (`GroundBend` and the crocodile's own) bend the body for a grown animal's length, and a young one's body pieces are much shorter (a model pixel is far smaller), so the bend lands the tail in the step. Looked at only in numbers, not on film. Goes with the crocodile's own moves (left over at step C) and BUGS 243.
264. **Fixed: a crocodile mother killed with hatchlings in her mouth deleted them with her** (found 2026-11-21 by the code review of eggs, breeding and hunting). `Carried` keeps young out of the world as saved data; the tilapia lets hers out in `remove`, the crocodile had no such step, so up to 4 hatchlings vanished when a player or anything else killed her on the way to the water. Fix: `CrocodileEntity.remove` lets them out at her jaws (or beside her) on KILLED or DISCARDED, never on an unload. Lesson: every animal holding a `Carried` must let it out on death; check it whenever a new animal carries young.
265. **Fixed: about half the world's nest mothers had a male's size** (found 2026-11-21 by the code review). `CrocodileNestFeature.mother` rolled a life (sex, then a size for that sex) and only then loaded `sex: female`, keeping a rolled male size (0.95 to 1.1 against a female's 0.7 to 0.85); size is the one way a player tells them apart. Fix: `AnimalLife.setUp(young, sex, random)` sets the sex before the size is rolled. Lesson: never change an animal's sex after its life is rolled; ask for the sex in `setUp`.
266. **Fixed: a hatching let out its whole clutch whatever room was left** (found 2026-11-21 by the code review). `RoomToBreed` was a yes or no, then every egg hatched: a crab at 11 of her limit of 12 let out 8 babies (19), and the same for snails, catfish mats and tilapia fry; the crocodile's nest alone capped it, but always let out at least one even with no room. Fix: `RoomToBreed.left` / `leftAt` (room left, 0 or more); the shared clutch (`EggClutchBlock.hatch`), the crab's babies, the tilapia's fry and the crocodile's nest let out at most that many, the rest failing. Lesson: a limit that gates a batch must cap the batch's size too.
267. **Fixed (not seen in play): a crocodile mother could pace for good after a nest spot she cannot reach** (found 2026-11-21 by the code review; only on uneven banks, the tests' banks are flat). `CrocodileBreeding.nestSite` picks the nearest dry bank by distance alone; after her minute ran out she looked again and picked the same spot, and while gravid she neither basks nor mates. Fix: `MotherGoal` remembers spots she ran out of time on or could not lay on and passes them by (`nestSite(croc, skip)`), cleared when she lays or when nothing else is left. Lesson: a search that retries after a timeout must not pick the same failed answer again.
268. **Open: three flakes in the river, life and worldgen run after the code-review fixes** (found 2026-11-21; each passed alone 3 of 3). `crocodilesCourtNestAndTheMotherCarriesHerYoungToTheWater`: "4 hatchlings from 9 eggs (5 wanted)": the test expects the local limit minus 1 (only the mother near), so one more crocodile was within 48 blocks of the nest, likely a neighbouring test's on the same server (the hatching count works the same before and after BUGS 266 whenever there is room for at least one); the test should count the crocodiles near at the hatching, or its scene keep 48 blocks clear. `catfishSpawnOnARainyNightAndScatterEggsOnTheAlgae`: stuck at stage 0, the female not ready (rain or night not seen in time beside other tests?). `aMonitorHurtMidClimbIsSetDownAndWalksOn`: "has not walked on since it was set down (0.00 blocks)", the sister of BUGS 255 (passed in the full run of 2026-11-21).
   - **Fixed 2026-11-25 (flaky-fixes session 3), the crocodile part:** cause as written: the test hard-coded room for the local limit minus 1 while the nest lets out min(eggs, room within 48 blocks). The test now reads the room the nest really has every tick while it stands (`RoomToBreed.leftAt`) and expects min(eggs, that), and counts only young whose mother is this female. The "0 hatchlings" kind was the shared cause, stand-in players left in the world, fixed at its source in `TestIsolation` (`letStandInPlayersGo`): each stand-in player goes once every test that was running when it came in is done. Found on the way: every passing test using `MatabeleDefenceTests.survivalPlayer` (the shark, Matabele and river tests) left its player behind, not only failing ones; any player in the level makes Minecraft despawn far-off young that are not kept (baby crabs off their mother's back, hatchlings, fry) and turns on the spawning caps of room to breed.
269. **Open: a hippo walking into water deeper than a block from a level bank has no move: its hips are drawn inside the bank for a moment** (found 2026-11-21 by the hippo's course, `bank_level`: hips 7.6 px inside the bank, 12 parts in all; and a tail snap of 20 degrees as the walk hands over to the underwater walk). Its moves cover a step up, a step down (also into water a block deep: wading) and out of water a block deep; a bank dropping into deeper water falls the old way, its 5-block body drawn rigid while the box drops. Lesson: a long rigid body needs a move (or a ground bend, as the monitor's and crocodile's) wherever its box drops a block. Fix when wanted: an "into deep water" move (the hippo slides in, chest first) from Neon, or the shared `GroundBend`.
270. **Open: three flakes in the river and life run after the hippo went in** (found 2026-11-21; each passed alone 3 of 3): `aCrocodileMotherSeesOffAMonitorFromHerNest`, `snailsMeetLayAtTheWaterlineAndHatch`, `aHungryCatfishHuntsAndSwallowsATilapia` ("no scraps on the bed after the meal"). No hippo in any of their scenes; the box-4 leak hunt is not done.
   - **2026-11-25:** the snail part, see BUGS 242 (measuring added, the player leak fixed).
271. **Fixed: hippos doubled up, spawned in the ground, and vanished** (the user's first look in-game, 2026-11-21: "3 of them then I go away and come back then there's 6", "they spawn in the ground a lot and suffocate", "they despawn randomly when I look or go away"). Four causes in the spawner's way: (1) the spawn rule let a new hippo in when it was near *every* member of a pod (meant for a pod's own members spawning one by one), so later spawns kept joining an old pod, and the group (cap 6, tried every tick) topped every pod up to 6; (2) a spawned hippo was dropped to the bed under its middle only, its 2-block-wide body lying in a sloping bed or bank, and nothing checked the water was open to the sky, so hippos spawned in water pockets under the land; (3) it was removed past 96 blocks but spawned out to 128, so ones spawned between died off within a minute or two, and all went past 128 (the render distance kept them running to 160), a new pod spawning on the way back; (4) Minecraft drew it only while its box (the 2-block barrel) was on screen, so the 5-block hippo blinked out at the screen's edge. Fix (the user's pick of the plan, "place with land"): no more spawning; new land lays pods (`worldgen/HippoPodFeature`, sites by `worldgen/PodSites` on the shared `RiverSites`) of 1 to 3 that are never removed, each spot checked for the whole body (3 by 3 columns clear two blocks up, water open to the sky); drawn by a box round the whole body, out to 128. Lesson: a big animal's spawn must check its whole box where it ends up, after any moving down; a "pod" rule that lets members near each other in also lets strangers in; a kept-near distance under the group's spawn reach makes animals flicker in and out. Test: `RiverLifeTests.hippoPodsAreLaidWithTheLandAndKeptForGood`.
272. **Fixed: hippo pods made a barricade across the river, killing every crocodile near** (the user, 2026-11-21: "they take up half the river in territory like a barricade killing every crocodile in site"). Every hippo, cows too, charged any grown crocodile within 6 blocks at once with no warning, chased it 16 blocks for 12 seconds and bit for 5 hearts (4 bites kill one); the whole pod joined in. Each hippo also moved its own home pool to wherever it rested 10 blocks off, so a pod drifted apart into separate pools along the river. The crocodiles' Minecraft avoid goal looks for a land spot to run to and often found none in the water. Fix: only a bull minds a crocodile, and only within 5.5 blocks of his middle: a yawn first, then a bluff charge that ends once it is 8 off, one bite at most (real pods let crocodiles bask beside them); a pod shares the bull's pool, moved only if the pool is gone, and wanders 5 from it; crocodiles have their own keep-clear goal that swims 12 blocks away from a hippo within 7 (14 if it is charging). Lesson: in an ecosystem a reaction should usually be a warning, not a fight to the death; "every member of a group" running the same rule multiplies it. Tests: `RiverLifeTests.aBullSeesOffACrocodileWithABluffNotAFight`, `aCowLetsACrocodileBe`.
273. **Fixed: a crocodile afloat against a one-block shelf could not climb onto it, and a hippo bull bit it again** (found 2026-11-21 by `RiverLifeTests.aBullSeesOffACrocodileWithABluffNotAFight`, failing 1 to 2 runs in 3 after two tries; BUGS 272's open part). Measured with a diary: the crocodile's keep-clear goal runs and its route to the bank 12 blocks off is found (12 squares, reachable, the move control wanting to move), but its x never changes: it floats with its feet about 0.02 under the top of the shelf block (water 2 deep beside a shelf 1 deep), and its "up and out" lift in `CrocodileEntity.travel` does not fire there, so it drifts sideways along the lip. The test pool also leaves no way off through water (11 wide, the flight 12). Its bull, his charge rest over, warns and bites it again. Tries so far: (1) flight to a bank as well as water; (2) the keep-clear goal over the plunge (a bitten crocodile on a bank plunged into the nearest water, beside the bull). Two strikes: the starting idea ("the bull stops because the crocodile gets away") was rethought with the user (2026-11-21, "do both"): (a) after biting a crocodile a bull lets crocodiles be for a minute (`HippoEntity.CROC_CALM`), got away or not; (b) the climb-out lift asks for no block in the way (`level().noCollision`), water allowed: Minecraft's `isFree` also refuses water, so the lift never fired against a ledge under the surface. Lesson: Minecraft's `Entity.isFree` means "no block and no liquid"; a swimmer's room check must ask for blocks only. Tests: `RiverLifeTests.aCrocodileAfloatClimbsOverAnUnderwaterLedge` (fails with the old check, measured), `aBullSeesOffACrocodileWithABluffNotAFight`.
274. **Open: a flake in the life run after the hippo pods** (found 2026-11-21, branch `claude/hippo-pods`; passed alone 3 of 3): `AnimalLifeTests.crocodilesCourtNestAndTheMotherCarriesHerYoungToTheWater` ("4 hatchlings from 9 eggs (5 wanted: room for that many)"), beside the river and worldgen tests; no hippo in its scene. The box-4 leak hunt is not done (as BUGS 268, 270).
   - **Fixed 2026-11-25:** see BUGS 268.
275. **Open: a tilapia breeding flake in the river and life run on 4 servers** (found 2026-11-21, branch `claude/hippo-pods`; passed alone 3 of 3): `AnimalLifeTests.tilapiaBreedInANestPitAndTheMotherKeepsHerFryInHerMouth` stuck at stage 0 with "room false" (no room to breed while beside other tests' fish), the female following a flock leader; the crocodile flake of BUGS 274 came again in the same run. No hippo or zone rule in its scene (the arena is off the savanna: no zone). The box-4 leak hunt is not done (as BUGS 268, 270, 274).
277. **Open: four river flakes in the mud and river run for mud step 4** (found 2026-11-21, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`, 6 servers; each passed alone 3 of 3; no mud in any of their scenes, and the coat rules run only on a muddy creature, so not from step 4). `aBullfrogLeapsIntoTheWaterFromAMonitor`: "never leapt (monitor 6.2 blocks off)" (as BUGS 248). `aCrocodileAfloatClimbsOverAnUnderwaterLedge`: "never got over the ledge onto the bank", still in water (new; BUGS 273's test). `aHungryCatfishHuntsAndSwallowsATilapia`: "bitten off, 0 remains by the catfish" (new message; the family of BUGS 217, 219, 220). `aMonitorHurtMidClimbIsSetDownAndWalksOn`: "not hurt and landed yet" (the sister of BUGS 255, 268). The box-4 leak hunt is not done (as BUGS 268, 270, 274, 275).

    Fixed 2026-11-25 (branch `claude/flaky-fixes`, overnight flaky-test session 2): the two monitor climb tests: a random stroll (or a mate search) took the monitor off the test's route, once to z 30.8 off the cleared ground, and it went up the step Minecraft's old way, never on a move; a bask or hunt starting at its random times could do the same. The scene (`monitorBeforeStep`) now keeps the monitor fed and sunned (`MonitorEntity.calmForTests`) and takes away its stroll and mate goals, so it walks only the test's route; the broken-step fail message tells what it was doing. 6 of 6 alone for each (after 5 of 6 with the calm hook alone), and the `river` run. Not fixed: a monitor that does come at a step at a bad angle still may step up the old way (the climb's narrow start window), a looks matter for the game, not these tests.
278. **Fixed 2026-11-22 (was open): the monitor's tail makes a W at a level bank's lip as it walks into the water** (found 2026-11-22 in the walk-in's in-game film, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`). As the hips sink below the bank, the dragged tail (`TailDrag`) eases its pieces over the water down to the surface while the pieces still over the bank lie on it, so from the side the tail goes down, up over the lip and down again for about half a second (the course record: tail7 to tail8 turning 32 degrees, a 3.7 px seam at tail3/tail2, tail4 1.2 px into the bank). The clip is not the cause (the drawn trunk follows its course in the record); the family of BUGS 237. Lesson: the tail drag judges each piece by the ground or water under it alone; where the ground steps down to water under the tail, the pieces need to hang as one rope from the lip. Proposal: let a piece over the water rest no lower than a straight line from the last piece on the bank toward the root (one rule in `TailDrag`, a test beside the root-ceiling one). **Fix:** over the water, while the tail behind still lies on the bank it came off, each joint lies no lower than the straight line from the joint in front to the lip (`TailDrag.lip`), a rope over an edge; only for a low lip: in full up to a fifth of the tail's length over where it floats, gone by two fifths (`BRIDGE`), so off a high bank the tail still floats and hangs down the face (laid as a rope to a two-block bank, its end flipped over the top). Tests `walkingOffALowBankTheTailRunsUpToTheLipWithNoDip` (failed before) and `offAHighBankTheTailStillFloatsAndHangsDownTheFace` (fails without the limit).
279. **Open: the catfish swallow test now fails alone most times** (found 2026-11-22 in the river run for the monitor's new walk-in, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`, 6 servers): `aHungryCatfishHuntsAndSwallowsATilapia` "bitten off, 0 remains by the catfish"; alone it failed 2 of 3 in the script's reruns and 2 of 3 again in three clean reruns (REAL by the 4-box rule; in BUGS 277 it passed alone 3 of 3). Not from the walk-in change: no monitor in its scene, and the change touches only the monitor's clip, its room check before walking in, and the drawing-only tail drag. The family of BUGS 217, 219, 220; box 1 or 3 to be judged when it is taken up.
280. **Open: the bullfrog leaping from a monitor fails alone 1 time in 3** (found 2026-11-22 in the same river run, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`): `aBullfrogLeapsIntoTheWaterFromAMonitor` "never leapt (monitor 8.4 blocks off)"; the script's reruns 2 of 3 failed, three clean reruns 1 of 3 failed. The test leans on the monitor wandering toward the frog by chance, so its timing is luck (as BUGS 248, 277). The walk-in change makes the monitor need a longer dry run-up before walking into water (`roomToEnter`), which may change where it wanders beside the test's small pool, but the failure is older. Proposal when taken up: make the test send the monitor at the frog (box 3) rather than wait for it.

    Fixed 2026-11-25 (branch `claude/flaky-fixes`, overnight flaky-test session 2): `aBullfrogLeapsIntoTheWaterFromAMonitor`: on its first tick the frog is still in the air (goals run before physics), and `EscapeGoal` then fell through to running away on foot instead of leaping. A frog in the air over land now decides only once it lands (`EscapeGoal.canUse`). 6 of 6 alone twice, and the `river` run.
281. **Open: a raid can crash the server as it starts** (found 2026-11-22 in the full run before the reed and hippo grass blocks, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`, test server 1, crash report `run-test-shards/1/crash-reports/crash-2026-11-22_10.30.02-server.txt`): `IndexOutOfBoundsException: toIndex = 2` in `RaidColumn.<init>` (`RaidColumn.java:447`, from `Raids.startRaid` via `MatabeleScoutGoal.walkHome`). The cause read from the code: when the scout's scent line holds no point farther out than the line's start (all removed by the `removeIf` before it), the trail is the start alone (1 point), and `trail.subList(0, Math.max(1, gather) + 1)` asks for 2. A crash in a normal world too, whenever a scout comes home with a very short line. Not from the oxbow, pan or plant work (raid code untouched). Proposed fix when taken up: give a one-point line a second point (the find) or cap the end at `trail.size()`, and a raids test with a scout finding termites right beside its nest; the test it stopped (and the rest of server 1's share) to be rerun.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** closed with 158 (see its line).
282. **Open: the full run after oxbows and pans steps 3 to 5 (not sorted: the user stopped the reruns, 2026-11-22, "we're only touching the rivers")** (6 servers, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`; server 1 stopped by the raid crash, BUGS 281, so about a sixth of the tests did not run): 9 required tests failed of the 258 that ran. `minorsBiteAChickenThatHuntsTheForagersWhoHurryHome` "the chicken is still alive (1.0 health)", **REAL** (failed alone 2 of 3, the one rerun that finished; the chicken tests of BUGS 207). Not rerun: `aScoutRingsALivingMoundItComesNearAndFollowsATermiteTunnelOutToTheFeeding` "no scout noticed the living mound yet"; `aCrocodileAfloatClimbsOverAnUnderwaterLedge` "never got over the ledge onto the bank" (the climb of BUGS 273); `aHungryCatfishHuntsAndSwallowsATilapia` (BUGS 279); `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember` "a tuft the brain never saw should not be foraged while away: cut 0" (BUGS 206); `tilapiaBreedInANestPitAndTheMotherKeepsHerFryInHerMouth` "her fry not taken into her mouth" (BUGS 239, 275); `crabsCourtFaceToFaceAndTheBabiesRideOnTheirMother` "crabs stuck at stage 0 ... closest to the courting distance" never measured; `crocodilesCourtNestAndTheMotherCarriesHerYoungToTheWater` "4 hatchlings from 8 eggs (5 wanted)"; and the optional `replay_soak4_raider_going_home` (a raider left standing outside). Every one but the crab courting has failed before as a flake. The river ones are the ones oxbows and pans could touch (the river zones were split into oxbow and pan zones), though their arenas hold no oxbow or pan: to be rerun alone with the river category when taken up.

283. **Fixed 2026-11-22 (same day; the user: "Yes fix it"): the crocodile's `JAWS_AHEAD` was half the drawn snout's reach (found 2026-11-22 while fitting its body hit boxes, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`).** `CrocodileEntity.JAWS_AHEAD` says "the snout's tip, 20 model pixels" (1.39 blocks at size 1), but since the longer head the model's snout tip is 40 pixels ahead of the animal's position (2.78 blocks; `geo/entity/crocodile.geo.json`: head cubes z -40 to -20, the position at the front of the hips). It sets where the jaws close (`jawPoint`, bites, grabs, where a carried animal sits in `positionRider`, and nine other uses), so a crocodile likely bites and carries from about the middle of its head, not its snout. The sideways snap had the same 20-pixel error: it swung the head from "shoulders" 6 pixels behind the position, the neck's joint being 14 ahead. **Fix:** `JAWS_AHEAD` 40 pixels (2.78 blocks, the snout's tip: bites, grabs, the mother's pick-up, where it drags from); a new `HOLD_AHEAD`, 30 pixels (2.08, the model's `carried` socket between the teeth), for where a held animal sits (`positionRider`); `NECK_BACK` 6 behind became `NECK_AHEAD` 14 ahead (`shoulders()`, the snap). The model's snout has been at 40 pixels since the first crocodile (`f65b40e3`), so the old number was never right. River and life tests pass (the courting and carrying test 3 of 3 alone); filmed stepped from three sides (`tools/film/crocodile_bite_frames.film`): it closes on a cow's head with its snout and holds it between its teeth through the rolls. The monitor's (`2.9 * MODEL_SCALE`, 2.18) matches its model (2.25). **Lesson:** a body distance written as "N model pixels" must be re-measured when the model changes; measure from the model file, not the old comment.

284. **Fixed 2026-11-22 (same step): body hit boxes counted as their animal in searches by type (found by the river and life run while building the hit chains; branch `claude/engine-every-animal`).** NeoForge gives every `PartEntity` its animal's entity type, and `Level.getEntities(EntityTypeTest, box, ...)` returns parts too, so `RoomToBreed` counted each catfish three times and each crocodile eleven: `catfishSpawnOnARainyNight...` failed alone 3 of 3 ("female ready false") and the crocodile courting test likewise. A search by type would also hand back a box cast as the animal (a crash waiting for the first one to call an animal method on it), and `@e[type=...,distance=..]` would find the boxes. **Fix:** `BodyPart.getType()` answers its own type, `colonyframework:body_part` (`ModEntities.BODY_PART`, never spawned, saved or summoned, an empty renderer); the hit test checks a search by the crocodile's type finds it once. Both tests then passed alone 3 of 3 (beside others they stay the old flakes of BUGS 268). The tilapia's smaller box (same step) made `noTilapiaNestWhileTheRiverIsFull` fail 1 to 2 of 3: it put exactly the local limit of fish in the pond, so one drifting past the count's edge let the male nest; it now puts in three more (a full river; box 3 of the 4-box rule). **Lesson:** a multipart animal's parts are entities of their own in every level search; give them their own type so only searches by class or by "any entity" see them.

285. **Open: two new flakes beside other tests in the river and life run after BUGS 283's fix (2026-11-22, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`; each passed alone 3 of 3).** `aCrocodileClimbsOutToBaskAndGoesBackIn` (13 s, the slowest of the run; the jaw point is not used in basking, so likely load) and `aBullfrogLeapsIntoTheWaterFromAMonitor` "the bullfrog leapt but never came down in the water". The same run's other failures were the old flakes of BUGS 268 (catfish spawning, crocodile courting, the monitor hurt mid-climb). Not looked into yet. **Lesson:** none yet.

    2026-11-25 (overnight session 1, branch `claude/flaky-fixes`): `aCrocodileClimbsOutToBaskAndGoesBackIn` passed 12 of 12 beside the other crocodile tests and 6 of 6 alone after BUGS 305's clock fix (it is one of the day-clock tests: a night test's clock beside it took its sun away); counted fixed with BUGS 305.

286. **Open: two more river flakes beside other tests, found by the test speed step 2 check runs (2026-11-22, branch `claude/engine-every-animal`, `river,worldgen` on 6 servers dealt by length; each passed alone 3 of 3).** `aMonitorWatchesTrailsAndBitesAPlayer` "the monitor never watched the player: doing 1, player 11.0 away" and `aCrocodileSwimsToASandBankToBask` "the crocodile has not basked on the sand bank". 287. **Open: the two old river flakes again beside other tests, in the bull shark's `river,worldgen` run (2026-11-22, branch `claude/bull-shark-e387b8`, 3 servers; each passed alone 3 of 3).** `aHungryCatfishHuntsAndSwallowsATilapia` "no scraps on the bed after the meal" (as BUGS 220 and 236) and `aCrocodileSwimsToASandBankToBask` "has not basked on the sand bank" with a sand spot found this time (kin of BUGS 244).     2026-11-25 (overnight session 1, branch `claude/flaky-fixes`): `aCrocodileSwimsToASandBankToBask` after BUGS 305's clock fix (the "sand spot none" mode gone) and a route check on basking company (no company was headed for): still 2 of 12 beside the other crocodile tests, "has not basked" with the sand spot found (42,19,20) and the crocodile stuck in the channel at (39..40, 18, 16..17), right by the sand's corner; 6 of 6 alone. Two tries done, so stopped (two-fails rule). Its fail message now prints in water, on ground, sun time, route done and the running goals: read those next time. Guess, not checked: the climb from the channel onto the sand (one block above the water) at the corner where grass meets sand, like the ledge's bank climb (BUGS 303); the floating lift's change of 2026-11-25 may help.

288. **Fixed: otters drowned in the river (2026-11-22, `master`, found by the user in-game).** Afloat at its float depth (feet 0.71 under the surface) the otter's eye point (0.7 up, Minecraft checks 0.11 lower) sat just under the water, so its air never refilled even with its head out; about 16 s into the water it began to drown. Every other river animal already said it cannot drown (`canDrownInFluidType` false) and lets its own dive rules bring it up; the otter had only a longer air supply. Fix: the same as the others (its dives, at most 20 s, decide its breath). Test `anOtterAfloatInAPoolDoesNotDrown` (75 s afloat, never hurt; failed at 16 s without the fix). **Lesson:** a land animal that floats low needs its eye point checked against its waterline, or Minecraft's air system judges its breath behind its back; a new swimming animal says who decides its breath.

289. **Fixed: the monitor's legs, and most of all its hind legs, snapped into the swim pose going into the water (2026-11-22, found by the user in-game, an old complaint).** Each move in folded the legs in its last 0.5 to 1.2 s, so they seemed to change form at once. A first fix the same day held the legs hanging in the game and turned each leg bone straight from hanging to the swim clip's pose over 2 s: the offline check showed the feet nearly half a turn apart between the two poses (124 to 170 degrees), and turning each bone on its own sent the hind feet the long way round, up past the back (seen in the film). Fix: the clip maker drifts and folds them itself (HISTORY 2026-11-22, "the monitor eases into the water"), each foot moved along the fold's own route with the leg reaching to it. **Lesson:** to blend two poses far apart (a foot turned more than about 90 degrees), never turn each bone separately in the game: move the foot along a route and let the leg follow (the clip maker's way), and check the hand-over with `clip_check --switches` before filming.

290. **Open: the monitor test `aMonitorWhoseStepIsBrokenMidClimbFallsBack` fails now and then (2026-11-23, found by the `river` run of the bull shark reactions, which do not touch the monitor).** In the six-server run it failed with "the monitor never got its box on top of the step (on a move 0 ticks, highest on one -1.00)", and alone it failed 1 of 3, so it is not only crowding. 294. **Fixed: the otter's caught fish lay tilted 22 degrees across its jaws, and a gulped barb floated at the upper lip (2026-11-22, found by Neon, `docs/plans/otter-held-fish-rethink.md`).** GeckoLib 4.9.3's `GeoEntityRenderer.actuallyRender` treats any rider of a living vehicle as a sitting rider (it never asks `shouldRiderSit`): a rider whose head faces across its vehicle (the held fish, turned 90 degrees across the otter) gets its body clamped to 85 degrees from its head and then nudged a fifth further, 22 degrees off whatever the drawing code expected; `OtterRenderer` undid only the vehicle's turn, so the fish kept a 22 degree tilt in the game while the offline check (no GeckoLib) showed it level. And the fish was placed by a fixed jaw gap, so when the gulp clip opened the jaw wide the barb stayed at the upper lip. Fix: a fish held by an otter skips its own `applyRotations` (`TilapiaRenderer`, `BarbRenderer`), so the otter alone turns it; `OtterRenderer` notes its head's place as GeckoLib draws the carry bone (a `GeoRenderLayer`, as `CatfishRenderer`) and turns the fish down about the jaw's hinge with the live jaw (`HeldCatch.ride`: halfway while gripped, lying on the lower jaw when the jaw is open wider than the fish is thick); `clip_check.py --hold` does the same offline; the carry bone moved from inside the muzzle (10.65) to the lip line (10.45); the film's eating and gulping takes now really eat and gulp (tags `doing.eat`, `doing.gulp`: a `film:` name only played the clip). Lesson: anything drawn riding a GeckoLib entity gets GeckoLib's rider turn, not vanilla's; when a renderer places a rider itself, switch the rider's own turn off rather than undoing it, and film it, since the offline engine has no GeckoLib in it.
295. **Open: frozen, stepped films draw a clip's clock two ticks a picture while the body moves one** (found 2026-11-23 filming the otter's noodle bound, `tools/film/otter_bound_frames.film`). In a burst (`cmd tick freeze`, `burst ... 1`) a running otter moved 0.13 blocks a picture (its 2.7 blocks a second, so one tick), but its 8-tick bound clip came round in 4 pictures; the in-place `film:` clips show the same (the 17-tick pounce in about 8 pictures, `frames 4` bursts in half the pictures expected). Filmed live (the game not frozen, `tools/film/otter_bound_live.film`) the clip keeps pace with the ground (about one stride a block, as made), so the game is right and the stepped camera is not: likely the client runs its entities' own tick (GeckoLib's clock reads `tickCount`) twice for each stepped tick (`client/FilmStep`), while their places come from the server once. Until fixed: judge poses from stepped films, timing and speed only from live films or the offline checker. Lesson: a film's timing needs its own check against the ground before it is trusted.
296. **Fixed 2026-11-23 (was open): `amonitorhurtmidclimbissetdownandwalkson` fails** (the river tie-up: on a clean
`master` it passed alone 3 of 3 but failed beside others; the cause found reading the flight: hurt by nothing it could see,
with no water near, `FleeGoal.run` had no place to run to, so the monitor stood frozen the whole 15 s it was startled and
the test's walk-on depended on a lucky stroll after; now it bolts 12 blocks any way. Lesson: a flight needs a way to go
when there is nothing to flee from and nowhere to hide) (found 2026-11-23 by the `river` run after the otter's noodle bound; REAL, failed alone 2 of 3: "the monitor has not walked on since it was set down (0.00 blocks)"). The working folder held uncommitted monitor changes from before that session (`MonitorEntity.java`, `MonitorModel.java`, `framework/.../body/BlockMoves.java`), which the otter work did not touch; check whether it fails with them stashed before looking further.
297. **The otters' swimming chase ended the moment it started** (found 2026-11-23 by the new porpoise test,
`playingOttersPorpoiseInADeepPool`; there since play part 1, 2026-11-22). A swimming round starts with the two nose to nose
after the poke, and the chase counted the chaser "caught up" (closer than the rearing gap plus a block) on its very first
tick, so every round was spent at once and the bout ended: the swimming chases of part 1 never really happened. Fixed:
a catch counts only after the runner has had 30 ticks' start (`OtterEntity.Bout`, `runFor`). Lesson: a "caught" test
needs the chase to have begun; check where both start before trusting a distance rule.
298. **Open, one cause fixed 2026-11-23: two catfish tests flaky under load** (the river tie-up: alone it failed 1 of 3
with "no scraps on the bed": `ScrapsBlock.leave` gave up when the mouth was above the water or algae lay on the bed under it
and its four sides; now it starts from the nearest water beside the spot (a mouth above the water or inside a wall) and tries the corners; the test now has 2600 ticks, since a missed chase gives up at 600 and rests 1200. A load-only failure, "held in the mouth 0 ticks"
(no catch in 900 ticks beside 4 other servers), is still open; also BUGS 279) (found 2026-11-23 by the `river` run for the otter chase and tag, 4 servers,
with another session's test servers possibly running at the same time). `ahungrycatfishhuntsandswallowsatilapia` ("no

("0 barbs gone") 1 of 3; both passed alone straight after, and the run before that day's otter work passed them. Nothing

that runs past the test's wait when the server is slow) before calling it a game bug.
299. **Fixed 2026-11-23 (was open): two otter play spacing tests fail most runs** (the river tie-up, traced stage by
stage in the test: box 1, the guess below was right. The keep-out-of-bodies push shoved the one stepping in to the rearing
gap back (2.0 to 2.2 for 1.69) and the one walking to lie beside the other off its flank, so its 3 seconds ran out 1.5
blocks short and it lay crosswise; now the push is off in a bout's face-to-face part and between a settling otter and the
one it lies beside. Box 3 too: the spacing test started the pair 7 apart, past the 6 of playmate reach, so some runs never
played; now 5. Lesson: a rule that keeps bodies apart must know which meetings are meant) (found 2026-11-23 by the `river` run for the hippo's bank rest
and lawns, 4 servers). `playingOttersTouchNosesAndRestSideBySideWithoutOverlapping` failed alone 3 of 3, each time
differently ("they never poked noses"; "resting their middles lay 1.58 apart, not about 0.9", 1.40 along: one lay well

("they reared 2.33 apart, not about 1.6875"). Not from the otter triggers work: the same two fail the same way on the

the poke, the rear and the rest is set once and then each otter walks to its own spot, so a likely place is the two
arriving at different times (one still walking when the other lies down or rears, or the new keep-out-of-bodies rule
pushing one off its mark); the 4-box rule: likely box 1 (a real spacing bug the user saw) or 3.

303. **Open: the full run after the river food web's step 3 (2026-11-23, master plus step 3 uncommitted, 6 servers, 45 minutes).** Test server 1 crashed with the old raid start crash (`IndexOutOfBoundsException: toIndex = 2` in `RaidColumn.<init>` line 447, from `MatabeleScoutGoal.walkHome`: a raid set out on a scent trail of one point; the same as BUGS 158, 198 and 281, still open), so its 65 tests did not all finish. `aCatfishTakesABarbAndTheSchoolBursts` failed beside others and alone 1 of 3 ("0 barbs gone"; it failed alone 2 of 3 before today, BUGS 291 and 292, so older than the food web; the food web's barb change, meals over Minecraft's fish swimming and a wider straight stretch, is a suspect to rule out). Flakes that passed alone 3 of 3: `aBullfrogLeapsIntoTheWaterFromAMonitor`, `aMonitorWhoseStepIsBrokenMidClimbFallsBack`, `tilapiaBreedInANestPitAndTheMotherKeepsHerFryInHerMouth`, `aCrocodileAfloatClimbsOverAnUnderwaterLedge`, `crocodilesCourtNestAndTheMotherCarriesHerYoungToTheWater`, `replay_soak4_raider_going_home` (optional). Every food web test passed. **The catfish test fixed 2026-11-23** (it failed 2 of 6 alone; logged chases showed two causes, both prey at the surface: the catfish dashing up after a barb overshot out of the water, and a barb breaking the surface for one tick stopped counting as prey; either ended the hunt, and any early end cost the catfish a minute's rest meant only for a 30 s chase run out, so the test's minute ran out). Fix in `HuntTilapiaGoal`: it aims no higher than its own height under the water's top (`underTheSurface`, the lunge too), and prey lost before the chase runs out is looked for again in 10 s (`LOST_IT`); 12 of 12 then, the river run clean but the old frog leap flake. The raid crash and the other flakes not looked into. **Lesson:** a long rest after a failure turns a one-tick blip into a lost minute; let the rest match the failure it was meant for. And the raid start crash has now stopped a server in four full runs; it needs its fix.

    2026-11-25 (overnight session 1, branch `claude/flaky-fixes`), `aCrocodileAfloatClimbsOverAnUnderwaterLedge`: measured with a tick trace. Two causes found and fixed: (1) the crocodile does climb out, but it does not want the sun, so its middle reaches only x 26.0..26.3 on the bank (STEP_X 26) before it turns back to the water, and the test wanted 26.5: the test now counts its middle over the bank (STEP_X); (2) the floating lift asked for room 0.6 up, but a grown male floats with his feet 0.6..0.7 under the bank's top (18.37 under 19.0), so it just failed: `CrocodileEntity.travel` now asks room to rise onto the top of the block its feet are in (at least 0.6). Still failing 2 of 6 alone after both: once the crocodile's own `RandomSwimmingGoal` took over the test's route (it does not want the sun, so its mind sends it elsewhere), once it got out with `AshoreGoal` right at the 400-tick end. Two tries, so stopped. **Recommended new starting idea (not built):** the test should not steer with raw navigation against the crocodile's own goals; give its arena a noon clock and `wantSun()`, so climbing out is what the crocodile itself wants and `AshoreGoal` takes it over the shelf to the nearest bank, with a longer timeout.

305. **Open: three crocodile flakes beside other tests, in the river run for the food web's step 6, fish round hippo dung (2026-11-23, branch `claude/food-web-6`, 6 servers; each passed alone 3 of 3).** `aCrocodileSwimsToASandBankToBask` "sand spot none" (BUGS 244), `aCrocodileSnapsUpFishAndCallsOthersToTheSchool` a time-out (as in BUGS 286's lag) and `crocodilesSpawnApartAndBaskTogether` "the first crocodile is not basking on the bank".     Fixed 2026-11-25 (branch `claude/flaky-fixes`, overnight flaky-test session 1): the crocodiles read the world's sun (`level().isDay()`) while six crocodile tests set the whole world's clock (`helper.setDayTime`), so the last one to set it decided day or night for every crocodile test beside it. Now `CrocodileEntity.sunTime()` asks the shared clock (`Colony.isSunUp` at `Colony.dayTimeAt`, each test arena its own clock) and the six tests use `TestClock.still` (noon 12.0, midnight 0.0). That arena-own midnight then let the school test's two crocodiles court (breeding time is night by the shared clock) and the female left to nest (`MotherGoal`): both are now set up as grown males. Also: a crocodile heads for basking company only if it has a route there (`AshoreGoal`, as the sand branch already did). Beside each other (the five crocodile tests, 6 servers, 4 runs) `crocodilesSpawnApartAndBaskTogether` and `aCrocodileSnapsUpFishAndCallsOthersToTheSchool` (after the males fix: 12 of 12) pass; alone 6 of 6 each; in the `river,life` run (6 servers) all five crocodile tests passed (that run's 4 failures were other tests: the distichodus grazing, the two monitor climbs of BUGS 292/301, the playing otters' nose touch). `aCrocodileSwimsToASandBankToBask` is not fixed, see BUGS 287. **Lesson:** an animal that goes by day and night asks the one shared clock, never the world's; a test that moves the clock for one arena uses `TestClock`, and a night clock also wakes night breeding.
306. **Open: the Matabele raids are broken and inconsistent in play** (the user, 2026-11-23: "the march works but the raids do not"; they moved on after two weeks of patching, so M8 was marked closed on 2026-11-10 though the raids never worked reliably). The raid code (about 6,500 lines, 119 tuning numbers) grew by more than a dozen fix rounds, each patching the last; the open raid bugs (93, 103, 111, 158, 166, 168, 171, 189, 197, 198, 202, 212, 281, the raid crash in 303) are its symptoms. Not patched again: rewritten from scratch by a Fable session, `docs/plans/raid-rewrite.md` (keeps the march, the trails, the fight, rescue, the prey pile and the termites' side). Lesson: when a part needs a fix for its fix week after week, stop and rewrite it; and moving on is not always a pass: ask once when the user leaves a feature that had open bugs.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** steps 1 to 5 of the rewrite are built (`docs/plans/raid-rewrite.md`): one raid machine (`colony/Raid`) on the kept march, with or without bodies and saved through a reload, the scouts' bookkeeping in `colony/Scouts`, the old column and its unused numbers deleted.
