308. **Open: a Matabele minor on the harvester's old guard scenes shows small body faults the harvester did not** (found 2026-11-23 by the harvester removal's step 3, branch `claude/harvester-removal-49898c`: the six walker scenes were re-filmed with a Matabele minor, a body never filmed on them before). (a) `a_walk`: at the change from the normal walk to the fast one a front or hind leg moves 0.06 blocks in a tick (usually 0.02), 5 times: a small pop at the pace change. (b) `c_climb`: at the top of the pillar the head, antennae and jaws jump about 0.11 blocks in a tick (usually 0.03), and starting down the body tips 30 degrees in one tick (55 to 85). (c) `e_ceiling`: the known snap onto and off a ceiling (30 degrees a tick, the harvester's approved known bug) puts more of this longer body in the roof: head, front legs and antennae up to 0.22 blocks (the harvester 0.15), the gaster and hind legs too for a few ticks. `d_ledge`, `b_turn`, `n_down_turn` and the crowd show nothing new (the down-turn 12 small foot jumps, worst 0.11). Not fixed here (its own step, the Matabele's body, not the removal). The ceiling scene's first burst went from 150 to 190 ticks: the minor walks the wall and roof at 1.1 blocks a second and had not reached its place when the next order came. Lesson: a guard scene proves a body only for the body filmed; a new body on an old scene is a new test.

309. **Fixed 2026-11-23: with the desert mod gone the termites' underground hung over holes, because Deep Soil was the desert mod's block** (found by the harvester removal's full run after step 4, branch `claude/harvester-removal-49898c`: `replay_soak4_termite_in_cast_going_home` and `replay_bug145_termite_in_feeding_cast` failed alone 3 of 3, termites stuck or floating 3 to 7 blocks under the ground, and both passed on the code before the removal). The harvester's mod also laid Deep Soil, 5 to 7 soft layers under every dirt surface in every overworld biome, and the termites' rooms, galleries and foraging tunnels had been running through it all along (vanilla dirt is only 3 or 4 thick, stone under it cannot be dug). Deleting the mod took the block out of the game: saved places lost it (air where it was), and a new world would have had stone there. The removal's own lists missed it twice: step 1 looked for desert names in code and readable files only, and my check of the replays read the packed `.nbt` files without unpacking them. Fix: Deep Soil is now the framework's own block and world feature (`colonyframework:deep_soil`, `block/DeepSoil`, `worldgen/DeepSoilFeature`, in `colonyframework:diggable`), same look, same layers; the 14 replay places and the `raid_bumps` test place name the new block. Lesson: before deleting a mod, list what it puts into the world for everyone (world features, shared tags), not only who names its classes, and unpack packed files before searching them.
310. **Open: `aStandingDeadTreeIsSensedAndGetsAGalleryToItsFoot` fails by chance, about 1 run in 4** (found 2026-11-23 by the harvester removal's step 5 run, branch `claude/harvester-removal-49898c`, 6 servers: "no way out to the dead tree's foot ... sensed 0 ... not in the plant scan, the colony does not know it", failed alone 2 of 3). Not from the removal: run 10 times each on the code before step 5 and after it, it failed 2 of 10 before and 1 of 10 after, the same way. The colony never senses the dead giant in those runs; a likely place is where the test sets the tree down (its nearest open wood 13 to 16 blocks from the nearest hole, the holes differing run to run) against the sensing reach. Box 1 or 3 of the 4-box rule, not looked into.
    - **Fixed 2026-11-25 (overnight flaky-test session 4, branch `claude/flaky-fixes`; box 1, a real bug in the game): the dead giant was judged by one block 20.02 to 20.10 blocks off against a reach of 20.** Logged (each measure's start, blocks, foot, reach; the listening holes): in every failure the big tree was measured whole enough (about 420 blocks, reach 20), every hole was listening, but `DeadWoodCall.listen` asked only whether the wood's **foot** was in reach, and the foot is the grounded block nearest the colony's middle, not the hole: on a tree 21 blocks from its nearest hole it stood 4 blocks round the trunk, just past 20, while the tree's near side lay about 16 off. First try (the test measuring only from listening holes) was wrong: all 30 holes listened. Fix: the wood is heard if **any** of its blocks lying open on the ground is within reach of a hole or tip (`Wood.grounded`), as the colony smells the wood itself; the foot it learns is unchanged. Real termites sense the wood from its nearest part, so this changes nothing a real colony would not do; it lets a big tree be heard up to its own width farther than before. Proof: alone 12 of 12 passed after the fix (5 of 12 failed before it, tonight's two launches).
311. **Open: the full run at the end of the harvester removal (2026-11-24, branch `claude/harvester-removal-49898c`, 357 tests, 6 servers, 38 minutes): 349 passed, 8 failed beside others, none of them a test the removal moved or code it touched.** New: `anOtterWalkingThroughAnotherGoesRoundItsBody` ("the walking otter went 0.31 blocks into the standing one's body"), failed alone 1 of 3; a river animal the removal never touched, not looked into (box 1 or 3). Known ones again: `minorsBiteAChickenThatHuntsTheForagersWhoHurryHome` failed alone 2 of 3 (BUGS 207, the chicken alarm), `aStandingDeadTreeIsSensedAndGetsAGalleryToItsFoot` 1 of 3 (BUGS 310); passed alone 3 of 3: the tilapia breeding (BUGS 227), three crocodile tests (BUGS 244, 305) and the crabs' courting.
   - **Fixed 2026-11-25 (flaky-fixes session 3), the crabs' courting ("0 babies scattered"):** the babies are kept only while they ride (vanilla `requiresCustomPersistence` is `isPassenger`) and were despawned the tick they climbed off whenever a stand-in player was anywhere in the level; the shared cause, stand-in players left in the world, fixed at its source in `TestIsolation` (`letStandInPlayersGo`): each stand-in player goes once every test that was running when it came in is done. Found on the way: every passing test using `MatabeleDefenceTests.survivalPlayer` (the shark, Matabele and river tests) left its player behind, not only failing ones; any player in the level makes Minecraft despawn far-off young that are not kept (baby crabs off their mother's back, hatchlings, fry) and turns on the spawning caps of room to breed. No change to the crabs themselves (my pick: in a real world a player is near, so babies near the player stay as Minecraft's own young do). Passed in the two clean `life,river` runs.

    Fixed 2026-11-25 (branch `claude/flaky-fixes`, overnight flaky-test session 2): `anOtterWalkingThroughAnotherGoesRoundItsBody`: measured, not size (it failed at sizes 0.98 and 1.09 alike); the keep-apart rule pushes only once the bodies already overlap, so it reaches 0.16 to 0.32 (0.40 once) at any size, against a limit of 0.3 right in that spread. Box 3: the limit is now 0.45, still well short of the 0.6 and straight through without the rule; the sizes go into the log and the fail message. 6 of 6 alone, and the `river` run.
    **153 and 164, update 2026-11-24 (the raid rewrite's part b, run `raids,away` on 6 servers, branch `claude/raid-rewrite-edbbde`):** the same test failed once more, a third way: "the find at ... is not at the four termites at the east tunnel's end" (a find worth 60, 24 blocks out, 1 mound ringed, 1 tunnel followed, 1 find); alone it failed 1 of 3, and it passed in the two runs before on nearly the same code. Part b changed no scouting code (in `Raids.found` only when a diary line is written). Still not looked into: the scouts' pass of the big three's system-by-system work finds its cause.
313. **A watched raid's leader stood still 30 s on the ridge and the watch rule `raider-stuck` broke (found by a test run, 2026-11-24, raid rewrite step 4; open).** `aRaidOverARidgeAndRoundAPondRunsEveryStageInItsTimeAndBringsItsPreyToThePile` failed once in a `raids,away` run (before part c was merged in) and 1 of 3 times alone: in the attack the leader, a former scout, was told to a place 6 blocks off and did not move for 30 s ("no foe it may go for in reach: it searches round the spot", then going for termites 5.9 blocks off). It passed in the next run, after the merge with part c. A raid with bodies, code step 4 did not touch. Lesson: none yet; look at the leader's search place in the attack when it shows again.
    **93 and 212, update 2026-11-24 (the run `raids,away,soldiers` on 4 servers after the raid rewrite's step 4 was merged, branch `claude/raid-rewrite-edbbde`):** the dead-mound whole-chain test failed once the same way ("no carrier was out on the way home to kill") and passed alone 3 of 3. Measured in the failing run's `[raid]` diary: the raid was whole (70 out, 6 termites picked up, march home 23 s, 70 home, 6 on the pile), the find lay 17 blocks from the nest's middle and the march home's first place (the entrance side of the dead mound) about 10 blocks from the find. The test's hook kills a carrier only once it is 10 blocks or more from the find, so its window is the last step or two at the entrance, where an ant may already be going in: a race in the test's moment (box 3), not in the raid. The same run: `minorsBiteAChickenThatHuntsTheForagersWhoHurryHome` "the chicken is still alive (3.0 health)", failed alone 2 of 3 (BUGS 207; termite alarm code the rewrite does not touch). Next for 93/212: make the hook's distance a share of the trail's length (or kill the carrier at a set place along the way home) instead of 10 blocks.
    - **The raid rewrite, step 5 (2026-11-24):** again in a step 5 run (part 2, a change to no walking code), here as the watch rule `not-stuck` (a major with a route not moving 30 s); failed alone 1 of 3. Still open.

315. **Open: a second raid, begun right after the first turned home with its scout killed, stood in its muster 25 s (deadline 20 s) (found by a test run, 2026-11-24, raid rewrite step 5, branch `claude/raid-rewrite-step5-e14ef4`; not fixed: the rule, bugs found by test runs go on the list).** `aRaidWhoseScoutIsKilledOnTheMarchOutTurnsHomeAndNobodyIsLeftOut` failed once in a `raids,away,soldiers` run on 3 servers ("the raid hung in muster for 25 s"), and passed alone 3 of 3; it passed in the two runs before (after parts 1 and 2). The story it printed: muster 6 s, march out 5 s ("turns home: the scout is lost, and nobody else knows the way out"), march home 0 s ("all home"), done, then a new muster of 30 ants that lasted 25 s; the colony's line at that moment says "a raid of 29 (29 real out), muster, 0 s in this stage" and "1 scout(s) out". Two things to look at, read from that line and not tried: the march home ended in 0 s with 29 ants only 5 s out along the trail (all counted home at once?), and the new raid's own clock said 0 s in muster while the test had counted 25 s (the muster begun again each second, or a new raid made each second for the same find). First seen in the run after part 3 (the scouts' bookkeeping moved into `colony/Scouts`, a move) and the start crash fix (a march line of one point gets the find as its second); neither is known to touch a muster, but they are the nearest change, so step 6 should run this test alone about 20 times (`ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1`).
    **315, update 2026-11-24 (looked into when step 5 was merged into `claude/raid-rewrite-edbbde`):** most likely the test's own clock, not the raid (box 3). The chain tests' stage book (`MatabeleRaidChainTests`, `began.putIfAbsent(stage, tick)`) keeps the first tick each stage began, so a second raid of the same colony is timed from the first raid's muster: begun about 25 s after it, it "hung in muster for 25 s" on its first tick, which is the "0 s in this stage" the colony's line showed. The second raid is real behaviour: the scene's colony sends two scouts by itself, and in a passing run's diary the second one took the same termites 27 s after the first muster (the test had ended by then); when it is a little quicker the test is still watching. The "march home 0 s, all home" line is in passing runs too: when the scout is lost the raid ends at once and its ants walk home one by one (the test's own check that nobody is left out passed); the diary's words "29 home" say more than is true at that moment and want a look in the review. Next: the book should begin anew for a new raid (or the test end at the first raid's "done"); no need for the 20 runs alone.
316. **A scout setting out could freeze the game: the termites' feeding places were read in land not loaded** (review round 11, R11-1, 2026-11-24; found by reading, never reported from play). A Matabele scout asks every termite colony within about 133 blocks for its by-day feeding places at the start of every trip, and a termite colony out of range keeps its record of its ways to food; `FeedingSpots.galleryByDay` read the block over each gallery cell, `FoodStore.isFeedableFromInside` the cells beside the food and `FeedingGallery.joinCasts` each gallery cell, none asking `LandReady` first, so a gallery in land not loaded was loaded or made new on the spot. The same path froze the game 1 to 3 s on 2026-11-10 (`Mound.footReach`'s comment); that fix covered the mound's shape only. Fix: the three reads ask `LandReady` first; a feeding place in land not loaded is no by-day place now. Test (failed first): `aFeedingPlaceInLandNotLoadedIsNeverRead` (a living colony with a feeding tunnel 4,000 blocks off; after the scout's question that land is still not loaded; the test first proves it can see a read). Lesson: a guard put at the place that froze is not a guard on the question; every block read behind a question another colony may ask from far away needs its own "is this land loaded".
317. **A scout with no way on searched for a route every tick** (review round 11, R11-2, 2026-11-24; found by reading). `MatabeleScoutGoal` asked its legs again on every tick the navigator was done, with no wait after a failed search (BUGS 76's rule, which the raid goal and the way-home goal keep): shut in a box it asked for 40 and more routes in 10 ticks. Fix: one retry clock in the goal (`walkAgainAt`, half a second, my pick): after a try that found no way anywhere (the search, the look at a find it cannot get near, the walk home) it stands that long; and a point with no way to it is skipped one a try, never a row of them in one tick. Measured in the test: about 152 routes in 200 ticks shut in, where it was about 800. Test (failed first): `aScoutWithNoWayOnAsksForARouteOnlyNowAndThen`. What a failed search costs in milliseconds is still not measured (the perf run). Seen in play: a scout that finds no way stands half a second between tries, so a hemmed-in one takes some seconds to give up, not under a second.
318. **Attacks ran to their 2-minute deadline: "goes for a termite" kept the quiet rule awake** (review round 11, R11-3, 2026-11-24; found by reading; it fits the old diaries' long attacks). The attack ends `quiet_ticks` (15 s) after the last work at the spot, but every fight diary line except four counted as work, "goes for X" among them, and the shared fight goes for a termite it has no way to again every 10 s (`FightGoal.UNREACHABLE_TICKS` 200). Fix: `Raid.worksAtTheSpot`: only what changes something counts (a grip, a sting or a miss at a held termite, jaws sliding off, a termite twisting away, a lost grip, a breach picked or bitten open); going for a termite, a grab at one already stung or held, and jaws closing on nothing do not. No number added. Test (failed first): `RaidRulesTest.onlyWhatChangesSomethingAtTheSpotKeepsTheAttackAwake`. For the soak to show: how long attacks last now.
319. **A raid the player is watching was folded into numbers when its nest's land went out of range** (review round 11, R11-4, 2026-11-24; found by reading; it needs a view distance of about 8 chunks or less and a raid far out). A colony whose nest's chunk is not loaded counts as away whoever is near its raiders, and `Raids.tickAway` sent the raid on as numbers a minute later: every ant vanished. Fix: `Raids.watchedOut`: a raid with a watcher within 64 blocks of its front or tail keeps its bodies while its colony is away (it is stepped once a second as before); with nobody near it goes on as numbers at the next look. Test (failed first): `aRaidAWatcherIsNearKeepsItsBodiesWhenItsNestGoesOutOfRange`. Not covered: the first minute after the nest's land goes (review R11-11: nobody calls dusk or rain in the colony's grace minute).
320. **A raid out as numbers took the real termites standing in front of a player** (review round 11, R11-5, 2026-11-24; found by reading). A player arriving right beside an unseen raid (a teleport, a login, fast flight) meets a raid that stays numbers (within 50 blocks its ants would be seen appearing), and its hidden catch (`Raid.takeHidden`) carried off the termites in the open: termites vanishing with no ant in sight. Fix (the user's yes to my pick, 2026-11-24): with somebody who can see within 50 blocks of the spot, the hidden catch leaves the termites in the open alone and comes from under the mud only (the termite colony's numbers). Test (failed first): `aRaidOutOfSightTakesNoTermiteStandingInFrontOfAWatcher`.
321. **Termite mounds of new land were lost when the game was left, and could turn up in the next world** (review round 8, R8-11, 2026-11-24; found by reading). World making hands each mound's place to the server through a list outside the world (`NewLandMounds.QUEUE`), looked at only every 10th tick of a ticking world, keyed by the dimension's name, never saved and never emptied. Quit while land was still being made (the world stops ticking when the pause menu opens) and the last strip's mounds were gone for good; open another world in the same sitting and they were put in there, at the old coordinates, in any biome. Fix: each place is kept with its own world; when a world closes, after its last save, the places still waiting go into its book and the book is written again (`NewLandMounds.closing`, `LevelEvent.Unload`); the list is emptied when the server has stopped. Test: `aMoundHandedOverAsTheWorldClosesIsNotLost`. **Lesson: anything `static` that world making fills must be tied to its world and emptied when the world closes; "saved every few ticks" is not saved when the world stops ticking.**
322. **Ravines and caves still cut into the flat savanna along its rivers** (review round 8, R8-18, 2026-11-24; found by reading; "no ravines or caves in the savanna", the user, 2026-11-07). The rule that takes the carvers out named `#minecraft:is_savanna`; the savanna's own river (added the same week) is not in that tag and listed all three carvers, and a ravine started in a river chunk runs on for about 100 blocks into the plain. Fix: the river's file lists none and the rule names `#antcolonies:savanna_waters`. Test: `noRavinesOrCavesStartInTheSavannaOrItsWaters` (as the game sees the biomes, after every modifier). New land only. **Lesson: a rule written for a tag must be checked against every biome added after it.**
323. **A courted animal whose suitor died or left the loaded land stayed "taken" for good** (review R9-1 and R10-2, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The courting mark on the one courted was only taken off by the suitor's own goal, which never runs again for a dead or unloaded animal: a female crocodile never hunted or mated again, a tilapia male's nest pit never bred. Fix: the mark proves itself, `AnimalLife.partner()` drops it when the mate is dead, gone or not naming this animal back. Test: `aCourtedAnimalWhoseSuitorDiesOrVanishesIsFreeAgain`. **Lesson: a mark one animal puts on another must not depend on the first one's goal ending well; let the mark check itself.**
324. **The crocodile hunted the player's own animals** (review R9-5, 2026-11-24; found by reading). "No tame pets" knew only wolves, cats and parrots; a tamed horse, donkey or llama, and any named or leashed farm animal, was prey (small ones swallowed with no drops). Fix: `CrocodileEntity.playersOwn` (owned, named, on a lead, saddled). Test: `theCrocodileLeavesThePlayersOwnAnimalsAlone`.
325. **In Peaceful the crocodile still seized the player and rolled them twelve times** (review R9-6, 2026-11-24; found by reading). Its grab is not damage, so Minecraft's Peaceful rule did not stop it. Fix (my pick): in Peaceful it does not hunt players and a nest mother only hisses. No test.
326. **A catch in an otter's jaws at a save was stuck at its snout for good** (review R9-27, 2026-11-24; found by reading). Minecraft saves a rider with its mount, helpless as it was held; the otter's own note of it was not saved. Fix: an otter with a rider it does not know of lets it go properly. Test: `aCatchSavedInAnOttersJawsIsLetGoAfterALoad`. **Lesson: anything made helpless while it rides (will off, cannot be hurt) must be freed by whoever finds it riding, not only by the one who remembers taking it.**
327. **An otter with a catch and no bank in reach hung in mid-river for ever** (review R9-28, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The carry had no time limit and searched for a bank ten times a second. Fix: 30 seconds at most in the water, then it eats afloat; the search once a second. No test.
328. **An otter whose playmate was killed or removed mid-game stood in the game for good** (review R9-29, 2026-11-24; found by reading). Only the otter that started a game checked whether it was still on. Fix: either otter's play goal ends a game that is no longer on. Test: `anOtterWhosePlaymateVanishesMidGameIsFreeAgain`.
329. **Otters vanished one at a time 32 blocks from the player** (review R9-30, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The only river animal without the "kept within 96 blocks" rule. Fix: the same rule as the others. Test: `anOtterIsKeptWithin96BlocksOfAPlayer`.
330. **A monitor froze before a step whose middle block was not a whole block** (review R9-37, 2026-11-24; found by reading; half fixed). The room check asked for whole blocks under the two feet only, the move for one on the middle line, so the move started and was cut every tick. Fix: the room checks ask the middle line too. Tried and taken back: letting a first-tick cut fall through to ordinary walking (it broke the angled climb one run in six). Still open: a first-tick cut for any other cause; the cure is the shared `MoveRider` (R9-42). **Lesson: "is there room" and the move itself must ask about the same blocks.**
331. **A bigger monitor's walk into the water was cut 9 to 36 ticks short** (review R9-38, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The move file had one length (the smallest size's) while each size's path is longer. Fix: a move's length is its own size's row (`BlockMoves.Move.ticks(size)`). Tests: `BlockMovesFileTest`, `aBiggerMonitorsWalkIntoTheWaterIsNotCutShort`; filmed at size 1.15 from one side.
332. **Open: `aTadpoleBesideFourFrogsDoesNotBecomeAFifth` failed twice beside others, "5 bullfrogs (4 wanted: no froglet beside the 4)"** (found 2026-11-24 by the world and river runs for the review fixes, branch `claude/review-fixes`, 6 servers; once before any animal code had changed; passed alone 3 of 3). Not looked into. Likely box 4 (something beside it: a frog of a neighbouring test counted, or a fifth spawned by it).
333. **A bullfrog climbing out of the water could be left hanging with no gravity for good** (review R10-8, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The climb switched gravity off and only the climb's own tick switched it back; a puff at a player, a female's clasp or a save in those 16 ticks took the climb away, and Minecraft saves the no-gravity mark. Fix: in the frog's tick, "no gravity" is taken off whenever its action is not the climb out, "no air drag" whenever it is not the leap. Test: `aBullfrogLeftWithoutGravityGetsItBack`. **Lesson: a mark Minecraft saves (no gravity, no will, cannot be hurt) must be tied to the state that needs it by a check every tick, not by that state's own ending.**
334. **Every tadpole sent every player a packet every tick** (review R10-9, 2026-11-24; found by reading). Its age was synced data counted up each tick. Fix: the age is the server's own count; one "has legs" flag is synced. **Lesson: never sync a counter; sync the fact the players' side needs.**
335. **Crabs, snails and catfish never grew up and bred only once in their lives** (review R10-12 and R9-41, 2026-11-24; found by reading). Each animal had to call its life's clock itself and these three (and the hippo and the monitor) never did. Fix: the kit counts every animal's life clock from one place (`AnimalLife.tickEvery`), with `HasLife.grew()` for an animal that changes its look. Test: `everyAnimalWithALifeGrowsUpAndRestsAfterBreeding`. **Lesson: what every animal must do each tick belongs in one shared place, not in a line each new animal has to remember.**
336. **A crab whose first bite took the last of the food was hungry again in seconds** (review R10-13, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The meal counted only from the second bite on, so on thin algae or last scraps it stripped patch after patch. Fix: a meal with one bite down counts. Test: `aCrabWhoseFirstBiteTakesTheLastOfTheFoodIsFed`. (A first try set the hunger again as the goal stopped and broke `aHungryCrabPicksRemainsBeforeAlgae`; the hunger is set there only for a meal not already counted.)
337. **With a player playing, river animals almost never bred** (review R10-1, 2026-11-24; found by reading). Breeding asked whether the animal's spawning group was under its cap, the same room Minecraft's spawner fills every tick, so the spawner always won; in spectator the question was switched off, so the user watching in spectator saw breeding that a playing friend never would, and the tests (no player) took the spectator's path. A tilapia mother with no room on the one tick her eggs were due lost the whole brood. Fix (the user: breeding refills the river): breeding and hatching ask only the kind's local limit; the spawner fills a breeding kind to half its count; the mother keeps her eggs and asks again. Tests: `aTilapiaMotherWithNoRoomKeepsHerEggsUntilThereIs`, `theSpawnerLeavesHalfOfABreedingKindsCountToBreeding`. **Lesson: two rules must never fill the same room; give each place one owner. And a rule that answers differently for a spectator is a rule the tests and the user's watching both miss.**
338. **Open: three breeding tests failed together beside other tests, and the catfish's hidden-barb test failed once alone** (found 2026-11-24 by the `river,life` run for review R10-1 on 4 servers, branch `claude/review-fixes`). `tilapiaBreedInANestPitAndTheMotherKeepsHerFryInHerMouth` "a player beside her and her fry not taken into her mouth", `crocodilesCourtNestAndTheMotherCarriesHerYoungToTheWater` "0 hatchlings from 9 eggs", `crabsCourtFaceToFaceAndTheBabiesRideOnTheirMother` "0 babies scattered from 5": all on one server, each passed alone 3 of 3. Likely cause (read, not proven): the pretend watcher is one list for the whole server (`Watchers.setPretend`), and these tests of the `breeding` batch set and clear it over each other; the cure would be a pretend watcher each test adds and takes away for itself. `aCatfishPassesOverABarbHiddenInTheReeds` "the catfish has not taken the barb in open water (12.6 blocks off)": failed alone 1 of 3 in the rerun, then passed 8 of 8 alone; cause not looked for. Also in the run, known: BUGS 244, 255, 287.
339. **A big tree coming back after many days away counted the ground under its whole crown once for every missed day** (review R7-1, 2026-11-24; found by reading; the hitch was estimated, not measured). Fix: counted once per catch-up, the number kept as branches fall. **Lesson: a catch-up loop must not redo a whole-area look each turn; look once, then keep count.**
340. **A fire could eat a living giant's crown, which never greened again** (review R7-10, 2026-11-24; found by reading, likely). Fire scorched a green leaf brown and brown leaves burn away fast, so the fire made its own fuel. Fix (the user: scorch, then regreen): a scorched leaf is marked, cannot burn away and greens again by itself. **Lesson: when one rule turns a block into another, check what every other rule does to the new block.**
341. **A falling tree broke a slot through a termite mound** (review R7-11, 2026-11-24; found by reading). Mound blocks are softer than stone so a player can dig them, and the fall smashes anything softer than stone. Fix (the user: the log rests on it): a block tag of what a falling tree never breaks. **Lesson: hardness is a player's digging number; never use it alone to decide what the world may break.**
342. **Half fixed: giant trees of land made just before quitting were never in the tree book and stood frozen for good** (review R7-15, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The waiting list lived only in memory and was read once a second. Fix: taken in as the world closes, emptied at server stop. Open: a crash, and worlds already hit. **Lesson (again, BUGS 321): anything handed from world making to a book must be taken in before the world closes.**
343. **A rot-killed log that fell uphill left wood hanging in the air after it crumbled** (review R7-16, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The crumbling took its columns' heights from the flat-ground shape, not the log as laid on its land. Fix: the laid shape's heights. **Lesson: whatever is placed through `rest` must be taken away through `rest`.**
344. **A dead sapling left a thin branch post sunk in a pit in the grass** (review R7-17, 2026-11-24; found by reading). A thin stem starts one block under the ground and removing a tree skipped everything under the ground. Fix: the soil closes over a thin stem.
345. **Every fig branch was a full solid cube** (review R7-27, 2026-11-24; found by reading). The shape's sum was done in whole numbers and came out 0. Fix: decimals. **Lesson: a test of a shape must check the shape, not the number it is made from.**
346. **Shears on a bare fruit bunch gave endless fruit** (review R7-28, 2026-11-24; found by reading). A bunch placed by hand started ripe, and shears give a bunch back at any stage. Fix: placed by hand it starts bare.
347. **Animals walked straight into thorny twigs** (review R7-29, 2026-11-24; found by reading). Minecraft's path planner knows berry bushes by name; any other hurting block must say so itself. Fix: the twig says it hurts. **Lesson: a block that hurts needs `getBlockPathType`.**
348. **Open: the full run after the four review-fix batches, 9 failures of 390, none failing alone but one known chance failure** (found 2026-11-24, branch `claude/review-fixes`, 8 servers, 33 min; each rerun alone 3 times). New: `aQueuedMoundIsGrownAsideThenPutInQuickly` "its dead tree still waits" (passed alone 3 of 3; it reads the whole world's count of waiting trees, `treesWaitingForTests`, so another new-land test beside it can leave one waiting; likely the review's own `aMoundHandedOverAsTheWorldClosesIsNotLost`, not proven); `aCalmTermiteNestStandsStillMostOfTheTime` "idle insects walk too much: termite_worker still 73%" (3 of 3 alone); `fishCrowdRoundAHipposDungInItsPool` "0 barbs and 2 tilapia" (3 of 3 alone). Known: `crocodilesCourtNest...` and `crabsCourtFaceToFace...` together again (BUGS 338), `aSnailAndATadpoleGrazeDyingHippoGrass`, `aCrocodileSwimsToASandBankToBask` (BUGS 244), `aMonitorWhoseStepIsBrokenMidClimbFallsBack` (failed alone 1 of 3; BUGS 255, 290). The replay `replay_soak4_raider_going_home` failed as a not-required test (the raid rewrite's step 6).
349. **Fixed (2026-11-24; the fix and what was measured at the end of this entry). Was: open: the soak's 16 "not-stuck" alarms are wound care, not stuck ants; but the care happens in the nest's doorway** (found 2026-11-24 by reading all 5 not-stuck reports of the raid rewrite's soak, `docs/soaks/2026-11-24-raid-rewrite-4x2.txt`; proven in the reports). A raider home with a termite clamped on is held still by the nestmate taking it off (`Clamp.holdStillFor`) while its `GoInsideGoal` still holds a route, so the rule sees "a route, not moving 30 s". In every report a nestmate with `ClampCareGoal` stands beside the stuck ant for the same seconds. Three things to settle: the rule should not count an insect held still for care; the patient is treated on the first nest cell under the dead mound's shaft, where every ant coming home must pass (up to 5 ants on one cell, one standing half a block up on another); up to 10 nestmates walk to one patient at once (a carer is only written down on arrival). **Measured before the fix** (four readers over the code, the 5 soak reports and the replays, 2026-11-24): every soak not-stuck alarm at a nest was a clamped raider held for care (a dead head in every case checked); the patient was held on the shaft's foot or the 1-wide tunnel, frozen with its walk to the rooms still planned (`AbstractAntEntity.isImmobile` skips the whole mind and legs, so the route stays); one care round lasts about 60 s, not the 30 s written (BUGS 357), so the 30 s rule always tripped; and far more carers came than 10: 35 at once in soak 3, 12 in soaks 1 and 4, 25 in the replay, all for one patient (the claim was written only on arrival; when one carer got there the rest dropped out within 3 s). Real Megaponera: the papers say only "in the nest" (Frank et al. 2017, 2018, 2023; lab nests with no chambers); the mod's design (the user's pick) is that the hurt rest and are tended in a room near the entrance. **The fix (framework, both care goals):** (1) a carer claims its patient when it picks it (`AbstractAntEntity.claimCare`, one claim for each kind of care, `CareJob.CLAMP` and `WOUND`, so a head and a wound are still tended side by side; renewed every goal tick, `CARE_CLAIM_TICKS` 40, freed when done or given up); (2) a patient is picked, and held still, only once it has come to rest in a room (`restsForCare`: not carried, its legs stopped, on a room cell); at its side the carer waits for that, the wait counting toward its give-up time. No change to the watch rule: a patient is never held with a route now. **Proof:** new test `MatabeleFoodTests.aClampedAntHomeIsCaredForInTheRoomsByOneNestmate` (a worker with a dead head set down at the entrance: never held outside the rooms or while walking, one carer, the head off; passes 3 of 3, fails 2 of 2 on the old code with 2 and 3 carers; in this small test nest the patient reached the rooms before any carer looked, so the place half is proven by the replays, not the test). Both soak replays (`bug350_*`) pass 3 of 3 runs for the first time; a temporary log showed all 16 care rounds there on a room cell with the patient standing. `colony`, `soldiers`, `raids`, `away` pass. An independent review (three readers, each finding checked by a skeptic) confirmed one fault in the first version, one claim shared by both kinds of care (a lick could wait for a 60 s head removal and miss the infection's deadline), fixed by the claim per kind. **Left, as their own bugs:** 357 (care counters at half speed), 358 (the dead mound's entrance floats above the worn hump), 359 (homecomers stop on the room's first cell, so care happens there).
350. **Fixed by BUGS 354 (2026-11-24; the cause was the go-home rule, not being hurt). Was: hurt Matabele sent home by themselves stand outside for minutes or hours** (found 2026-11-24, the soak's raider-stuck "left outside with nothing to do there" 8 times and both home-by-night; cause not proven). Two kinds. An ant with **legs gone** "stops and calls for help", then 2 s later is told to walk home along a trail and stands (servers 3 and 4). An ant with a **living termite clamped on** (slowed 75 percent) walks a few blocks and then stands at one spot, its height flickering between 71.8 and 72.0: three of them at exactly one spot by the termite mound from 19:09 until after 01:20, home only by 03:56 (server 1). On server 4 a healthy ant ("left behind: not at the halt in time") stood stuck on the very spot a clamped one stuck on later (2079.9, 73.9, 2040.9, on the side of its own nest mound), so the spot itself may be at fault, not only the slowing. Next: replay the saved places (`run-soak/<n>/reports/*raider-stuck/place.nbt`) and watch one ant.
351. **Fixed by BUGS 360 (2026-11-25: each raid follows its scout's newest line; in the replay the next raid reached its halting place in 20 s, attacked and carried termites home). Was: cause found, not fixed (2026-11-24; two tries set aside). Was: open: a raid's leader finds no route to its next place on the march, the whole column waits, and the raid goes home on its deadline with nothing** (found 2026-11-24, the soak's raider-stuck "told to a spot 5 or 6 off, no route (last search failed)" 4 times; cause not proven). On server 2 two raids on different days stopped at the same cell (18661, 72, 2283, the next place 2 blocks higher at 18666, 74, 2281) and three raids there ended "the march's time is up, 71 s for 41 blocks". The deadline did its job (the raid ended and said why), but the same find is raided again and fails again. Not known: what stands at that place. Next: the saved place of `run-soak/2/reports/*raider-stuck`.
352. **Fixed by BUGS 356 (2026-11-24; the column stood at a loop in its line, the deadline and the pace were right). Was: open: the march home's deadline cut off a column that was still marching** (found 2026-11-24, the soak; cause not proven). Server 1, day 1.3: 27 ants on the way home, the hindmost "walking true" 42.7 blocks along after 90 s, "ends after 100 s: its time is up with 27 still out", `done: 0 home, 27 let go` (the land had been unloaded and the ants had just come back into sight). Server 3: stage-limit, march home 94 s against its longest of 60 s. Either the march is slower than the deadline assumes (2.5 blocks a second designed) or the deadline does not count the time out of sight; to measure before changing either number.
353. **350's half fixed by BUGS 354 (2026-11-24; not the legs: the go-home rule); 351 open. Was: open, with replays: BUGS 350 and 351 replayed from the soak's saved places (2026-11-24); both come from the place, not from the dice or from being hurt.** Replays `bug350_stuck_on_nest_mound`, `bug350_clamped_left_outside`, `bug351_leader_no_route` (known bugs, not required; 5 minutes for the three). **350:** in 3 runs of 3 a Matabele going home by itself stood stuck at 2079.8, 74.0, 2040.9, and it was a different ant from the soak's two (a major, not clamped): it climbs the one-block wall of its own nest mound (`termite_mound` at 2079, 73, 2041, open air on top) and stands at the top edge with a route (to 2077, 79, 2043), never stepping over. At the other place, 3 of 3 again, ants stand at 2060, 72, 2148: the lip of a pit the termites dug under a fallen branch (air at y 71, grass blocks round it, a `fallen_branch` in the cell above, `mud_crust` beside). So: an ant at the top edge of a one-block climb does not get over the lip at these spots; the slowing of a clamp is not needed for it. The fault is in the legs (`AntNavigator`, the edge-over move); not read yet (read `docs/BUGS.md` on the legs first). **351:** the leader again had no route from 18661, 72, 2283 to its place 18666, 74, 2281. The saved land shows a fallen dead tree there with the termites' mud crust on it; the crust under the place has side faces only (no crust in the place has a top face), so the march's route has a point with nothing to stand on. Not known: how that point got into the route (the scout's own walk over the crust, or the route maker).
354. **Fixed (2026-11-24): the cause of BUGS 350 and 353: an ant going home was given a new walk every tick or two, so a step over an edge never finished; it was the goal, not the legs.** Found with the replays and a log line naming who starts each walk: the stuck ant on its nest mound got 16,693 new walks in 5 minutes, all from `MatabeleWayHomeGoal`, none from the legs (0 replans, the step's clock never past 1 tick). The goal asked every tick where to walk (`Raids.wayHomeOrder`: a place 6 blocks ahead on the trail, or the entrance) and began a new walk whenever the answer lay 1.5 blocks from the last one. Where the answer jumps, an ant shifting a twentieth of a block got two answers in turn: on the mound "the trail's last point" and "the entrance" (the trail's end), at the termite pit two trail points 4.5 blocks apart (beside a bend, where the nearest piece of the trail changes). A step over an edge begins by backing off a little, which was just the shift that flipped the answer back: each new walk began the step again. Also seen: on plain ground the same rule dropped 11 walks of 12 for a new one, a route search every block or two for nothing. **The fix (one rule less):** a walk under way is walked to its end, and only then is the next place asked for (`MOVED`, `walkingTo`, `moved`, `settled`, `idleAndOff` deleted). **Proof:** both replays, 3 runs of 3: the ants now cross both spots and reach the nest (new walks from the goal: 16,693 down to 18 for the same ant). The replays stay known bugs: what they still show is BUGS 349 (care in the doorway) and 352 (the march home standing until its deadline; new there: the column's hindmost was off as a scout, `goals [MatabeleScoutGoal]`, while the front waited for it). New test `MatabeleRaidTests.anAntGoingHomeWalksEachWalkToItsEnd` (fails on the old rule: 11 of its 12 walks dropped). **Lessons:** (1) my guess before this was the legs' edge-over move, and before that the clamp; both wrong: log who starts each walk before reading the legs. (2) A rule that asks afresh every tick and acts on every change needs the answer to be steady; finishing what is under way needs nothing. `MatabeleRaidGoal` has the same rule for a raider's place (its places are halts, which do not jump; left alone, to watch).
356. **Fixed (2026-11-24): the cause of BUGS 352: a column's front stood for good where the scout's line came back to its own cell, and the whole column behind it till the deadline let everyone go.** Measured first: the march itself is as fast as designed (the soak's column did 23 blocks in 10 s once it moved), so neither the pace nor the deadline was wrong. In the soak and in the replay the front stood at the same cell, 2066, 71, 2145, "its place 0.0 off, walking false", 11 along, for 60 s and more. A log of the route there showed the scout's line going east to that cell (10.9 along), two blocks north and back to the same cell (14.9 along), then on east. The front is told to a place 6 along ahead and moves its aim on once it counts as within 3 of it; "how far along" is the nearest place on the line (`Route.along`), and on the cell where the loop closes that is the first pass. So the front stood on its place, counted as 3.7 short of it, and never got a new one. (The same loops made an ant's place on the way home jump, BUGS 354.) **The fix:** `Route` leaves the loops of its line out (`Route.withoutLoops`: where a later point is in the same cell as an earlier one or the one beside it, no more than a block up or down, what lies between is dropped), for the raid's routes and the way home's alike. Real side: the trail a column follows is the one the scout lays on its way home, a fairly straight line; an ant on a trail that meets itself goes on. **Proof:** both replays, 3 runs of 3: the march home ends "all home" in 68 s and 49 s (before: stood till its time was up, all let go); the raider-stuck breaches are gone; what the replays still show is BUGS 349 only (care in the doorway). Unit test `RouteTest` (the soak's own line). **Seen on the way, open, small:** after a reload an ant's goals ask "am I a raider" before the colony's first tick has read the saved raid back (up to a second), and in the replay one raider was made a scout in that window (`scout 4e96b2c4 sets out` at tick 13157, the raid read back at 13176); it came home with the column all the same. **Lesson:** the bug note guessed a slow march or a short deadline; ten lines of the diary (the front's "along" not changing while it stood on its place) said neither. Read the stage's own lines before touching its numbers. **The test run after it** (`raids,away,legs`, 4 servers, 77 tests): three failures, all three known chance failures seen again, none new: BUGS 164 (`aScoutRingsALivingMound...`, failed alone 1 of 3), 313 (`aRaidOverARidgeAndRoundAPond...`, a raider still 30 s; passed alone 3 of 3), 315 (`aRaidWhoseScoutIsKilled...`, the second muster 31 s; passed alone 3 of 3). The run before this fix passed all 77, so they are to be watched in the next runs.
357. **Open (found 2026-11-24 while fixing BUGS 349): the care goals count at half speed, so every care time is twice what the species file and the design say.** Minecraft ticks a goal that does not ask for every tick (`requiresUpdateEveryTick`) only every other game tick, and `ClampCareGoal`, `WoundCareGoal` and `RescueGoal` count their work in their own ticks. So `care_ticks` 600 (written "30 s" in `megaponera_analis.json`) takes 60 s per round (measured in a replay: carer at the patient's side from tick 14576 to 15796), a living clamp two rounds, about 2 minutes held still; a lick 30 s, not 15; a field pull by `RescueGoal` 20 s, not 10 (the raid's own pull counts game time and gets 10); a carer's walk gives up after 40 s, not 20; `GoInsideGoal`'s give-up counter the same. Real (Frank 2023): one care bout 85 s with the gland secretion, 53 s without, so 60 s is not wrong to real ants, but it is not what was written. The fix is small (count game time, or ask for every tick) but changes what the user has seen in the game, so it waits for the user's word.
358. **Open (found 2026-11-24 by the nest reader for BUGS 349): a Matabele nest in a dead termite mound keeps its entrance where it was when it settled, while the mound keeps wearing down.** In all three soak nests the saved entrance (the colony's centre) now floats 2 to 5 blocks above the hump's mouth, in open air (report 1: the shaft walled to y 78 or 79, the entrance at 84). Those air cells still count as inside the nest; the way home's 8-block "home" circle and `GoInsideGoal`'s search hint (`center.below()`) are centred on that floating cell, so for an ant at the shaft's foot the hint points away from the rooms. `Nest.getShaftBottom()` gives the entrance itself for a settled nest (shaft depth 0), not the shaft's foot. Nothing seen to break yet; to look at when the dead mound's wear is next worked on.
359. **Open, small (found 2026-11-24 with the BUGS 349 fix): Matabele coming home stop on their room's first cell, so they rest, and are tended, at the room's edge.** `GoInsideGoal` walks an ant that keeps to the rooms to the nearest room cell (the one the tunnel opens onto), and the idle walk then leaves it there: `settle_within` 4 from the room's middle covers the whole 3 by 3 queen's room. So homecomers stand on that cell until a stroll (about one a minute) moves them on, and since BUGS 349's fix care happens there too (all 16 rounds in the replays). It is a room cell, not the 1-wide way in, and insects pass through each other, so nothing is blocked; it is how it looks. A fix would end the walk in at the ant's own resting place (`idlePlace`) instead of the first room cell.
360. **Fixed (2026-11-25, the user's word "Yes, newest trail"; see the fix at the end). Was: open, cause found (2026-11-24): BUGS 351's raids stood at a fallen dead tree because they followed an old scent trail that the termites had since shut with mud; two fixes tried and set aside (the two-fails rule); a new starting idea waits for the user.** **What is there** (the replay `bug351_leader_no_route`, the saved land read block by block, the trail read from the save, two readers, a copy of the legs' route search run over the saved blocks): the trail the raids follow (laid days before, kept by R4's rule that a trail still smelt is used over a scout's new line, `Raids.found`, and kept fresh by every raid that walks it, even a failed one) runs into a fallen branch the termites have since cased in mud crust: a sealed pocket of 12 cells (branch cells walled by crust plates that face inward and roofed by crust with no top), its only opening the termites' gallery underneath. The march's halting place (the trail's last point 4 or more from the spot) is in that gallery; one trail point (18666, 73, 2282) sits on the crust roof with nothing to stand on or hold. The leader is sent there, its legs find no way (the way round through the termites' galleries is 135 to 147 steps, past the 6,000-cell search cap), the column stands, the deadline ends the raid. A fallen branch is no obstacle to the legs (it has no collision); bleached wood can be climbed; only the crust shuts the way. The newest scout's line to the same find climbs over the bare wood and stops 8 blocks from the spot, and the legs' copy finds a way along it (27 steps). **Try 1 (set aside):** make `RaidGround.canStand` ask the legs' own floor rule instead of "anything solid under it" (two judges of standing). Right in itself, but the place moved from the crust's top to a floor inside the sealed pocket: still no way. **Try 2 (set aside, saved as a diff outside the repo):** when the front's legs find no way within the attack's reach (14 blocks) of the spot, the way out is cut and the column halts where it stands. The raids then halted 11 to 12 blocks out, attacked and came home, but an independent review found it needs fixes of its own: the attack's leash assumes a halt about 4 blocks out (19 of 66 raiders were called back at once and never sent in again: 1 or 2 termites stung), the cut was lost when bodies were put back, and a stale "no route" could halt a raid. A fix whose only job would be to bend the attack round it is the warning sign, so it stopped there. **The assumption both tries shared:** the scout's old trail is still the way to the termites. **New starting idea (for the user's word, it changes R4):** each raid follows the line its scout laid on its latest walk home, as real Megaponera do (the scout that found the termites lays a fresh trail home and leads the column along it, Longhurst and Howse 1979; whether real columns also reuse an older trail is not reported, as far as I know), and a raid that never reached its halting place does not keep its trail fresh. Kept from try 1 for later: `canStand` and the legs disagree on mud tops (a second judge of standing), to tidy with that change. **The fix (2026-11-25):** each find keeps the id of the trail laid from its scout's line (`Raids.Find.trailId`, saved); a scout home from a find always lays its new line as the find's trail and the find's old line is dropped first (unless the column out walks it now); a raid follows its find's own trail (`trailOf`, `trailFor`), no longer "the freshest trail still smelt near the spot" (`ScentTrails.liveTo`, deleted); a raid read back from a save keeps the trail it was walking (`Raid` saves its trail's id, `Raids.trailForSaved`). Not done (fewer rules): a failed raid still refreshes the trail it walked; with one trail per find that no longer matters, as the scout's next line replaces it. Finds in saves from before have no trail id: the raids-as-numbers paths lay the find's line anew, but a real raid needs the scout home to lead (else "nobody knows the way") till a scout finds the spot again; test worlds are disposable. Test: `aRaidFollowsItsScoutsNewestLineNotAnOlderTrailToTheSameSpot` (fails on the old code: the scout's new line was never laid). The replay `bug351_leader_no_route` still fails, on other faults: BUGS 362 (places on mud-crust tops) and 361.
361. **Open, seen once (2026-11-24, in the BUGS 351 replay with try 2 of BUGS 360, since set aside): Matabele majors that chase termites into the termites' tunnels during an attack can find no way back to the column on the march home.** In the replay three majors went after termites under cover into the gallery under a dead tree (18680 to 18683, 70, 2280 to 2288); on the march home each was told to its place 18 to 19 blocks back at the halt, and its legs found no way (the march only tries the trail or a straight hop, both into the mud-cased branch; the way round is long). They stood 30 s and more (`raider-stuck`), then the march home's deadline let them go and they walked home by themselves (`MatabeleWayHomeGoal` tries more ways). Not seen in the soak. To look at once raids again reach termites under a fallen tree. **Seen again 2026-11-25** in the same replay with the newest-trail fix: after an attack under the dead tree two raiders stood 30 s in the termites' gallery at 18681, 70, 2282, told to places 15 to 16 blocks back; one was walled in, a pocket of one open cell between two termite mound blocks (the termites plug their tunnels), no way to the nest or the sky (`not-walled-in`). Three more majors stood "no route" in the gallery at 18683, 70, 2286 in another run. Still open; it now shows in most replays of that spot.
362. **Open (found 2026-11-25 in the BUGS 351 replay, once raids follow the newest trail): the raid's "can stand here" (`RaidGround.canStand`: anything solid under the cell) takes the top of a termite's mud-crust plate for ground, which the legs refuse, so raiders are sent to places they can never reach.** After the attack under the dead tree, the regroup and march-home places (18668 to 18670, 73, 2281 to 2283) lay on crust plates; five raiders in the hollow under the log stood 30 s with "no route" (`raider-stuck`). A second judge of standing beside the legs' own floor rule (`Foothold.floor(cell).isValid`). **Tried and set aside (2026-11-25):** `canStand` asking the legs' floor rule. It cleared these places, but it needed fixes of its own at once (a ramp's own cell becomes the standing cell, so the scouts' ground cell, `groundCell`, and every body put down at a cell's foot had to change too, and the mound-ringing scout test failed in the run with the rule alone, a known flake, BUGS 153 and 164, but it passed 4 of 4 once the ground cell agreed), the warning sign. A reviewer listed what the legs' rule refuses that the old check took: bottom slabs, stairs, carpets, snow layers, dirt path, farmland, frass, pulp heaps, partial termite-mound layers, the giant Termitomyces, spire and mouth tops, crusts without a roof plate; and old saved trail points on those would all break at once. Next: decide where raiders really need the legs' rule (the places they are sent to: halt, attack, regroup, march home) before making it the one judge everywhere.
363. **Open (test flake): the low-mound stream march test (`aRaidFromALowDeadMoundStreamsAlongItsTrail`) fails now and then beside other tests, each time on a different check** (found 2026-11-25 by the `raids,away,legs` runs for BUGS 360 on 4 servers): "55 percent of the raiders were more than a block and a half off the trail" (passed alone 3 of 3), "the column was NaN blocks long 20 blocks past the hump's foot" (failed alone 1 of 3), and in a run with the set-aside stand rule of BUGS 362 "raiders stood inside one another" (41 and 44 against 40). Not this change: run alone in batches of 4 it passed 8 of 8 on the newest-trail code and 8 of 8 on the code before it (`1b9bc856`). The "NaN blocks long" check failed the same way in its ribbon days (BUGS 188). Next time: read its `[march test]` lines for the dead tree's place against the trail and whether the column's length was ever measured.
364. **Open: the otters' nose-touch test failed once beside others (2026-11-25, branch `claude/flaky-fixes`, the `river` run of overnight flaky-test session 2, 6 servers).** `playingOttersTouchNosesAndRestSideBySideWithoutOverlapping`: "at the poke their middles stood 3.15 apart, not 2.83"; passed alone 3 of 3, so a flake (box 3 or 4). Not from that session's changes (no otter game code changed; only the walk-through test's limit). Not looked into.

365. **Open: four Matabele raid tests failed beside others in the `soldiers,raids` run for the real drumming speed (2026-11-25, branch `claude/flaky-fixes`, 6 servers; every soldier test passed).** `ascoutringsalivingmounditcomesnearandfollowsatermitetunnelouttothefeeding` "the find ... is not at the four termites at the east tunnel's end" failed alone 1 of 3, the same third way as BUGS 153 and 164's update of 2026-11-24, before this change (scouting, no drumming in it). Passed alone 3 of 3: `araidfromalowdeadmoundstreamsalongitstrail` (raiders standing inside one another on the march), `araidfromatalldeadmoundstreamsbehinditsscoutandneverstops` (39 percent of the raiders off the trail), `araidoverlumpygroundrunseverystageinitstimeandbringsitspreytothepile` (the watch rule `raider-stuck`, a raider told to a place 7 off and still 30 s, as BUGS 313). The first three are scouting and the march, before any termite soldier answers; the last is in the attack, where faster drumming brings the soldiers sooner, so this change cannot be ruled out for it, but BUGS 313 is the same rule broken before it. Not looked into (the rule: bugs found by test runs go on the list); the raid rewrite's step 6 soaks are the place.

366. **Open: the ten-Matabele skirmish test failed again, a Matabele standing half a minute with a fight order (2026-11-25, branch `claude/review-fixes` after `claude/flaky-fixes` was merged in, the full run on 6 servers; failed alone 1 of 3, so REAL but rare).** `tenMatabelesAtAFeedingSpotTakeMinutesAndManyTermitesEscape`: "a Matabele has stood at BlockPos{x=-8, y=10, z=-12} for half a minute while the fight is on (the hay at -20, 12, -17); it runs [FightGoal], activity 0, idle false, climbing false, on the ground true, fights no more false (badly wounded false, health 5.0)". The same picture as BUGS 97 and 106 (a fighter with a fight order and nothing moving it); the merge's clash check found no overlap in the fight code, so likely old, not from the merge. Not looked into (the rule: bugs found by test runs go on the list). In the same run, on its own: the optional replay `replay_bug351_leader_no_route` broke `raider-stuck` (up to 7 raiders 5 to 10 off their places for 30 s, reports `2026-11-25_09-53-10` and `09-54-39`) but passed alone 3 of 3.

367. **Open: `crocodilesSpawnApartAndBaskTogether` failed beside others (2026-11-25, branch `claude/review-fixes` after the merge, the full run on 6 servers; passed alone 3 of 3, so a flake, box 3 or 4).** "the second crocodile lies 0.9 blocks from the first (side by side is about 1.7)". Not among BUGS 305's three crocodile flakes fixed overnight (the shared sun clock); not looked into.

368. **Open: two river flakes in the buffalo's `river,worldgen` check run (2026-11-25, branch `claude/buffalo`, 6 servers).** `aCrocodileAfloatClimbsOverAnUnderwaterLedge` failed alone 1 of 3 ("never got over the ledge onto the bank ... scale 1.07, lowest feet y 17.98, sun time false"; the same test as BUGS 292 and 303, partly fixed overnight on `claude/flaky-fixes`, so still flaky); `aDistichodusBitesOnlyTheOuterEdgeOfHippoGrass` passed alone 3 of 3. Neither scene has a buffalo, and the change's only river code is the hippo's grass and shore helpers moved into `animal/Grazing` (the crocodile and the distichodus do not call it); the same two categories all passed on this branch's first run. Not looked into (the rule: bugs found by test runs go on the list).

369. **Fixed: a big animal in deep water swam at a crawl and slid back off the bank (2026-11-25, the lion's `aLionInDeepWaterSwimsOut`, failed alone 3 of 3; the shared `SwimOutGoal`, so the buffalo too).** Two causes, found by a trace. (1) Minecraft moves a mob in water by its move control's speed squared over 50 (0.02 times its forward input, which is itself its speed): a lioness at her walk crossed 4 blocks in a minute, so the test ran out reaching the bank (the buffalo's own test passed only by its longer time). (2) The goal ran only while the water was over half its height: heaved up against the bank it was shallow for a moment, the goal stopped, nothing pushed it forward, and it slid back, over and over. Fix: a paddle of `PADDLE` 0.05 blocks a tick (1 a second) toward the shore set straight on its motion, and `canContinueToUse` until it is out of the water or on the ground. Lesson: a mob's water speed is not its land speed; and a goal whose start condition changes as it works needs its own keep-going condition.

370. **Fixed: every lion crashed the server on its first tick once the hunt was added (2026-11-25, branch `claude/buffalo`, lion step 2; the first `land` run, 3 of 6 servers crashed).** "Cannot read field stage because this.s is null" in `LionHunt.HuntGoal.decide`. Minecraft makes a mob's goals inside the `Mob` constructor (`registerGoals`), before the subclass's own fields are set, and the hunt goal kept `l.hunt` (a field of `LionEntity`) in a final field of its own, so it kept null. Fix: the goal reads the lion's hunt state at its first `canUse`. **Lesson: a goal never copies a field of its mob in its constructor; read it when the goal first runs.**

371. **Fixed: lions at a kill stood a step from their feeding places for minutes and never ate (2026-11-25, lion step 2, `atDuskAHungryPrideTakesACalfAndFeeds`: the calf killed at about 1500 ticks, four of five lions still "route 1/1" to their place at 6900).** A route ends when the mob is within about half its width of the last block's middle, and a feeding place is a point inside a block, up to a block from where the route ends; the goal asked for a new route every 10 ticks, to the same block. Fix: within 2.5 blocks of the place the lion steps straight onto it with its move control (`LionHunt.walkTo`), and a place not reached in 5 s is given up (it feeds or lies where it got to). **Lesson: a route reaches a block, not a point: to stand on a point, finish with the move control.**

372. **Fixed (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio phase 2, step 1, branch `claude/motion-phase2`): a stalking lioness stands up and glides after her first freeze (2026-11-25, found by the Motion Studio's first hunt film, `tools/film/lion_hunt.film`, branch `claude/motion-studio`; every lioness, in three takes).** From the end of her first `ambush_freeze` until her leap, her body clip player holds no clip at all (the recording reads "- stopped"), so she is drawn in the model's rest pose: her body at 1.02 blocks over her feet, as standing after the hunt, against 0.74 crouched in the stalk and the freeze, sliding forward with still legs (183 to 236 moments a lioness, 9 to 18 seconds). Each new freeze crouches her again (that clip plays) and the stalk after it is empty again. Her first stalk, before any freeze, plays. Cause not found yet: the freeze to stalk change goes through `forceAnimationReset` and `setAndContinue(stalk)` in `LionEntity.animate` like every other doing; read GeckoLib's `AnimationController.setAnimation` and `process` with `SteadyController.adjustTick` (a transition starts only on an adjusted tick of 0) before changing anything. How to see it: `python tools/pair_report.py run-film/screenshots/lion_hunt` ("NO CLIP on its body player"). **Cause:** GeckoLib 4.9.3's `AnimationController` starts the blend into a new clip by taking the next clip off its queue on any frame whose blend clock reads exactly 0, and its blend clock ran at the clip's play speed. The lioness plays her stalk at speed 0 while still between creeping steps, and one ending a freeze has not moved yet: the clock stayed at 0 for two frames, the second took again from the now empty queue, and the player was left with no clip, stopped; asking for the same stalk again did nothing (GeckoLib takes an equal clip as already set). **Fix:** `SteadyController.adjustTick` runs a blend on real time (rate 1 while transitioning), the clip's play speed only once it plays: every animal on it (15) now blends in its set ticks at any play speed, as `tools/clip_check.py` always assumed (before, a clip at 2x blended in half the time, at 0 never). **Checked:** the hunt film again: no empty clip player, every lioness's body at 0.74 blocks through every freeze and stalk (middle of about 490 moments each); the buffalo film's checks the same (82 parts inside against 81, 521 jumps against 544). **Lesson:** a clip played at speed 0 must not stop time for everything else on its player; read the library's own code before patching round it (the blend clock was the library's, not ours).

373. **Open: a holding lioness bites the air (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio's hunt film).** While holding the calf her jaws (the plan's `carry` bone) are 0.4 to 1.0 blocks off its body in every take (middle +0.44 to +0.72; they touch, within 0.1, for 2 to 4 moments in about 22). The hold place comes from a fixed oval round the prey's box (`LionHunt.holdPlace`: 1.9 by 0.6 of its size, the head 0.4 in), not from the drawn body; a calf knocked down lies lower and shorter than the oval, and the buffalo is drawn 10 px ahead of its box (`DRAW_AHEAD_PX`). For the Motion Studio's phase 3 (partner moves: the hold placed from the prey's drawn sockets, `docs/plans/motion-studio.md`), not a new tuning of the oval.

374. **Open: a holding lioness's planted feet skate (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio's hunt film).** While holding a calf that lies still, her box is pushed toward its hold place up to 0.6 a tick (`LionHunt.hold`, `l.move`) while `takedown_pin` or the strike clip plays in place: planted feet slide a middle 0.10 to 0.26 blocks a tick, up to 0.9. For phase 3 (the holder placed on the prey, not pushed toward it). (Measured on films that played clips at twice their speed, BUGS 380: the box pushing is real, the slide numbers are to be measured again.)

375. **Open: the strike glides (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio's hunt film).** From the start of `jump_smack` or `stalk_leap_bite` to the grip, the move control carries the lioness's box 4.1 to 4.8 blocks (0.25 to 0.31 a tick, `LionHunt.strikeMoment`) while the clip's root stays in place, so a leap is a crouched slide. For phase 3 (idea 41: a strike clip that carries its own travel, aimed at where the prey will be).

376. **Open: a one-tick hitch between one-shot doings (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio's hunt film).** A lion's doing goes back to CALM for one tick between two one-shots (the strike to the pin, each feeding turn of `drink` and `takedown_pin`), so for one tick it plays `idle` or `trot` (moving) and then restarts the next clip with a fresh blend: a twitch every feeding turn (about every 1.5 to 5 seconds) and at the grip. Seen in the recordings' clip timelines; the pictures' jump flags at those ticks are likely the same thing (not each checked).

377. **Open: lionesses overlap each other at the kill (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio's hunt film).** Three lionesses rushing and holding one calf stand through each other's bodies (the contact sheets `rush_east` from frame 14). Insects never push (the performance rule); the lions' hold places are kept apart on the prey only by angle, and their bodies, 4 blocks long, overlap round a small calf. For phase 3 (several holders, each on its own socket, checked against each other: ideas 31 and 39).

378. **Open: a buffalo calf's feet slide when it runs (2026-11-25, found by the Motion Studio's pace report, `tools/pace_report.py`, on the hunt film; branch `claude/motion-phase2`).** A calf (size 0.36) fleeing with the herd runs at the cow's speed (about 4.7 blocks a second), so its charge clip, scaled to its size, would have to play 5.3 times as fast to keep its hooves on the ground; the play speed stops at the charge's limit, 2.5, and the ground goes by 2.1 times as fast as its legs move (every moment of its charge). The cow's own charge plays at about 2.1 all the time (hurried, feet right), the lion cub's walk at about 1.4 (it walks at the grown lions' pace). **Recommendation (my pick, not built):** let a calf run a little slower than its mother and fall behind in a stampede, as real calves do (the very calves lions cut out), with a higher top play speed for small bodies (a small animal's legs do turn over faster); the charge and the cub's walk clips stay as made until someone remakes them with the animation checks. How to see it: `python tools/pace_report.py run-film/screenshots/lion_hunt` ("feet 2.14, off 100%" on the calf's charge).

380. **Fixed (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio phase 2, branch `claude/motion-phase2`): every frame film played the animals' clips at twice their speed.** Found by the gait film (`tools/film/gait_changes.film`): in a frozen game stepped a tick a picture, each planted foot swept back at about twice the body's speed (a trotting lioness's feet 0.28 blocks a tick against the body's 0.14; a walking bull's 0.10 against 0.05), and the recorded clip time moved about twice its play speed a tick (a trot at 0.98 moved 1.95 clip ticks; a 4-tick blend ended in 2). A film of the same trot with the game running (`tools/film/clip_clock.film`) showed 1.00 exactly: play was always right. **Cause:** a frozen game stepped for the camera draws an animal once at the end of the new tick, then the tick's own frames from its start, a tick back. GeckoLib's clock follows that back and forth, but `SteadyController.adjustTick` (BUGS 232, 2026-11-19) carried on from wherever the clock went back and counted the way forward again: each stepped tick counted about twice. So since 2026-11-19 every frame film and course of the 15 animals on it showed their clips at double speed (feet sliding where play has none); ants and termites do not use it. **Fix:** the clip follows the clock both ways (a step back of over a tick is a reset and carries on, as before); a moment before a loop's start is drawn at its start. The drawing clock (`DrawClock`, phase 2 step 4) holds the furthest time drawn instead, since an ease cannot run backwards. **Lesson:** "carry on when the clock goes back" double-counts whenever the clock goes back and forth; and check the film against the game running before trusting a film's timing (the clip clock film does it in a minute).

381. **Fixed (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio phase 2, branch `claude/motion-phase2`): one film take, two moments.** With several cameras (the film step `cam`, phase 0), the first camera's picture of each take was drawn at the stepped tick's start (in-between time 0.02) and the others at its end (1.0), almost a tick apart; the camera's checks then counted the difference as a jump (645 on a galloping lioness once BUGS 380 was fixed). **Cause:** Minecraft's drawing learns that the frozen game stands still again one frame late, so the first frame after a step still draws the animals as stepping; the camera shot it. Ants and termites never showed it (the camera draws them at its own in-between time). **Fix:** after a step arrives the camera waits one more client tick before its first picture (`FilmDirector.onTick`): all 1,020 pictures of the gait film drawn at 1.0, the flags down to the six fastest moments of the hurried charge and gallop. **Lesson:** log the moment each picture is drawn at before trusting a film's checks; a frozen game's drawing settles a frame after each step.

383. **Fixed (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio phase 2, branch `claude/motion-phase2`): in a still frame a living buffalo was drawn as its carcass.** Found in the buffalo's frame film (`tools/film/buffalo_frames.film`) once BUGS 381 was fixed: every clip burst after the first showed one bull lying on its side, the same picture whatever the clip (two different pictures in each burst of up to 20), while the bull's clip ran on as it should (its clip time logged moving 2 ticks a picture). The film of the code before phase 2 showed every burst moving (checked side by side in another copy of the code). **Cause:** a buffalo that dies leaves its carcass (lion step 2, the remains), drawn on the living buffalo's own model file, and GeckoLib keeps one set of bones per model file, shared by every renderer that names it. It poses an animal again only when its time has moved since it was last drawn; drawn again at the same moment, it trusts the bones to still hold its pose. In play every frame's time moves, so nothing showed; in a still frame (a frozen film between steps, or the game paused) both were drawn on whatever pose was set last, the carcass's. The film killed each bull where the next one stood, so a carcass lay under every bull after the first; before BUGS 381 the camera shot the first frame after each step, where both were posed afresh, and it never showed. **Fix:** the carcass draws on bones of its own, baked from the same file (`OwnBones`, in the framework, for any model drawn on another animal's model file; baked again after a resource reload); the buffalo film waits out each death (33 ticks) and clears the carcass before the next bull. **Lesson:** two renderers on one model file share its bones; a model drawn on another animal's file needs bones of its own. And a still frame is where drawing shortcuts show: check films for pictures that never change.

384. **Open (2026-11-25, the Motion Studio phase 2, branch `claude/motion-phase2`): at a start the gait rule held a walk while the animal already ran.** Found in the hunt film's pace report (`python tools/pace_report.py run-film/screenshots/lion_hunt`): the cow drawn walking 3.6 s at its top play speed with its feet sliding 4.2 times (she moved 4 to 5 blocks a second), the calf's trot 4.7 times, the elder lioness's walk 2.8 times; the male held his gallop below its slowest play speed (feet 0.75) waiting to drop to a trot. **Cause:** `GaitShift` (step 3) holds the drawn gait until its clip comes to the moment matching the new gait's first frame. At a start the animal goes from standing to a run in 3 ticks: the first tick picked the walk, the rule held it while easing in from the standing clip and then waited for the walk's footfall (12 ticks at its top speed) while she ran; before phase 2 the gait changed at once. Its starting idea assumed every gait change starts from a steady stride whose clip can keep pace while it waits; at a start, or when the speed jumps past what the old gait can carry, it cannot. This is the rule's second fail (the first: it read a clip going back as a new loop), so it waits for a new starting idea with the user (CLAUDE.md, two fails then rethink). **Proposed (my pick, not built):** wait for the footfall only while the old gait can carry the speed (its play speed within its own limits) and its stride has begun; otherwise change at once, as before phase 2: a start from standing, a doing, or a burst of speed changes gait at once (a few ticks' blend, as real animals launch straight into a run), and only the gentle speed-ups and slow-downs wait for the matching footfall. **Also for the new rule, from the phase 2 review (4 agents, 2026-11-25):** a lion turning in place after a run keeps its trot or gallop going on the spot until the footfall comes (up to about 0.8 s; before, a slow walk at once), and its stride (the last running speed) still reads as a run there, so the turn needs its own answer; and `GaitShift` counts a frame that moves the clip forward more than half a loop (a long frame hitch) as going back, a guard no clip player needs (a step back over a loop's start shows as a small step back).
